# Phase 0 — Discovery and reproduction

Captured 2026-10-29 UTC from the VPS checkout at /opt/ogami-erp. The initial discovery and reproduction findings below are a pre-fix baseline. Subsequent implementation changes and their scope are documented under “Current-state deltas after baseline.”

## Scope and runtime

The running application is Laravel 12.69.3 on PHP 8.3.36, React 18/Vite 7.3.7, PostgreSQL 16, Redis 7, and Reverb. The prompt described Laravel 11; the checked-out and running project is Laravel 12. APP_ENV=local, APP_DEBUG=false, and APP_URL is ogamiph.dev. The original PostgreSQL volume and business data were retained.

The local Compose app and Vite server bind-mount the working tree. Public HTTPS on ogamiph.dev proxies the SPA to Vite on port 5173, API traffic to PHP-FPM, and Pusher/Reverb websocket paths /app and /apps to Reverb. Vite HMR is configured for wss on ogamiph.dev:443. The runtime config is in the ignored, persistent .dev-runtime directory; it leaves tracked production Compose and Nginx files untouched. Root .env remains unchanged. Reverb key equality was checked without printing the key. API and worker processes report local environment with debug disabled.

After the local Nginx restart, an external HTTPS HEAD to https://ogamiph.dev returned 200 through Cloudflare. A headless Chromium page loaded the sign-in screen using the ogamiph.dev hostname and valid TLS; Vite reported connected and Reverb returned connection_established. That Playwright test mapped the hostname directly to the VPS origin, so public-edge websocket behavior has not independently been measured. Public curl verified the Cloudflare HTTP path. The first browser check found a blank SPA because the temporary local proxy mounted the production CSP; the local proxy now includes the existing dev-only security header file, and the app renders. Nginx config test passes. The checked-in dev CSP explicitly permits the Vite React Refresh inline preamble; production CSP remains untouched.

At initial discovery, the local stack used the same PostgreSQL and Redis containers/volumes, with Redis DB 2 for queues and DB 3 for cache; production DB 0/1 keys were not flushed during the dev-mode switch. Sessions use the database driver. Mail is redirected to the log driver. The local scheduler has run `production:notify-upcoming-work-orders`, `purchasing:close-due-rfqs`, `outbox:dispatch --limit=100`, and `pricing:review-agreements --dispatch-only` against the retained database. No migrations or destructive database/Redis cleanup were run during the local-mode switch; the later, explicitly scoped cache-prefix cleanup after cache-host separation is documented below. A previous restore attempt ran migrations and returned Nothing to migrate.

The repository was on branch main when inspected. Pre-existing dirty files are listed in PROGRESS.md and were preserved. The user subsequently authorized committing and pushing the complete workspace after the task; no commit, push, merge, or branch deletion has happened yet.

## Inventory produced

The project has 23 API modules: Accounting, Admin, Assets, Attendance, Auth, B2B, CRM, DSS, Dashboard, Forecasting, HR, Inventory, Landing, Leave, Loans, MRP, Maintenance, Payroll, Production, Purchasing, Quality, ReturnManagement, and SupplyChain.

The live `route:list --json` count is 1,303. The current route/mutation inventory contains 681 mutating HTTP route rows (679 application mutations plus two local Laravel Ignition POST endpoints): POST 381, PUT 55, PATCH 174, DELETE 71, plus five GET RFQ routes with status-changing deadline/view side effects. It indexes 70 scheduled command invocations plus one scheduled Accounting closure and one cross-cutting AuditLog model-create side effect, for 758 mutation/matrix rows total. `00-record-types.csv` contains 254 PHP model files plus the non-model Setting cache entity (255 rows). The current async entrypoint sweep contains 338 jobs, commands, observers, listeners and scheduler entrypoints; all 70 `Schedule::command` invocations and the scheduled Accounting closure are indexed. Every route/scheduled row now has a source disposition in the matrix, but source notes do not prove the event was delivered or that every state transition has been represented separately. The current event catalog contains 182 event classes and the broadcast payload/channel audit contains 127 broadcaster rows after Delta 818; the latest mutation and stale-risk inventories each contain 758 rows, including one cross-cutting AuditLog model-create side effect. These counts are source-static and do not prove producer coverage for every mutation; channel-policy parity and authorization outcomes remain selective. The deduplicated cache-source ledger contains 441 unique PHP source/line pairs: 141 under `api/app` across 50 files, 243 under tests, and 57 under database/migration/other sources. All recorded snippets match current source after indentation normalization; a direct Cache facade/helper identifier scan found no unregistered candidates under `api/app`, `api/tests`, or `api/database`. Dynamic package cache calls and semantic invalidation remain outside that narrow check. The ledger indexes shared `FailOpenCache`/`CacheGeneration` helpers and the `Repository::get/add/put` operations inside them. The polling inventory has 21 rows: 8 active declarations, 1 unreferenced declaration, and 12 removed historical entries. The active recovery-operation poll is the documented short-lived bearer-token security exception in `00-polling-call-sites.csv`. This remains a route/entrypoint inventory rather than a complete catalog of distinct domain-state transitions; that state-machine-level inventory and per-transition runtime matrix are still outstanding.

The HTTP route inventory is exhaustive for registered runtime mutations. It does not imply each job, import, webhook, observer, or direct database writer was dynamically executed. Those source entrypoints are indexed separately. The Phase 1 matrix has one row for each mutating HTTP route and a separate row for every scheduled invocation; unverified paths are marked explicitly.

Delta 839 added `RealtimeMutationCoverageContractTest`: the guard compares the 680 inventoried write routes to Laravel's live route collection, checks the 685 HTTP write/side-effect rows are represented in the stale-risk matrix with populated evidence/deferred-reason cells, confirms the five state-changing GET routes remain registered, verifies every `*RealtimeObserver` is registered by an application service provider, and checks direct status-writer rows have an event producer or an allowed no-runtime-event disposition. The two additional B2B broadcast-auth POST routes are explicitly treated as non-mutating channel handshakes, not domain record writes. The isolated test passes 2 tests / 3,241 assertions. This guard detects route/inventory drift and missing observer registration; source assertions and focused producer tests are still needed to prove a writer actually stages the event, and no end-to-end result is inferred from this static check.

Employee source review found that the only pre-existing `Employee` observer refreshed linked-user access scope when `department_id` changed. SalaryAdjustment create/approval/rejection also had no shared list/detail event, so queue viewers could retain stale status/actions until reload. The daily due-salary command instead updates Employee pay columns through Eloquent without any employee record/list event, while employee detail displays those columns and had no employee subscription. The new `EmployeeRealtimeObserver` feeds a content-free, transaction-coalesced `EmployeeChanged` signal through the after-commit outbox; `SalaryAdjustmentRealtimeObserver` similarly emits `SalaryAdjustmentChanged` for maker-checker and scheduled adjustment writes. record and list subscriptions refetch the authorized API representation. Department list channels are scoped to the viewer's current department, company-wide lists require `hr.employees.view_sensitive`, and the personal channel requires the exact linked user. Related references include old and current department/position IDs so both sides of a move can invalidate. This source trace does not prove event delivery or UI latency.

The authenticated API cache-header policy has a no-store middleware plus a matching exception-responder defense-in-depth layer. A focused feature run verified the current policy on the successful `/auth/user` route and its unauthorized 401 response; this did not reproduce a missing-header failure. No live 403/422 header or cross-origin `Vary` capture was made.

## Backend event and dispatch pipeline

Broadcast configuration defaults to Reverb. The browser connects over HTTPS/WSS to ogamiph.dev:443; server-side broadcasting targets the Reverb service on the Docker network at reverb:8080 over HTTP. Allowed browser origins are ogamiph.dev and www.ogamiph.dev. Reverb message/request size defaults to 10,000 bytes, ping interval 60 seconds, activity timeout 30 seconds, and scaling is disabled. No explicit per-connection cap or broadcast payload-size guard was found.

Private channel authorization is defined in api/routes/channels.php:

| Channel | Authorization found |
|---|---|
| production.dashboard | production module; production.view + production.dashboard.view, dashboard.plant_manager.view, dashboard.ppc.view, or dashboard.company.view (refresh-only dashboard signals) |
| quality.dashboard | production module, dashboard.quality.view; output-only refresh ping |
| production.wo.{hashId} | production module/view permissions and scoped work-order visibility |
| production.machine.{hashId} | MRP module and machine-view permission/scope |
| payroll.period.{hashId} | payroll module and scoped period visibility |
| maintenance.dashboard | maintenance module and maintenance.view |
| inventory.dashboard | inventory module and inventory.view; refresh-only stock signal |
| entity.item.visible | inventory module; inventory, Purchasing catalog, or MRP BOM item-read permission |
| entity.work_order.visible | Production module; production.view + production.work_orders.view |
| entity.maintenance_work_order.visible | Maintenance module; maintenance.view |
| entity.ncr.visible | Quality module; quality.ncr.view |
| rfq.{hashId} | purchasing module and `RequestForQuoteAccessPolicy` record visibility |
| user.{userId} | notifications.view and decoded user ID must equal the current user |
| user.notification-preferences.{userHashId} | exact active internal user with notifications.preferences.manage; matches the preference endpoint |
| user.preferences.{userHashId} | exact active internal user; matches the personal /auth/user/preferences owner scope |
| entity.user_invite.visible | active internal users with `auth.users.invite`; matches the invite-list endpoint permission |
| badges | any authenticated user; payload is empty and clients refetch permitted counts |
| settings.refresh | active authenticated ERP users; content-free settings refresh ping, followed by permission-scoped API refetches |
| dss.refresh | active users with the DSS feature and dss.view, dss.manage, or dss.admin; content-free transaction-coalesced refetch signal |
| entity.{entityType}.visible | entity allow-list, module enabled, matching list permission; row-level data remains API-scoped |
| approval-delegation.user.{userHashId} | exact active delegator or delegate whose self-service list can include the row |
| entity.approval_delegation.managers | active user with admin.delegations.manage_any, matching the manager's all-delegations API scope; payload is refresh-only |
| leave-request.{hashId} | leave module, leave.view, and the same employee/department/HR row scope as the leave API |
| entity.leave_request.visible | leave module and leave.view; content-free signal only, refetch remains row-scoped |
| entity.leave_type.visible | leave module and leave.view; content-free reference-data refresh |
| entity.leave_balance.visible | leave module and leave.view or either leave approval permission; no employee/type metadata, refetch uses the scoped balance API |
| supplier.vendor.{vendorHashId} | supplier_portal guard; active, unlocked user and active matching vendor; b2b_portals enabled; initial password change complete |
| chain.{entityType}.{hashId} | entity type allow-list, feature and permission checks, and model existence; explicit record access policies are applied to purchase requests and purchase orders, while several other types fall through to permission + existence |

There are no presence channels. Entity-list channels carry only a refresh ping; the API remains responsible for row/scope filtering. `dss.refresh` is permission-gated and carries no detector or recommendation identifiers. Frontend usePermissionSync also subscribes to public channel settings; it is not declared in routes/channels.php. There are no role-wide channels in the channel file.

The internal SPA authenticates `/api/v1/broadcasting/auth` with Sanctum stateful cookies and Axios credentials/XSRF handling. Laravel also registers `/broadcasting/auth` as a compatibility endpoint; Delta 848 moved it from the default `web`-only stack to `api` plus `auth:sanctum`, so the shared inactive-session and password guards run before channel callbacks. A focused regression reproduced the old inactive-user access (HTTP 200) and passes after the middleware change (active user 200, inactive user 401). The supplier portal uses the separate `/api/v1/b2b/supplier/broadcasting/auth` endpoint behind `auth:supplier_portal`, portal password checks, module gating and B2B tenancy middleware; the Echo authorizer selects that route only for `supplier.vendor.*`. The two-context supplier retest received 200 for its own vendor channel and 403 for a different vendor in the backend authorization test. CORS allows the configured FRONTEND_URL with credentials. All flows are cookie/session based, not bearer-token based.

At initial discovery, fourteen event classes implemented ShouldBroadcast, ShouldBroadcastNow, or both: SalesOrderConfirmed, MaintenanceWorkOrderCreated, WorkOrderOutputRecorded, WorkOrderStatusChanged, MachineBreakdownDetected, MrpPlanGenerated, MachineStatusChanged, MoldShotLimitNearing, MoldShotLimitReached, PayrollProgressEvent, BadgesChanged, PermissionOverrideChanged, UserNotificationCreated, and ChainStepAdvanced. At that time ChainStepAdvanced was ShouldBroadcastNow and its normal producer staged it through the durable event_outbox. It now implements queued ShouldBroadcast on Redis queue `broadcasts`; the outbox still stages it after commit and routes/console.php still provides recovery dispatch. Its payload remains only `refresh=true`. Many business/domain event classes do not implement a broadcast contract.

There is no one canonical event for every mutation. ChainBroadcaster is called from selected CRM, Purchasing, SupplyChain, Inventory, Accounting, Production, and ReturnManagement service paths; it maps eight entity types and writes the outbox chain signal. Other events are emitted from separate services/jobs/observers. No mutation-path guard was found that fails when a state change emits no event.

Queue configuration uses Redis and sets after_commit=false. The dedicated `broadcasts` Horizon supervisor consumes `redis_broadcasts`, now mapped to Redis DB 1 by default through the `broadcasts` Redis connection; the ordinary job lanes remain on the default DB 0 connection. Both DBs reside on the persistent queue Redis service with `noeviction`; `redis-cache` remains a separate bounded `allkeys-lru` instance, and sessions use PostgreSQL. The new `redis_broadcasts_legacy` queue connection is reserved for draining pre-cutover DB 0 broadcast jobs; the deployment runbook requires that drain and no queue keys are flushed. The broadcast supervisor keeps `block_for=1` and Horizon `sleep=0`; it runs one worker in local mode. Queued `ChainStepAdvanced`, `BadgesChanged`, `AdminDashboardChanged`, `ActivityFeedChanged` and `CustomerComplaintChanged` events select this connection/queue. `BadgesChanged` now fans out the same content-free signal to each active internal user’s `badge-user.{userHashId}` private channel; the channel callback matches the authenticated user to the opaque hash, and badge/approval hooks subscribe only to their own channel before refetching through the API. Queue telemetry records precise `JobQueued`→`JobProcessing` wait and job duration without logging payload contents. A current source audit of 122 broadcast classes found all implement `ShouldBroadcast` and `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`, use `BroadcastsOnDedicatedQueue`, and provide an explicit `broadcastWith()` whitelist; this does not prove every mutation producer emits a signal or each queue delivery succeeds at runtime. No queue migration, Redis operation, delivery, browser scenario, or performance measurement was run for this config change.

Production Nginx proxies /app and /apps with Upgrade/Connection headers and a 3,600 second read timeout. The repository dev Nginx config originally proxies /ws, not the Pusher /app path. The active local runtime override routes /app and /apps correctly. Public site uses HTTPS; browser Vite HMR and Reverb use WSS. No stale config cache is active in the local runtime: bootstrap/cache/config.php is absent. The production entrypoint has its own optimize/config/route cache behavior; local dev starts without config cache. Docker production images and worker restarts are part of deployment, not this discovery-phase source change.

The confirmed stale Horizon event-codec run establishes an additional local workflow requirement: mounted PHP source is visible to request processes, but PHP-FPM and long-lived consumers retain loaded classes/config. The first `make dev-refresh-php` run used bare Compose, recreated API/workers with production configuration, and those containers restarted on boot checks; Nginx/Vite stayed reachable. Recovery used the verified local image/runtime override and preserved DB/Redis data. A second attempt using only the tracked override created duplicate unstarted containers because the active VPS services also carry a temporary `/tmp/ogami-dev.local.yml` override. The target now avoids Compose selection: it refuses unless the named API container uses `ogami-erp-dev:local` and reports `APP_ENV=local`, clears Laravel config/route/event/view artifacts, then restarts the existing API, Horizon, mail worker and Reverb containers in place. It does not clear application/Redis data caches or rebuild images. Restart can briefly interrupt API/WebSocket requests. This refreshes mounted PHP changes and loaded PHP state; changing container environment variables still requires recreating containers with the complete active local Compose file set. The corrected target completed successfully: API/Horizon/mail/Reverb returned to running state; Artisan reported `local`, Horizon and `supervisor-broadcasts` running, and public app/Vite requests returned HTTP 200. The Horizon container health check was still in its startup window during the immediate sample, so readiness is supported by Artisan's live status rather than a settled Docker health state.

Delta 839's fresh container inspection found that this local split had recurred: SPA/Nginx/Reverb were on the local overlays, but API/Horizon/mail/scheduler had production Compose labels and the API had no source mount. This was a code-freshness/deployment-mode mismatch, not evidence that an API or Redis cache returned stale records. The four PHP services were recreated with `COMPOSE_FILE=docker-compose.yml:.dev-runtime/docker-compose.override.yml`, using `ogami-erp-dev:local`, `APP_ENV=local`, `APP_DEBUG=false`, and the `/opt/ogami-erp/api` source bind mount. DB, queue Redis, cache Redis, Vite, Nginx, and Reverb were not recreated; there was no migration or cache/data cleanup. Afterward, the site, `/@vite/client`, and `/api/v1/health` each returned HTTP 200, and Horizon became healthy. The old test invocation against the production API container could not run because `artisan test` is not registered there and its image excludes tests; one focused coverage test was subsequently run in an isolated network-disabled dev-image container.

## Frontend event, data and cache path (baseline plus verified deltas)

At initial discovery, Echo was a lazy singleton in `spa/src/lib/echo.ts` using Pusher/Reverb, the same-origin session cookie, and `/api/v1/broadcasting/auth`. Pusher-js handled transport reconnection, but the SPA had no general reconnect catch-up and each hook owned subscriptions. The current SPA adds `RealtimeProvider`, ref-counted subscriptions, explicit catch-up for active queries on reconnect/focus/visibility, per-channel pending/ready/error tracking, catch-up after subscription acknowledgement, an offline indicator and fallback, and the central invalidation map. A failed channel auth now changes the state to Reconnecting, activates mounted degraded fallbacks and calls authorized refetch callbacks in shared hooks. The root provider also remains active for a mounted private portal subscription when no internal ERP user exists; Echo routes `supplier.vendor.*` auth through the supplier guard endpoint. The acknowledgement/error path covers selected record/list hooks plus badge, notification, permission-sync, inventory, RFQ and supplier-listing consumers; focused tests cover those callbacks and live PO/supplier E2Es prove selected catch-up and cross-guard refresh. No live 403 was induced, wiring is not comprehensive, and explicit refresh/re-auth of every active channel after a role change remains unverified.

The baseline `useEcho` subscribed and cleaned up per component; `useChainProgress` covered selected Sales Order, Work Order, PO, Delivery, GRN, Bill and Invoice pages, while Purchase Request detail had no subscription. The detail omission was reproduced and fixed. Current hooks centralize subscriptions and add Purchase Request detail/list, chain count, and inventory stock invalidation. The current `spa/src` source scan finds no page-level direct Echo channel creation: `echo.private()` appears only in the shared ref-counted subscription manager, and the Echo singleton is instantiated only in `lib/echo.ts`. Convenience wrappers (`useEcho`, `useChainProgress`) delegate to the shared realtime hook. This is static source evidence; it does not prove every record page is subscribed to the correct channel or that delivery/UI freshness is complete.

The shared TanStack QueryClient defaults to `staleTime: 30s`, retry 1, `refetchOnWindowFocus: false`, and `networkMode: offlineFirst`. Cache keys and invalidations remain page/hook-specific. Many record keys omit user or role identity, but `applyUser` now clears the whole query cache when user, role, employee/department, delegated approvals, active state, permissions or features change; login/logout and the terminal 401 path also clear it. No persisted React Query cache was found. Logout additionally deletes app-owned `ogami-` Cache Storage entries. The root `RealtimeProvider` now runs one visibility-aware catch-up timer for all active queries only while the transport or any subscribed private channel is not live: first delay 5 s, then exponential backoff capped at 60 s. A focused provider test verifies the offline catch-up; per-module event invalidation remains selective. A headless Chromium run on `https://ogamiph.dev` verified the shared-session logout boundary with two same-origin tabs signed in as `employee@ogami.test`: the peer redirected to `/sign-in` and `/auth/user` returned 401. This does not verify a different-user/role switch or physical persisted-storage cleanup.

The current source scan found no unconditional pass-through `placeholderData: previous => previous` in application pages. Paginated screens use `sameQueryExceptPage` to preserve rows only across page-number changes with every other key scope equal (78 call sites across 75 page modules); date/entity/search/report transitions either wait for their current-key result or use an explicit scope comparison. This is static source evidence only; runtime navigation and account-switch behavior were not exercised in this work slice.

The baseline actor pages commonly used local optimistic status mutations plus invalidation/refetch. The shared status-action hook now reconciles successful responses from returned server records, but only pages using it receive that protection. Lists, approval inboxes, dashboards, counts, badges, and detail pages still do not share comprehensive event invalidation. `useBadges` now invalidates on `BadgesChanged` plus subscription acknowledgement and has no periodic interval while delivery is healthy; the shared provider owns visibility-aware catch-up only while degraded. Notifications invalidate on a user-channel event and catch up on channel acknowledgement or auth failure. Permission sync also refreshes its API-provided identity on acknowledgement or auth failure. Procurement Chain now subscribes through shared realtime hooks. While disconnected, the provider's active-query fallback covers all mounted API queries, replacing the prior subset of page-owned list timers; this fallback does not establish healthy-socket event coverage.

The PWA worker precaches a small app shell, uses network-first for navigation, and runtime-caches only Vite build assets under `/assets/`; API/Sanctum and all other non-navigation GETs remain network-only. A service-worker unit harness reproduced the old behavior for a synthetic same-origin private-download GET fixture: the general non-API branch entered Cache Storage, and global `caches.match` searched outside the app's named cache. The source audit did not confirm that a currently used authenticated download route sits outside `/api/`, so this was a verified unsafe policy path rather than a reproduced user incident. The worker skips that route class and reads/writes only its own named cache. The cache name is now scoped to the embedded SPA build ID; activation deletes caches from old builds and immutable hashed asset hits return without a redundant network request. The offline shell fallback also reads only that same named cache. Logout clears app-owned `ogami-` Cache Storage entries; development unregisters workers and clears owned caches. Production Nginx now marks `/sw.js` no-cache, while the version manifest is no-store. Hashed-asset header behavior has not been independently measured in this dev run.


### Current-state delta 99 — Production tabs can detect a newer SPA build

Vite production builds now embed a build ID and emit a matching public `version.json` manifest. The exact production Nginx route sends no-store cache directives. A global SPA notifier checks the manifest only in production, while the tab is visible, on focus, and at a one-minute interval; a mismatch offers Reload/Later and never forces a reload. Version fetches omit credentials. Vite development skips the check and continues using HMR. Source changes and config were reviewed only; no build, browser, or runtime header check was run under the instruction to stop testing.


### Current-state delta 100 — Offline shell fallback stays inside the Ogami cache

Source review found that navigation fallback called origin-wide `caches.match('/')`, which could return a root document held by another application cache namespace on the same origin. The service worker now opens `ogami-pwa-{buildId}` and reads only its own offline shell, returning an offline network error if it is unavailable. The focused VM-level `serviceWorkerFetchPolicy.test.ts` passes 8/8, including the own-cache-only fallback and API/Sanctum/network-only cases; `sw-register.test.ts` passes 3/3 for logout cache cleanup. This does not verify an installed floor-tablet/browser lifecycle.


### Current-state delta 101 — Service-worker cache generations follow SPA builds

Production service-worker registrations now include the same embedded build ID as the SPA bundle. The worker uses that ID in its Cache Storage name, so activation after a build switch removes old build caches even when worker source behavior is otherwise unchanged. Production Nginx explicitly marks `/sw.js` no-cache to let registrations resolve the current script. Worker tests now cover build-specific namespaces; no build, test, or browser update lifecycle was run.

At the baseline, API responses used `Cache-Control: no-cache, private`; no API-wide no-store or credential Vary policy existed. Auth-protected API responses now receive `private, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, `Vary: Cookie, Authorization`, and legacy no-cache headers from middleware, including rejected protected requests. No Nginx shared API response cache or API-wide ETag/Last-Modified implementation was found. These headers prevent browser/shared intermediary storage; they do not invalidate Laravel cache entries.

## Backend cache and Redis topology

Baseline discovery found one Redis process for both queue and cache: production DB 0/1 and local DB 2/3, with `noeviction` and no `maxmemory` bound. That avoided LRU eviction of queued jobs but left the process unbounded and allowed memory exhaustion to reject all writes. The Compose configuration now defines two processes: persistent `redis` for queue/default connections with a 256 MiB `noeviction` cap, and disposable `redis-cache` for cache connection with a 96 MiB `allkeys-lru` cap. Local queue data remains on DB 2; cache uses the configured `REDIS_CACHE_DB` (DB 3 locally and DB 0 by the production example) on the separate cache instance. Cache lock leases remain on the non-evicting default Redis connection. Session driver is database. Reverb scaling remains disabled, so it does not use Redis for cross-node pub/sub.

Cache keys are also environment-prefixed: the active local container has `APP_ENV=local` and `CACHE_PREFIX=ogami_local_cache_`; `.env.production.example` sets `ogami_erp_production_cache_`. Laravel's default prefix now derives from both `APP_NAME` and `APP_ENV`. Cache audit and cleanup operate only within the active prefix; when migrating prefixes, stop old consumers and remove only the exact retired app prefix from the cache Redis DB.

At discovery, Redis reported approximately 2.7 MB used memory, no maxmemory, noeviction, and active keys in DBs 0–3. DB 0/1 retained pre-switch entries; no global flush occurred. One specific local cache key, `procurement_chain_pr`, was removed only to establish a clean pre-mutation cache read for the reproduction. No cache flush command was run. During the later cache-host cutover, all 166 keys under the exact local application cache prefix in legacy Redis DB 3 were removed after API, Horizon, mail worker and scheduler restarted onto `redis-cache`; queue DB 2 was untouched. The legacy DB 3 then had zero keys. After the final live scenario the new cache instance's local DB 3 had 119 expiring keys. A `cache:audit` after the delayed-auth E2E inspected 88 keys (3 auth, 1 badge marker, 84 settings), with no missing TTLs, oversize entries or size-read failures. The latest scan after the RFQ/PO approval E2E inspected 113 keys (17 auth, 1 badge marker, 95 settings) and again found zero missing TTLs, oversized entries or unavailable sizes. These point-in-time samples do not prove all future writers are bounded.

Application Cache call sites include settings, user role permissions, dashboard and badge summaries, inventory/production dashboards, financial statements, holidays, government tables, payroll progress, DSS parameters and locks, output idempotency, procurement-chain aggregates, and operational dedupe/system-user entries. At initial discovery Cache::forever entries included analytics version counters, badge version, and the resolved system-user ID. Current application code no longer calls `Cache::forever`: these markers now use bounded TTLs and unique generations; the system-user key is scoped to its configured email. The framework's `queue:restart` command was also found to create an unbounded worker-control key; a post-command listener now gives it a 30-day TTL. JournalEntryObserver's tag flush originally ran inside model callbacks; it now waits for commit and has an explicit transaction regression test. Exact raw source hits are indexed in `00-cache-call-sites.csv`.

A current source scan finds no application use of global `Cache::flush()`, `Cache::forever()` or `rememberForever()`; tag-scoped statement invalidation remains intentionally allowed. Laravel's framework queue-restart command was an exception outside app source: it writes `illuminate:queue:restart` forever. The app now bounds that same control marker to 30 days after a successful `queue:restart` command. `CacheSafetyPolicyTest` now token-strips PHP comments and rejects global cache flushes/unbounded entries on the Cache facade and cache helper, including named stores. The guard passes (1 test / 1 assertion).

## Current-state deltas after baseline

### Current-state delta 793 — cached dashboards expose their snapshot age (2026-11-04 UTC)

`DashboardShell` only rendered freshness when a page passed `updatedAt`; several role dashboards did not pass it and their API response omitted a top-level generation timestamp. A red-first shared-shell regression failed to find “As of” when the response contained `generated_at`. The shell now uses `updatedAt` when present, otherwise the API snapshot's `generated_at`, and displays the time for fresh and stale snapshots. Cached dashboard services set `generated_at` inside their cache-miss closure, so a cache hit retains the snapshot's true generation time. Portal dashboards now include request-generation time; Production already returned `generated_at`, and the configurable layout already derives freshness from widget timestamps. Versioned dashboard key schemas were advanced so timestamp-less payloads are bypassed after deploy and expire within their existing 30-second TTL. The focused component regression passes (DashboardShell file 6/6); one Admin shape test passes with 10 assertions and the known container warning; ten touched PHP services pass syntax checks; targeted ESLint and SPA `npm run typecheck` pass. This verifies a display/response-contract gap, not a stale-value incident; no two-user socket scenario ran and no matrix cells were promoted. No cache payload was deleted.

The live dev path was checked after this change: `https://ogamiph.dev` and `/@vite/client` return HTTP 200, the Vite-served DashboardShell module contains the new timestamp code, and API-container OPcache timestamp validation is enabled with a two-second revalidation frequency. This confirms source pickup settings and SPA serving, not a role-authenticated API read.

### Current-state delta 794 — dashboard widget queries still poll while connected (source audit, 2026-11-04 UTC)

`spa/src/components/dashboard/RoleDashboardShell.tsx` and `spa/src/pages/dashboard/default.tsx` both set `refetchInterval: DASHBOARD_REFRESH_MS` (60 seconds) on active widget-data queries. This is normal connected polling, while the freshness contract reserves polling for disconnected/degraded fallback. `dashboard_layout_changed` and selected entity signals invalidate widget data, and the provider has disconnected active-query catch-up; however, a complete per-widget source-to-channel subscription/producer map is not proven. Removing the interval before that coverage is completed could leave widgets stale, so this deviation remains open for the dashboard event-coverage slice. No user-visible stale symptom was reproduced from this interval and no code or runtime matrix cell changed.

- Auth-protected API responses now receive `Cache-Control: private, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, `Pragma: no-cache`, `Expires: 0`, and `Vary: Cookie, Authorization` from `PreventAuthenticatedApiCaching`. Feature coverage includes an authenticated response and a rejected request to a protected route. ETag/If-Match is not implemented.
- Admin cache-generation rotation now stages a content-free `CacheGroupInvalidated` in the same transaction as its audit row. Its after-commit publisher runs after cache generation rotation for dashboard, statement, and procurement-chain groups. The SPA invalidates mapped active query families; the acting tab schedules its invalidation from the successful API response. A focused channel test verifies permission-matched access and denies unrelated/unregistered scopes; live socket delivery and two-user refresh remain unverified.
- The dedicated `broadcasts` Horizon supervisor has one local worker. `ChainStepAdvanced` sends its content-free `chain.refresh` signal to an authorized per-record channel and an entity-list channel; the payload is only `refresh` and a UUID `event_id`.
- Follow-up source audit on 2026-10-30 found that the 15 direct `ShouldBroadcast` classes present in that audit lacked a uniform after-commit/dedicated-lane contract. Only three implemented `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`, while both Redis queue connections had `after_commit=false`. Laravel 12's `BroadcastManager::queue()` reads `$event->connection` and `broadcastQueue()`; a `broadcastConnection()` method alone does not select the connection. The shared `BroadcastsOnDedicatedQueue` trait now sets the actual `$connection` property, the `broadcasts` queue, and bounded retry delays, and all 31 current broadcast classes implement `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`. `BroadcastEventContractTest` discovered-vs-listed inventory guard, private-channel source guard, transaction test, framework queue-manager selection test, and explicit payload whitelist checks pass (the current combined payroll/auth/contract run passed 57 warning-marked tests / 496 assertions); the transaction test proves no job is queued before commit and a job is queued after commit. PHPUnit warnings include attempts to read absent `/var/www/.env`; the container receives test configuration through process environment. This standardizes broadcaster contracts only: it does not make every mutation emit a domain event or prove every producer's transaction boundary.
- A source audit of the original 15 broadcaster serializers found that `BadgesChanged` had no `broadcastWith()` allow-list and could use Laravel's public-property serialization; permission-override broadcasts also included a permission slug, old/new grant type and free-text reason that its SPA handler did not consume; payroll progress broadcasts included run counters and status. Each now has an explicit content-limited serializer. The payroll SPA treats the signal as a ping and refetches its authorized period resource; a terminal signal invalidates the payroll list. `UserNotificationCreated` retains only the notification ID/type used to fetch the item through its authorized API. `BroadcastEventContractTest` now requires explicit serializers and private-channel constructors for all 27 current classes; focused permission and payroll serializer tests pass (1 / 3 and 2 / 5 assertions). Static/source test evidence confirms the serializer boundary; no unauthorized data receipt was reproduced. See `00-broadcast-payload-audit.csv` for every channel callback and current wire shape.
- The SPA provider now centrally refetches all active TanStack queries with visibility-aware exponential backoff while disconnected/reconnecting; the earlier list-specific fallback calls were removed to prevent duplicate timers. The focused provider suite passes 3 tests, including an offline first catch-up at 5 seconds; the inventory event-hook test passes 1 test and the full SPA TypeScript check passes. This proves fallback mechanics only, not correct event routing for all active query families.
- `ProcurementChainCacheObserver` invalidates PR/PO/GRN/Bill aggregates after commit. `BadgeInvalidationObserver`, shared `BadgeService::touch()`, `User::flushPermissionsCache()`, and UserAdmin single/bulk role-key invalidation now defer shared-cache mutation until commit. Explicit transaction tests cover P2P summary, badge-version and permission-key invalidation.
- `InventoryDashboardService` now reads a generation-scoped 30-second stock snapshot. The durable stock movement and reservation outbox paths rotate stock-derived cache generations after commit and queue a content-free `InventoryStockChanged` ping to private Inventory, item-catalog, Production work-order, Maintenance work-order, and Quality NCR list channels, each using the corresponding API permission boundary. Active authorized queries refetch through their own APIs; disconnected active screens use visibility-aware backoff. Delta 434 adds the `inventory.reservations` family to the central stock-signal map; `StockLevelsPage` retains its stock listener while the Reservations tab is selected, so linked issue movements can refresh those active rows. This shared mapping may also refetch reservations after unrelated stock movements; only active queries refetch and adjacent events coalesce, while performance impact remains unmeasured. The Inventory dashboard's redundant page-level 30-second timer has been removed. A two-role stock-list browser check covers only a stock-adjustment approval path, not every Inventory mutation or scope. Dashboard UI and the newly added linked-channel fan-out have not had a two-role browser run.
- Redis cache and queue traffic now use separate instances with bounded memory: 96 MiB `allkeys-lru` on cache, and 256 MiB `noeviction` on the persistent queue Redis. Local dev was switched, and a 166-key legacy cache-prefix cleanup was performed after restarting all PHP cache consumers. The post-switch audit found 119 local cache keys with TTLs and no no-TTL application cache keys. Production Compose and environment defaults are updated but production deployment has not been executed or verified.
- `PurchaseOrderService::create/update/delete/restore/submit()` now stage the standard `ChainStepAdvanced` refresh in their transaction; PR conversion-state changes also stage a PR refresh when visible conversion fields change. Submit's missing signal was reproduced before the change and the after-fix flow was verified with a buyer actor and Finance viewer on open detail and list pages.
- A focused Purchasing source audit found `PurchaseOrderService::acknowledgeBudget()` updated the PO budget-acknowledgment fields without staging a refresh, unlike the PR counterpart. A red-first test failed with zero chain outbox rows; the service now locks/rechecks visibility and stages the refresh inside its transaction. The focused regression passes (1 test / 2 assertions). This is source/test evidence only; actor and cross-role screens were not browser-tested.
- `SalesOrderService::create()` now stages the same draft refresh after its lines are persisted, inside the order transaction. Before the change, a CRM actor's create returned 201 while a different-role System Administrator's open sales-order list stayed empty through four seconds and only showed the record after manual reload. Four after-fix two-context runs rendered the row without reload: response-to-render was 2,370 ms (event +1,733 ms), 981 ms (event +33 ms; list response +333 ms), 2,430 ms (event +1,346 ms; list response +1,855 ms), and 1,286 ms after the outbox-lane change (event +137 ms; list response +609 ms). In the 2,430 ms run, telemetry measured the outbox/high first-hop queue wait at 1,196–1,281 ms; the `broadcasts` hop waited 12.8–22.3 ms. After routing the outbox/high worker through the one-second `redis_broadcasts` connection with zero idle sleep, the next run measured 12.3–99.6 ms for the outbox jobs and 35.6 ms for the chain broadcast. This supports the targeted queue-latency fix; four API-response samples are not percentiles or commit-to-UI measurements. The 1,286 ms render sample was within the proposed 1–2 second target. Other-role detail, buttons, related data, counts and dashboards were not tested in this create scenario. The focused backend route file passed 13 tests / 76 assertions. Related Sales Order write paths now stage this refresh too: update/delete/restore are covered by one-event-per-write assertions; response submission/resolution, date negotiation, request-customer-confirmation and no-charge replacement creation use the same path. Focused response and Return Management tests verify only a subset; their browser behavior remains unverified.
- The same telemetry-driven optimization now routes `DispatchOutboxMessage` on the `high` queue via `redis_broadcasts`; the high and broadcast supervisors use `sleep=0` and the connection's one-second `block_for`. The queue-lane regression failed before the configuration/job change and passes afterward. This preserves separate `high` and `broadcasts` queue names and the same persistent Redis storage. The measured before/after pair is a limited local sample; the high-priority queue may contain more types of outbox work and still needs load/throughput monitoring.
- Distinct rapid chain writes now receive UUID event IDs, and durable outbox dedupe uses the event ID instead of `updated_at`. A regression exposed that database timestamp precision could otherwise collapse two writes in the same second; targeted tests now assert separate refresh entries.
- The serial live-procurement Playwright scenario `manual low-value PR reaches Finance and skips VP` now keeps the requester's detail and list mounted while Finance approves. Across two isolated role contexts, the detail changed to Approved, Cancel disappeared, and the list status changed to Approved without reload; the VP step remained skipped. Total scenario time was 23.9 seconds; commit-to-UI latency was not sampled. The E2E harness exempts only the URL-less console form of a 401; URL-aware response checks still fail non-API 401 and all unexpected API 4xx/5xx.
- The SPA now has a root `RealtimeProvider`, shared ref-counted subscriptions, catch-up on reconnect/visibility/focus, a central invalidation map and hooks, and a global connection indicator. Current map/page wiring covers eight O2C/P2P chain entity types, not all modules or mutations. Auth identity/role/employee-scope/delegation/permission/feature changes clear the query cache; permission-sync also refreshes `/me` on focus/visibility return.
- Two-context browser evidence now covers selected Purchase Request, Purchase Order, Sales Order, inventory stock-adjustment, and RFQ create/publish paths. This is still a small subset of the 679 mutating routes. No project-wide matrix result, all-role authorization proof, all-cache invalidation guarantee, or latency percentile is claimed.
- RFQ source review found that the internal create/edit/detail/list/quote/compare views did not use the shared realtime hooks, and the existing RFQ lifecycle event contains supplier/quote snapshots unsuitable for a shared refresh channel. A separate content-free `RequestForQuoteChanged` signal now stages refreshes from RFQ and supplier-quote write services after commit; it targets an API-authorized private record channel and a purchasing-view-authorized entity-list channel. A red-first service test observed zero outbox rows for RFQ creation before the new seam and now asserts one event per create/publish/update. Two two-context Playwright runs (`live-rfq-bidding.spec.ts`) verified draft-create list appearance and publish detail status/action refresh without reload; the publish API-response-to-viewer-render samples were 324 ms and 511 ms. The latest run also verified Finance → VP PO approval action progression and the final Sent status/action refresh across the two open detail pages (1,185 ms and 1,743 ms response-to-UI samples). This is source evidence and selected browser coverage, not a pre-fix user-visible RFQ reproduction or a p95. Quote submission, award, supplier UI, related-dashboard, badge, inbox, and all-channel-scope behavior remain unverified. Focused results: RFQ bidding 22 tests / 268 assertions before the latest exact-count additions, affected tests 2 / 55 after them; channel authorization 23 / 72; event contract 6 / 170; frontend RFQ hook/map tests and E2E TypeScript check passed. See the per-route matrix and `PROGRESS.md` for exact limits.

## Two-session reproductions

Each scenario used separate Playwright browser contexts with cookie isolation, a headless Chromium browser from Playwright 1.63.0, and seeded demo roles. Passwords and session cookies were not saved in evidence. The tests were run one scenario at a time.

Reported-symptom outcome: (1) the acting user's stale screen was not reproduced on the tested PR approval/cancellation, PO submission, SO creation, stock-adjustment approval, RFQ create/publish or PO approval paths; the actor rendered the successful API result/action state immediately in those exercised paths. That does not rule out other endpoints/pages. (2) another-role stale state was reproduced for PR detail approval, PR summary cancellation, PO detail/list submission and SO list creation. After fixes, the tested PR cancellation removed the viewer's stale Cancel action, and the latest PO approval E2E showed the VP's action appear after Finance approval and the Finance viewer's Sent status/action update after VP approval. (3) stale cached data was reproduced for the procurement-chain summary after the PR database count changed; a manual reload still received the stale Laravel-cached count despite the API's no-cache response headers.

A later pair of three-context HR scenarios verified salary-adjustment approve and reject: the acting Administrator's WebSocket was forcibly disabled and its open queue row still changed from the successful API response; the HR maker's open detail and a second Administrator's approval board updated through private record/list signals and authorized refetches. `PayrollAdjustmentChanged` and `BadgesChanged` frames were received. The approval/rejection board-refetch responses were 1,387 ms and 1,185 ms after the actor API responses in single samples. These are not commit-to-UI percentiles and do not cover linked records or numeric badge counts. The real-backend test is `spa/e2e-real/payroll-adjustment-realtime.spec.ts`; it created and approved one adjustment and created and rejected another against existing payroll rows in the current dev database.

### 1. Acting user's own screen after final approval

At 2026-10-29 19:41:20Z, a Vice President and a System Administrator opened the same pending demo purchase request. The actor approved it through the UI. PATCH /api/v1/purchasing/purchase-requests/{hash}/approve returned 200 at 19:41:37.378Z. The actor's detail refetch returned 200 at 19:41:37.623Z (about 245 ms later); the actor's page rendered Approved. This reproduces a successful actor update for this transition. It does not prove all mutations update their actor's page.

### 2. Other role viewing the same record

Before approval, the System Administrator's detail page fetched status pending. Its WebSocket was connected and authenticated subscriptions to settings, private user, and private badges succeeded. The Purchase Request detail page had no chain/record subscription, and there was no purchase-request detail refetch during the observation window. The actor's approval therefore did not update that viewer's status until the manual page reload, which then fetched the approved record. The UI gates Cancel and Approve controls using the actions object on that record response; the exact stale button label was not retained in the concise trace, so the button cell is partial/inferred rather than claimed as directly observed.

The browser event trace shows a healthy Reverb connection and successful private-channel auth. This scenario supports a client coverage/invalidation cause rather than a Reverb outage. It was one purchase-request record and one pair of roles, not proof for every module or permission combination.

### 3. Cached data after a status change

At 2026-10-29 19:44:19Z, a Purchasing user opened the Procurement Chain page in one context. It showed 2 pending PRs and 4 approved PRs. Its API response was 200 with Cache-Control: no-cache, private. The Laravel procurement-chain summary cache key was cleared once before this read so the observed value was freshly calculated and remembered.

A System Administrator in the second context cancelled a pending PR through the UI. The mutation response was 200 and the database then had one draft-or-pending PR. The Purchasing user's page did not receive a procurement-chain subscription; the event it did receive was BadgesChanged on the badges channel. The still-open page remained at 2 pending. A full manual reload also returned and displayed 2 pending / 4 approved because the backend key procurement_chain_pr remained populated for its 60-second TTL. A fresh database count was 1 draft-or-pending PR. The controller uses Cache::remember('procurement_chain_pr', 60, ...) and no invalidation path was found. This reproduces both stale list/dashboard data and stale server-side cache despite a no-cache HTTP response.

These scenarios prove two independent failure layers: missing client subscription/refetch for record detail and missing backend aggregate-cache invalidation. They do not establish a project-wide p95 latency or prove all transitions.

### 3. After-fix cancellation check (2026-10-29 20:10 UTC)

The local/Vite stack was exercised through the usable public origin `https://ogamiph.dev` in one headless Chromium process with two isolated browser contexts. System Administrator acted; Purchasing Officer viewed Procurement Chain. Both authenticated successfully, the viewer's `Live` indicator was visible, and private-channel authorization returned 200. The only remaining draft PR (`PR-20261029-0003`) was opened by the actor and cancelled through the UI. The actor's PATCH returned 200 and the detail page rendered `Cancelled` without waiting for an event. The viewer initially saw one pending PR and, without navigation or refresh, changed to zero; its socket received `BadgesChanged`. An authorized API read in the viewer context returned zero. The client observation loop saw the rendered change within 2.4 seconds after the actor's status had rendered; this is a single coarse upper bound, not a commit-to-UI timing sample or SLO proof. The viewer was not on the record detail page, so detail status/action-button behavior remains unverified by this after-fix run. The cancelled row was not deleted.

### 4. After-fix detail, action-button, outbox and dedicated-queue check (2026-10-29 ~20:24 UTC)

Using two isolated Chromium contexts at `https://ogamiph.dev`, a System Administrator actor and a Purchasing viewer opened a fresh draft PR (`PR-202610-0168`). The viewer had both the record detail and Procurement Chain tabs open. The actor cancelled the PR through the UI: PATCH returned 200 and the actor detail rendered `Cancelled`. Without navigation or refresh, the viewer's detail received `chain.refresh`, refetched the authorized API, and rendered `Cancelled`; its formerly actionable Cancel button disappeared. The viewer's summary tab changed from `1 pending PRs` to `0 pending PRs` and received `BadgesChanged`. All observed private channel authorization requests returned 200. The actor-to-viewer-detail render interval was 1.33 seconds; this is one sample measured after the actor screen rendered, not a commit-to-UI p95 or SLO result.

This run exercised the actual domain outbox path after queue tuning. The `ChainStepAdvanced` outbox row was `published` with one attempt; the live Horizon process list showed `supervisor-broadcasts` consuming `redis_broadcasts:broadcasts` with one worker, `--sleep=0`, and a one-second Redis blocking interval. A separate synthetic queue ping also reached the subscribed viewer and caused another authorized detail refetch. The fresh draft PR used for this test was intentionally cancelled and retained; no row was deleted.

### 5. After-fix inventory stock-list delivery check (2026-10-29)

One serial headless Playwright run used three isolated browser contexts on `https://ogamiph.dev`: Finance acted on a pending stock adjustment, while Warehouse and System Administrator each kept a Stock Levels page open for the same newly provisioned test item/location. The test required both stock-list pages to show the connection indicator as `Live` before approval, so the visibility-aware degraded polling path was not the mechanism under test. The Finance approval endpoint returned 200. Without reloading either stock list, both role-scoped pages refetched through the API and rendered the new quantity; the same scenario then completed its existing GL, audit, and reject/withdraw assertions. Test duration was 52.5 seconds and the single test passed.

The instrumented sample measured 325 ms from the Finance API response until both list refetch responses had arrived, 558 ms from the API response until both rows rendered the expected quantity, and approximately 1,235 ms from the response's `approved_at` value to both renders. `approved_at` is generated before the transaction commit and has only timestamp precision, so this last number is an approximation, not exact commit-to-UI latency. These are one scenario's measurements, not a percentile or SLO. The E2E typecheck also passed. This check does not prove all stock writers use the outbox, all user scopes receive the signal, or inventory detail/dashboard/badge behavior is correct.

### 6. Purchase Order submit event omission and after-fix check (2026-10-30)

One headless Playwright scenario used isolated Purchasing buyer contexts. Before the code change, buyer A submitted a draft PO and received HTTP 200; A's detail rendered `Pending approval`. Buyer B had the same record detail and PO list open and both still showed `Draft` after the five-second observation window. Buyer's socket was connected and private subscriptions succeeded, but the captured server event names contained only connection/subscription frames and `BadgesChanged`; there was no `chain.refresh`, and neither viewer page refetched. This is direct evidence for the PO submit transition only.

The source path agreed with the browser trace: `PurchaseOrderService::submit()` wrote `PurchaseOrderSubmitted` to the outbox for its notification listener but did not call the shared `broadcastChain()` path that the PO approval, rejection, send, cancel, and close transitions use. A focused PHPUnit regression failed before the fix because `event_outbox` contained zero `ChainStepAdvanced` rows for the submit; it passed after adding that centralized call, with a matching `chain_step_runs` row for `purchase_order` / `pending_approval`.

The role-verified two-context Playwright scenario uses a Purchasing buyer actor and a Finance viewer whose `/auth/user` response must have `role.slug=finance_officer`. The test now waits for the record and entity-list `subscription_succeeded` acknowledgements, then requires `chain.refresh` on both viewer tabs and `Pending approval` without reload. Post-catch-up samples took 1,251, 1,676, and 1,343 ms from API response until both viewer pages rendered. Queue telemetry later measured one `ChainStepAdvanced` queue wait at 1,987.2 ms (5.0 ms execution), explaining a 3,119 ms sample; after the dedicated low-latency worker change, observed chain queue waits were 2.2–3.3 ms and the latest API-response-to-both-render sample was 1,262 ms. In that run event receipts were +64/+51 ms and detail/list refetch responses +529/+725 ms. Samples do not establish commit-to-UI p95. One unguarded repeat failed to receive `chain.refresh` while the socket was Live; it did not record channel-ack timestamps, so its exact cause remains unassigned. The actor uses the successful API response path; this scenario does not assert action-button removal/addition. Finance has route-level purchasing read permission, and the API applies `PurchaseOrderAccessPolicy` to detail/refetch requests.

The trace also exposed redundant query invalidation: before the fix, one event called `invalidateQueries` ten times, triggering three PO-detail GETs, four badge GETs and a reference-options GET on the detail page. The central invalidation helper now traverses active queries once, and PO query keys distinguish detail, list and reference data. Post-fix two-role runs show one PO-detail GET and one PO-list GET, with no PO-options refetch. The same run's channel acknowledgements and queue logs make the previously missed subscription-readiness and worker-wait layers observable; other query families still need a project-wide request-cost audit.

A second serial headless run exercised the late-subscription case deterministically: the Finance viewer's record-channel authorization request was held while the actor submitted and the viewer's separately subscribed list consumed `chain.refresh`. The open detail stayed Draft and received no `chain.refresh`; after the auth request was released it acknowledged the subscription 168 ms later and its subscription-ready catch-up refetched the authorized detail API, rendering Pending approval 466 ms after release. The intentional auth hold lasted 8,070 ms. This verifies recovery when a mutation occurs before the record subscription is ready; the 466 ms interval measures catch-up after release, not end-to-end commit latency. One forced-delay E2E is not a percentile/SLO sample.

After channel-auth failures began setting the global state to `Reconnecting`, a rerun verified the degraded-mode behavior rather than requiring the page to remain stale during the intentional outage. With auth held for 8,257 ms, the Finance viewer's detail received no `chain.refresh` and had not subscribed to the record channel, but its visibility-aware fallback made an authorized detail GET and rendered Pending approval 3,338 ms after the submit response. The list received its entity event at +1,373 ms and rendered Pending approval; the list refetch response was +3,158 ms. After auth release, the record channel acknowledged in 194 ms and detail remained current. This is a forced-auth-failure sample and exceeded the 1–2 s target for both the fallback detail render and list refetch; it is not a normal-path SLO measurement or a percentile. The separate normal event sample remains 1,262 ms to both viewer renders. The latest E2E passed and recorded a detail GET while authorization was held, so the fresh detail state is attributable to the degraded API fallback, not a received WebSocket event.

After replacing page-owned list timers with the root-provider fallback, the same single PO E2E passed again: the detail channel auth was held 7,049 ms, the list received its entity event +67 ms after the submit response, an authorized detail GET returned +1,206 ms and the detail rendered +2,232 ms, while the list refetch returned +2,041 ms and both viewers rendered within +2,673 ms. This remains an intentionally degraded single sample, not p95; the screen's status/actions updated without reload. The trace counted 13 successful API GETs on the detail page and 7 on the list after submit, including repeated badge/notification reads and reference/options endpoints; the exact contributors to these extra requests remain to be profiled and reduced. No API response data was broadcast.

The list signal does not include a record ID, status, document number, actor, or other record data. The private list channel requires the enabled feature and the entity's list-read permission; each page refetches its current query through the authenticated API. PO-specific detail channel authorization uses the same `PurchaseOrderAccessPolicy` as the detail endpoint. Seeded `purchasing_officer` is granted `purchasing.po.view_all` by the purchasing permission catalog, so the earlier buyer/buyer run was same-role but remained within that role's company-wide PO scope. The final test uses Finance to exercise a cross-role viewer. Channel timing can still reveal that some entity in a readable module changed; scope-specific recipient fan-out is not implemented and remains a security review item for entity families whose list API visibility is narrower than their list permission.

The PO write audit also covered service create/update/delete/restore signals and partial PR conversion state. PHPUnit verifies staging for these service paths, but no two-context browser run covered them. A test also caught a dedupe collision: two writes within one DB timestamp tick produced the same `updated_at`-derived key and only one outbox record. Event-ID dedupe fixes this; targeted broadcaster/outbox tests pass.

### 7. Sales Order draft create event omission and after-fix check (2026-10-30)

Before the service change, the CRM actor's draft create returned HTTP 201 and rendered the returned record on its detail page. A System Administrator had the Sales Orders list open, had an acknowledged `private-entity.sales_order.visible` subscription, and did not receive `chain.refresh`; the row remained absent through the four-second observation. A manual reload then fetched and displayed it. The backend regression test likewise failed with zero `ChainStepAdvanced` outbox rows.

`SalesOrderService::create()` now stages a `draft` refresh after all sales-order lines are created and before its transaction returns. The route file passes 13 tests / 76 assertions, including create, portal pre-confirmation mapping, and one-event-per-write checks for the draft CRUD path. The after-fix two-context Playwright scenario passed four times: the actor detail showed Draft; each viewer list received `chain.refresh` and rendered the row without reload. Response-to-render measured 2,370 ms, 981 ms, 2,430 ms before the outbox-lane change, and 1,286 ms after it. In the last run event arrival was +137 ms and the list refetch response +609 ms from the actor API response. The slow run showed 1,196–1,281 ms waiting for `DispatchOutboxMessage` on `high`; the subsequent run's outbox waits were 12.3–99.6 ms and chain broadcast wait was 35.6 ms after the high supervisor moved to the fast Redis alias with zero sleep. Four single API-response samples do not establish commit-to-render p95. The tests did not open the viewer's detail page or exercise its actions, related records, counts, badges or dashboards.

On 2026-11-02, the scenario was run twice more against `https://ogamiph.dev` with Chromium and two isolated contexts (CRM actor and System Administrator viewer). The first rerun's create returned HTTP 201; the viewer captured `chain.refresh` 22 ms after the API response, the authorized Sales Order list GET completed 763 ms after the response, and the row rendered 1,718 ms after the response. Actor create duration was 493 ms. After the shared response reconciler was wired into the create page, the current-code rerun again returned 201: event +116 ms, list GET response +1,123 ms, row render +1,231 ms after the API response, with actor create duration 535 ms. The current-code submit-start-to-peer-render interval was 1,766 ms. These are individual samples, not commit-to-render p95; no status transition or peer detail/action view was exercised. The two reruns left two Draft Sales Orders in the local dev database.

### 8. Purchase Request draft create event omission and after-fix check (2026-10-30 ~00:59–01:02 UTC)

Two isolated Chromium contexts used a Purchasing Officer actor and a System Administrator viewer at `https://ogamiph.dev`. The viewer opened the PR list, the SPA showed Live, and `private-entity.purchase_request.visible` acknowledged before the actor saved. The actor's POST returned 201 and rendered Draft. In the baseline trace archived under `/tmp/ogami-realtime-pr-create-baseline-evidence/`, the viewer received only connection/subscription acknowledgements and `BadgesChanged`; no `chain.refresh` arrived, and PR-202610-0040 did not appear within eight seconds. The test failed at the row assertion. The API event audit found no chain staging in `PurchaseRequestService::create()`.

The service now stages one draft ping after its line items are created, inside the transaction. The same UI path passed at approximately 01:02 UTC: actor POST 201; the viewer received `chain.refresh` 49 ms after the actor's API response; the authorized PR list GET began at +652 ms; and the new row rendered at +1,311 ms without reload. The test's response-to-render sample is within the proposed 1–2 second target but is not commit-to-UI p95. Its after-fix trace is archived under `/tmp/ogami-realtime-pr-create-after/`.

The same service-level regression test failed before the change with zero `ChainStepAdvanced` outbox entries. It now asserts that draft create, edit, delete, and restore each stage exactly one refresh (1 test / 4 assertions). Browser coverage covers create only; the other three routes still lack two-role UI verification. `PurchaseRequestService` also now signals intermediate approval, budget acknowledgment, and visible sourcing-method changes. Those paths are source-covered but not yet separately exercised in two contexts.

### 9. RFQ and PO approval action progression (2026-10-30)

One serial headless run of `live-rfq-bidding.spec.ts` passed (one test, 2.1 minutes) with isolated browser contexts and the dev application at `https://ogamiph.dev`. The RFQ section repeated the draft-list insertion and publish-detail refresh: a System Administrator saw the RFQ appear in the open list after `entity.request_for_quote.visible`, then saw Open replace Draft and Publish disappear after `rfq.refresh`. The publish API-response-to-viewer-render sample was 511 ms in this run (an earlier passing run measured 324 ms).

The same workflow then tested the Purchase Order approval actions across roles. A VP opened the pending PO detail and had no Approve action while Finance was the current step. Finance approved; the VP's already-open record subscription received `chain.refresh`, refetched the authorized resource, and rendered Approve 1,185 ms after the Finance API response. Finance's own Approve action disappeared after its successful response. The VP then approved; the Finance viewer's already-open detail received `chain.refresh`, rendered Sent and removed its stale Approve action 1,743 ms after the VP API response. Each client trace included the private record event. These are response-to-render/action samples from one workflow, not commit-to-UI p95. The lists, approval inbox badge counts, linked records and dashboards were not held open/asserted during these PO approvals. RFQ and PO backend/API authorization remain source-tested separately; the scenario did not test socket-disabled actor behavior.

### 10. Supplier catalog refresh path (source/test evidence, 2026-10-30)

A source audit found that supplier listing submission, edits and review decisions had no content-free catalog refresh signal. Other writers also changed approved-supplier rows outside `ApprovedSupplierService`: PO approval creates or refreshes provisional links, approved-supplier CSV imports create rows and can roll them back, and the historical backfill command creates rows. Red-first outbox tests observed zero supplier-catalog refresh rows on listing submission, PO approval, CSV import and backfill. The code now stages the shared `SupplierCatalogChanged` outbox event from these paths, and the frontend supplier-listing and approved-supplier list screens subscribe through the central list hook. `SupplierCatalogChanged` uses private list channels, API permission gates, and a payload allowlist containing only `refresh`, `event_id`, `version`, and `action`.

Focused verification passes for the listing signal (2 tests / 11 assertions), approved-supplier service lifecycle and PO link creation (5 / 8), CSV import and rollback (5 / 22), backfill (3 / 9), supplier channel authorization (1 / 2; full broadcast authorization file 25 / 82), frontend invalidation and subscription (11 / 11), and SPA/E2E typechecks. The full supplier-listing feature file completed 17 tests / 87 assertions with one existing failure in the unrelated pending-badge permission assertion; it is documented in `FINDINGS.md`.

### 11. Supplier portal approval freshness (two-context reproduction and retest, 2026-10-30)

Before the portal channel work, two isolated Chromium contexts reproduced the stale screen at about 02:10 UTC: a Purchasing user approved the supplier's listing (PATCH 200); Purchasing immediately rendered Approved and removed Approve, while the supplier portal still rendered Pending review with Edit listing. The supplier context received no supplier catalog event and made no listing GET during a five-second observation (2 GETs before and after). This was the cross-guard case: the internal SPA used `/api/v1/broadcasting/auth` with `auth:sanctum`, while supplier sessions use `supplier_portal`; no supplier broadcast-auth route or vendor-private subscription existed.

After adding a vendor-owned private channel, separate supplier-guard broadcast authorization, and a portal list hook, the same two-context Chromium workflow passed without page reload. The supplier auth request returned 200; its socket acknowledged `private-supplier.vendor.{vendorHashId}` and received the listing-created and approval refresh events. The supplier's authorized item-listing GET count increased from 4 before approval to 5 after, and its status changed to Approved with Edit listing removed 471 ms after the Purchasing approval API response. Purchasing rendered Approved and removed its Approve action from its successful response. The event payload contains no vendor, item, listing, price, actor or record data. This is one normal-path response-to-UI sample, not commit-to-UI p95 or full module coverage.

The broader supplier-listing backend file still has the unrelated pending-badge permission failure noted in `FINDINGS.md`. Bulk portal submission, internal bulk approval, linked records, badges, dashboards, inboxes and other supplier-catalog transitions remain unverified in the browser. The event remains a list-level ping and omits record IDs, actor identity and related-entity references required by the Phase 2 standard event envelope; it is a scoped implementation, not completion of that contract.

Bulk supplier operations initially emitted one outbox refresh per successful row. Red-first tests reproduced two `SupplierCatalogChanged` outbox rows for two submitted or approved listings. The bulk service now stages one refresh after the successful row transactions, and bulk approval/rejection includes the unique private channel for each affected vendor while retaining the content-free broadcast payload. Two-row submit and mixed-vendor approve/reject cases are covered by backend tests. This reduces bursts for these three supplier endpoints only; coalescing across other bulk/import/scheduled paths remains unverified and the matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 12. Driver run-sheet freshness after office reschedule (two-context reproduction and retest, 2026-10-30)

Before the driver subscription change, an office user rescheduled an assigned delivery while a separate driver context kept its run-sheet detail open. The office screen reflected the committed date, while the driver detail retained the prior date through the ten-second observation; the driver page had no personal-channel subscription or authorized detail refetch. This reproduced a delivery workflow miss independently of the previously tested purchasing and supplier chains.

The delivery assignment/reschedule signal now targets the private user channels of the current and, on reassignment, previous driver. The driver route boundary subscribes once through the shared reference-counted realtime layer and invalidates only active `driver` queries. The signal is content-free; the driver then fetches its own scoped delivery API. Channel tests verify a driver can subscribe to its own user channel and cannot subscribe to a different driver’s channel. In the latest post-fix two-context Chromium run, the office reschedule returned HTTP 200, the driver received one `chain.refresh` on its own private channel 15 ms after the API response, made the authorized detail GET 1 ms after the event, and rendered the new planned date 499 ms after the API response, without reload. The test then restored the original scheduled date and asserted the driver API returned it. This is one response-to-render observation, not commit-to-UI p95. The scenario covered a driver detail refresh only: the actor’s actual UI control, driver lists, action buttons, linked records, badges, dashboards, offline recovery and other delivery transitions remain unverified.

**Current-source correction (delta 321):** `DriverRealtimeBoundary` and `useDriverDeliveryRealtime` are present and cover the driver route tree; the former discovery note at delta 320 saying no DriverDeliveryDetail/List subscription existed was stale. The normal seeded-driver run above authorized through the shared `user.{hash}` channel because seeded roles include `notifications.view`. Source inspection found that valid custom driver roles need only `supply_chain.driver.access`, so they could be denied that channel. The hook now joins exact private `driver.{userHashId}` with matching Supply Chain feature/permission checks. `ChainStepAdvanced` continues to target the legacy personal user channel for old bundles. Focused regression tests were authored but not run; custom-role runtime authorization and current-channel event delivery remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 13. Delivery create/archive/restore producer audit (backend regression evidence, 2026-10-30)

The delivery run-sheet route inventory showed that create, soft delete and restore were separate mutation producers from reschedule/status. Before changing them, red-first tests observed zero `ChainStepAdvanced` outbox entries after create and zero after a delete+restore pair. `DeliveryService::create()` now stages one scheduled refresh in the same transaction after item rows and stock reservations are written; idempotent retries return the existing record without staging another event. `delete()` and `restore()` each stage one event after their write while still in their transaction. The serial backend regressions now pass: `DeliveryCreateIdempotencyTest.php` 4 tests/12 assertions and `DeliveryDeleteGuardsTest.php` 5 tests/10 assertions. The test container reports a warning because `/var/www/.env` is absent; there were no assertion failures. No browser scenario exercised create, delete or restore, so all their actor, viewer, list, button and side-effect UI matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. Other delivery mutations (shipment plan, proofs/receipts, retry handoffs, truck returns and stock reservation) still need producer coverage.

### 14. Driver attempt-report, depot receipt and recovery freshness (backend regression evidence, 2026-10-30)

Source tracing of `DeliveryAttemptOutcomeService` showed that an attempt report sets the delivery to `return_pending`, depot reconciliation changes it to `delivered`/`returned`, corrections revise quantities, and late recovery changes linked return and cost records; none staged the chain refresh. Red-first tests failed with missing outbox rows for report, correction and late-recovery writes. The service now stages one content-free refresh after each successful report, depot receipt, correction and late recovery, in the same transaction as the related writes. Request-key replays remain idempotent and do not add another event. The complete affected `DeliveryAttemptOutcomeServiceTest.php` passes (14 tests/166 assertions), including the report/reconcile, correction/replay and late-recovery/replay regressions. A failed-green run exposed that `ChainDefinitions` also omitted valid statuses `return_pending` and `returned`; both now resolve to the last outbound checkpoint `in_transit`, so the event does not imply customer delivery or confirmation.
