The full `ChainDefinitionsTest.php` passes (23 tests/101 assertions). PHP tests warn that the API container has no `/var/www/.env` and log expected notification suppression cases, without assertion failures. These are service/outbox tests only; none of these status/action changes has a two-role browser scenario, so every UI cell remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 15. Delivery receipt/proof refresh gap (2026-10-30)

A red-first backend regression uploaded receipt evidence to a delivery already in `delivered`, where no status transition should occur. Before the service change, the proof row and delivery response were updated but `event_outbox` gained zero `ChainStepAdvanced` rows (expected one). `DeliveryService::uploadProof()` now stages one content-free delivery refresh in the same transaction when the upload does not itself transition the delivery. The existing `in_transit` receipt path already emits its single refresh through `updateStatus()`; a regression assertion ensures this change does not duplicate it. The full `DeliveryUploadTest.php` passes (5 tests / 11 assertions), with PHPUnit warnings about the API container's missing `/var/www/.env`. This is backend outbox evidence only. No two-user UI run covered receipt/proof uploads, and the matrix keeps all UI cells `NOT_VERIFIED`.

A further backend-only audit traced the delivery handoff retry endpoints. Before the fix, a CoC retry successfully changed `coc_handoff_status` from `manual_required` to `generated` with no new delivery refresh (expected one); the regression failed with 2 existing signals vs 3 expected. Invoice recovery through the outbox listener likewise created and linked the draft invoice and changed `invoice_handoff_status` without a delivery refresh; its regression failed with 2 vs 3 expected. CoC retry, its manual-required failure path, invoice retry branches, and invoice manual-required changes now stage a chain refresh in their write transaction when the visible handoff fields change. Successful invoice retry/replay remains idempotent at the event layer; the replay test asserts no additional refresh after the invoice is linked. Focused CoC and invoice regressions pass (1 test / 5 assertions each); expected CoC/accounting setup error logs are part of those failure-to-manual test scenarios. No two-user UI scenario covered handoff retry, so those UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 16. Delivery proof archive/restore producers (2026-10-30)

A direct controller-level regression reproduced the same omission for proof archive and restore: both writes succeeded with zero new delivery chain outbox rows (each expected one). These controller mutations were moved to `DeliveryService::archiveProof()` and `restoreProof()`, retaining the existing locking, last-proof rules, file-existence checks and response behavior while staging the delivery refresh in the same transaction. The new `DeliveryProofRealtimeTest.php` passes 2 tests / 4 assertions after both red cases. This verifies backend producer behavior only; open-detail refresh, list views and action buttons for proof archive/restore remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 17. Loading shipment-plan edit refresh (2026-10-30)

A service-level regression for editing an already-loading delivery's schedule/carrier with `dispatch=false` failed because the record was updated without any new outbox event (zero observed, one expected). `DeliveryService::saveShipmentPlan()` now stages one refresh for that path. Scheduled plan writes retain the existing assignment event, and dispatch requests retain their lifecycle transition event. The focused test passes (1 assertion); combined proof/upload/plan tests now cover three direct backend cases, but no browser scenario covers shipment-plan editing. Its UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 18. Delivery cost-handoff retry refresh (2026-10-30)

A backend service regression retried a manual customer-COGS handoff and observed the delivery-cost handoff change without a `ChainStepAdvanced` refresh. It failed with zero events; the retry path now stages a refresh in the same transaction when its handoff record changes. Replaying the same idempotency request key emits no duplicate refresh. The focused regression passes (1 test / 2 assertions). Loss-handoff retry uses the same change comparison, while attempt-outcome reconciliation remains covered by the parent delivery event. The API/UI path was not exercised in a two-user browser scenario; all UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 19. Legacy delivery stock-reservation recovery (2026-10-30)

A route-level regression released and removed a legacy delivery's reservation batch, then called the authenticated `reserve-stock` endpoint to recover it. The API succeeded and created the reservation, but the chain outbox gained no refresh (1 existing event vs 2 expected). Reservation recovery now runs through `DeliveryService::reserveStock()`, which stages one parent-delivery refresh inside the outer transaction; repeating the same request key emits no duplicate. `DeliveryStockReservationRealtimeTest.php` passes (1 test / 5 assertions). Inventory stock event behavior and a two-user UI scenario were not tested here, so all matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 20. Automatic delivery draft updates from outgoing QC release (2026-10-30)

`CreateDeliveryDraftOnQcPass` calls `DeliveryService::draftReleasedWorkOrder()` after an outgoing inspection passes. The service can change an already-existing automatic delivery in two distinct ways: restore a missing stock-reservation batch when all eligible inspection quantity is already represented, or append a newly released inspection as another delivery line and then reconcile reservations. The prior create signal covered only first-time drafts. A red-first service test reproduced the missing refresh on reservation refill, then exercised the sibling append path; with only the append-path signal removed, the test appended its second line while the outbox count remained 2 instead of the expected 3. Both existing-draft branches now stage one delivery refresh in their transaction. The listener's existing-later-output test now asserts one signal on the first added line and no duplicate from listener replay. `SalesOrderStockFulfillmentTest::test_refilling_an_existing_qc_delivery_draft_stages_a_refresh` passes (1 test / 5 assertions), and `CreateDeliveryDraftOnQcPassTest.php` passes (12 tests / 47 assertions). The listener was invoked directly by the feature test; the Quality review HTTP route and browser were not exercised, so actor/viewer UI, list, buttons, inventory views and dashboards remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 21. Quality inspection detail/list freshness (2026-10-30)

Source tracing found that inspection saves staged no inspection detail/list refresh event, and the inspection detail page had no realtime subscription while using a 15-second interval only before a terminal status. A red-first model lifecycle regression observed zero `InspectionChanged` outbox rows after repeated inspection writes (expected one). `InspectionRealtimeObserver` stages a content-free event in the transaction for inspection create/update/delete. The first implementation coalesced row count but kept the first action/version if the same inspection was saved more than once. The shipment-lot regression then exposed the shared outbox pattern, and `OutboxService::recordCoalesced()` now updates the pending row to the latest action/version while preserving one row and one after-commit dispatch. `InspectionRealtimeTest.php` now explicitly asserts the final action/version (1 test / 5 assertions, warning only from the test container's missing `/var/www/.env`). It broadcasts only to the private per-inspection channel and private visible-inspection list channel; authorization checks the Quality feature plus the API's `quality.inspections.view` permission and record existence. The SPA inspection detail/list use the shared hooks and invalidation map; the detail polling timer was removed because the provider owns visibility-aware degraded fallback. Successful review/cancel responses replace the actor's detail query immediately before list invalidation. The earlier auth/contract and frontend runs remain documented in the Quality inspection section below; a two-context browser test did not exercise an inspection transition. The actor's review-response rendering is marked PASS only from its page-level test; other UI matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The existing real-backend outgoing-QC spec needs manifests from a disposable finish-first stack, unavailable here, so it was not pointed at the shared dev business database. No HTTP test was run for every inspection mutation. Raw query-builder writes and child-row changes that do not save the parent remain outside this test's evidence and need route audit.

### 22. Non-conformance report detail/list freshness (2026-10-30)

Source audit found that the NCR detail page had no realtime hook and instead polled every 15 seconds while non-terminal; the list page had no realtime hook. The API exposes separate writes for actions, dispositions, close/cancel, bulk close, quarantine recovery and CAPA action verification. `NonConformanceReport` and `NcrAction` had no refresh event/observer in the registered Quality event path. A red-first `NcrRealtimeTest` failed with zero outbox rows after creating/updating an NCR and creating its CAPA action (expected one parent refresh). `NcrRealtimeService`, `NcrRealtimeObserver`, and `NcrActionRealtimeObserver` now coalesce Eloquent parent/action writes for the same NCR transaction into one content-free `NcrChanged` ping. The event uses private detail and visible-list channels; their callbacks check the Quality feature and the same `quality.ncr.view` permission as list/detail API routes. The NCR detail/list, CAPA effectiveness queue, and Quality dashboard (when the viewer has `quality.ncr.view`) now use shared realtime hooks and invalidation; the 15-second detail timer is removed, and mutation responses update the actor's query immediately (child action responses patch that action before an authorized refetch). Serial backend regression/auth/contract tests pass (37 warnings / 309 assertions; known test-container environment warnings). Focused SPA invalidation/subscription/NCR page tests pass (34/34). The actor's cancel-response rendering is now marked PASS from the page-level test; other actor routes and all cross-role/list/effect UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The 8 NCR mutation matrix rows document source producer coverage, but endpoint-by-endpoint/raw bulk writer coverage remains incomplete and no NCR two-context runtime trace was captured.

## Evidence limits

Two isolated browser contexts were used for the P2P, PO, Sales Order, RFQ/PO approval, supplier-portal and driver-delivery scenarios. The inventory scenario used three isolated contexts: Finance actor, Warehouse viewer, and System Administrator viewer. We verified login, session isolation, API mutation responses, a live Reverb connection, private-channel auth, client subscription sets, the actor's post-success detail fetch, the pre-fix stale detail/summary, manual reload behavior, and fresh database counts. After fixes, the tested P2P cancellation updated another role's open detail, removed its stale action button, and refreshed its list through the committed outbox/dedicated queue path; the tested PO submit updated a Finance viewer list through its event and recovered a deliberately delayed detail-channel subscription from the authorized API without receiving the event; the Sales Order create updated a different-role list from its event without reload; the RFQ publish and two-step PO approval workflow updated other-role detail/status/actions without reload; the tested inventory approval updated both other-role stock lists without reload while sockets were Live; the supplier portal approval updated the supplier's status and edit action from its vendor-private event without reload; and the office reschedule refreshed the driver's open run-sheet detail from that driver's private user channel without reload. We did not test all mutation endpoints, every role/scope policy, transport disconnect/reconnect or worker shutdown/restart end-to-end, multi-tab coordination beyond the P2P viewer's tabs, edit conflicts, revoked access, tablet service-worker upgrade, or the full test suite. These are not represented as passing evidence.

### 23. Calibration register and PPAP freshness (source audit + red-first producer test, 2026-10-30)

The calibration register mutations create/update/record calibration rows, and PPAP mutations create/update/submit/review/approve/reject a submission, update child evidence elements, and expire approved submissions from the scheduled `quality:expire-ppaps` path. Before this slice, neither `CalibrationRecord` nor `PpapSubmission` / `PpapElement` had a registered realtime producer; calibration list/form pages had no shared hook, and this SPA has no PPAP page routes to subscribe. The backend outbox test failed before the fix with zero messages after a calibration create and a PPAP plus its child element were written in one transaction.

The source now registers one Quality observer for calibration and PPAP parents, plus a child-element observer that stages a parent PPAP refresh. `QualityRealtimeService` coalesces same-record writes in one PostgreSQL transaction. `QualityRecordChanged` is after-commit, uses the dedicated broadcast lane, and exposes only refresh/event ID/version/action on the wire. Record and visible-list channels require the Quality module and the respective API view permission (`quality.calibration.view` / `quality.ppap.view`). Calibration list/form pages subscribe through the shared layer; list record-calibration responses patch the actor's cached row from the API response before invalidation. The red-first outbox test passes (12 assertions); the focused broadcast/auth contract suite also passes apart from the existing PHPUnit environment warnings. No calibration or PPAP two-context browser scenario was run; every UI matrix cell stays `NOT_VERIFIED`. PPAP page UI is absent from this SPA, so this signal currently supports authorized API consumers and future UI, not a claim of working PPAP screen refresh.

### 24. Inspection specification revision freshness (source audit, 2026-10-30)

`InspectionSpecService::upsertForProduct()` creates/updates the spec root and creates immutable revision/item rows inside a transaction; archive/restore changes the root. The public API and SPA editor/list have no event producer or subscription. Child item creation may also be performed by `InspectionSpecItem::booted()` compatibility behavior, so observing only the explicit controller would miss model paths. The editor holds revision, current-spec, SPC and product-specific queries; inspection forms read the current spec to render acceptance criteria. These are related query families for a spec revision event. A red-first lifecycle regression failed with zero outbox rows after a spec root and compatibility-created child item were saved together. A registered parent observer and child observer now produce one coalesced `QualityRecordChanged`; a private detail/list signal uses the same Quality feature gate and read permission, including archived rows because the API detail binding allows trashed specs. The editor/list subscribe through shared hooks and the acting editor installs the API-returned spec in its query cache before invalidating dependent data. The producer/auth/contract tests pass. No UI behavior is claimed from source inspection or two-context runtime evidence.

### 25. Grouped final-inspection page did not consume existing inspection refreshes (source audit, 2026-10-30)

The work-order final-inspection controller writes Inspection roots for save-evidence, submit, independent review and confirmed-check replacement; `InspectionRealtimeObserver` already produces a private inspection refresh for those writes. The grouped SPA page query is `['quality', 'work-order-final', workOrderId]`, but that page had no `useRealtimeList`/record subscription and the inspection invalidation map omitted that key. Thus other users could keep stale batch status and review/CoC actions while the individual inspection event was delivered elsewhere. The actor uses API response data in the panel callback. A focused regression failed because `queryKeysForRealtimeList(inspection)` did not include the grouped query. The final-inspection page now subscribes to the authorized inspection-visible signal, and the central map invalidates grouped final-inspection, production work-order and Quality dashboard queries. The focused frontend tests pass (22 tests across the map and final-inspection page), and SPA typecheck/targeted ESLint pass. This proves the consumer wiring in tests, not a cross-role browser transition.

### 26. NCR template catalog freshness and archive behavior (source audit, 2026-10-30)

`NcrTemplateService` creates and updates templates, and `deactivate()` changes `is_active`. The original source audit found no model observer/event producer or catalog/picker subscriber; a red-first model-event test observed zero outbox rows for template create+update in one transaction. The registered Quality observer and shared `QualityRecordChanged` outbox path now coalesce those writes. Private template record/list channels require `modules.quality` and `quality.ncr.view`; the catalog and NCR-create active picker subscribe through the central list hook/map. A follow-up audit found the edit form still lacked its per-record subscription and would not notice a peer update. A focused React regression failed at the missing subscription/remote warning; the edit route now subscribes, preserves dirty fields, pauses save after a remote change, and reloads the current API representation only on explicit user action. The focused editor test passes (1 test), while the earlier producer, channel authorization, map, and hook tests remain the source-contract evidence; no two-context UI result is claimed and matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. Separate functional finding: the list UI exposes an archive scope and restore action, and the API restore route is bound `withTrashed`, but `NcrTemplateService::deactivate()` only sets `is_active=false` and does not soft-delete; `NcrTemplateService::list()` does not apply a trashed filter, and the controller restore only calls `restore()`. This archive contract mismatch is logged in `FINDINGS.md` and is outside this freshness fix.

### 27. Shipment-lot creation did not refresh active traceability searches (source audit, 2026-10-30)

`ShipmentLotService::createForDelivery()` and `ensureForDelivery()` create `ShipmentLot` rows. The explicit creation endpoint may not update the `Delivery` row, so existing delivery broadcasts cannot reliably signal a changed lot. The open traceability page caches `['quality','traceability',term]` but had no shipment-lot subscription or invalidation relationship. A red-first shipment-lot model lifecycle regression initially observed zero outbox rows. The registered observer now stages a content-free after-commit `ShipmentLotChanged` signal, and the private detail/list channels require the Quality feature and `quality.inspections.view` permission. The traceability page subscribes through the shared list hook and the central map invalidates term-scoped trace queries. A second regression exposed that the shared coalescing callers retained only the first action/version when one aggregate changed more than once in a transaction; `OutboxService::recordCoalesced()` now replaces the pending row's payload with the latest metadata while preserving one durable row and one after-commit dispatch callback. Shipment-lot, calibration/PPAP, inspection-spec, NCR-template and NCR regressions pass (5 tests / 26 assertions); the shipment-lot producer test passes independently (1 test / 5 assertions), and its private channel/auth plus broadcast contract tests pass (42 tests / 357 assertions across the separately run authorization and contract files). These tests emitted the known missing `/var/www/.env` container warnings. The focused traceability invalidation/subscription tests pass (25 tests), and changed-file ESLint is green. No two-context traceability UI scenario has been run; its matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### 28. Incoming inspection linkage did not refresh active GRN screens (source audit + red-first regressions, 2026-10-30)

`InspectionService` creates incoming inspections and links them back to `goods_receipt_notes.qc_inspection_id` with query-builder updates, bypassing GRN model events. Separately, the GRN pages use `['inventory','grn', ...]`, while the central `grn` and Purchase Order invalidation definitions targeted `['inventory','goods-receipts', ...]`; an authorized chain ping could therefore fail to refetch the open GRN page. The new GRN invalidation regression failed on the mismatched prefix, and the backend observer regression failed with zero `GoodsReceiptNoteChanged` rows after the relation update. The map now targets the keys used by the screens. InspectionService routes the three incoming-inspection link sites through a locked Eloquent helper that preserves the former null-or-cancelled replacement conditions; the GRN observer emits only when `qc_inspection_id` changes, avoiding duplicate pings for unrelated GRN edits. The event is after-commit, uses the dedicated broadcast queue, targets the existing private GRN detail/list channels, and carries no receipt fields. Backend producer test passes (1 test / 5 assertions), broadcast contract tests pass (12 warning-marked methods / 251 assertions), and the invalidation-map test passes (15 tests). The full broadcast authorization file passes (34 warning-marked methods / 135 assertions), including GRN detail/list permission gates. No browser scenario exercised an incoming-QC-to-GRN update, so route UI and cross-role matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### 29. Item quality-plan revision history had no refresh producer (red-first regression, 2026-10-30)

The item quality-plan API publishes an immutable revision by updating the previously active `ItemQualityPlan` and creating a new row; the deactivate endpoint updates the selected row. The revision-history page used `['inventory','quality-plans', itemId]`, had no realtime subscription, and only invalidated after mutations in the acting browser. A red-first model lifecycle test reproduced the missing event: create and update in one transaction left zero outbox rows (expected one). `ItemQualityPlanRealtimeObserver` now stages the shared `QualityRecordChanged` ping coalesced by parent item and transaction, so publishing a revision that updates the old row and creates the new row still produces one refresh. The private item channel and visible entity channel require `modules.inventory` and `inventory.view`, matching the page's item and history read APIs; payload remains a content-free refresh. The page consumes the item-scoped private record channel and applies the API mutation response to its local revision list before invalidating the item/history keys. Focused backend producer test passes (1 warning / 4 assertions); the full channel authorization file passes (34 warnings / 135 assertions); frontend map/hook tests pass (28 tests). SPA typecheck and route-specific UI behavior still need verification. There is no two-context plan revision scenario; both write-route UI matrix rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### Current-state delta 30 — Return Request event producer and consumers

A source audit found `ReturnRequest` lifecycle writes had no realtime observer, while the detail/list queries lacked subscriptions. `ReturnRequestService::markInspectionHandoffManual()` used a direct query-builder update, and `Quality\InspectionService::replaceInspection()` directly reassigned the linked inspection, both bypassing model observers. The handoff now locks and updates the Eloquent record inside a transaction; inspection replacement now updates the linked Eloquent model only when it still references the replaced inspection. Return Request writes stage `ReturnRequestChanged` through the durable coalescing outbox. It is after-commit, queued on the dedicated broadcast lane, and contains only refresh/event ID/version/action. Its per-record private channel uses API-equivalent feature/permission/type checks. List signals target explicit customer/all scopes; supplier returns are never sent to the customer-only scope. The SPA detail/list subscribe via the shared realtime hooks and central invalidation map. Successful detail mutations reconcile the API response directly into the acting user's query cache.

Evidence run serially: `ReturnRequestRealtimeTest.php` and `ReturnInspectionHandoffTest.php` pass together (12 PHPUnit warning-marked tests, 34 assertions); `BroadcastAuthorizationTest.php` passes the Return Request scope cases (1 selected method, 11 assertions); `BroadcastEventContractTest.php` passes 13 methods / 265 assertions; focused SPA invalidation/list/detail tests pass 3 files / 32 tests. ### Current-state delta 38 — Long-lived Horizon process held stale event registry

The first live payroll approval attempt updated the period in PostgreSQL but HR and Finance viewer pages did not receive their refresh. The corresponding `PayrollPeriodChanged` rows in `event_outbox` had failed three attempts with `RuntimeException: Event App\\Modules\\Payroll\\Events\\PayrollPeriodChanged is not registered for durable publication.` The API container had the new event codec, but the long-running Horizon process still held code loaded before that event was added. Gracefully restarting Horizon loaded the current codec and resumed the dedicated broadcasts supervisor. A single rerun then passed the two-viewer status/action refresh described in delta 37. This confirms that mounted PHP code changes do not reload long-lived workers automatically; local hot reload of the SPA/API does not imply queue-worker hot reload. Restart workers after event/codec/config changes and monitor failed outbox rows. This is one confirmed local run, not proof of every production worker/deploy path.

### Current-state delta 56 — Delivery-health fallback verified against a paused worker

Added no-store `GET /api/v1/realtime/health`, returning only a healthy/degraded bit based on Redis queue depth plus failed or overdue pending `event_outbox` rows (two-second enqueue grace); Redis or database inspection failure reports degraded. It accepts internal SPA, supplier-portal, and customer-portal session guards. The provider uses credentialed same-origin fetch outside the internal Axios interceptor, so portal auth errors cannot redirect to ERP sign-in. At introduction, it checked every two seconds while visible and performed an immediate global catch-up on first degradation. Delta 482 changed the visible health cadence to ten seconds and removed the initial global catch-up because each private page channel already performs its own authorized catch-up on acknowledgement; real reconnects and later delivery-state transitions still trigger global catch-up. The provider keeps the two-second timeout and visibility-aware exponential fallback while degraded. This follow-up's focused provider tests pass 7/7; see the delta 482 evidence below.

Delta 677 restores a two-second visible-tab health cadence after a red-first provider test showed that a ten-second interval left a connected socket marked Live two seconds after the health endpoint had changed to degraded. The endpoint still returns aggregate delivery state only; channel acknowledgement remains the initial page-scoped catch-up and delivery transitions trigger the shared catch-up. Provider tests pass 9/9. No live worker outage or API/Redis request-rate benchmark was run; current probe rate is 30 per visible tab per minute.

The health endpoint initially reported degraded because its new audit found two historical failed `PayrollPeriodChanged` outbox rows. Horizon had loaded an older event-codec allowlist and exhausted retries with `Event ... PayrollPeriodChanged is not registered for durable publication`. After restarting Horizon, `php artisan outbox:dispatch --retry-failed --limit=100` requeued only those two refresh-only events; both published, leaving 2,001 published rows, zero failed rows, and empty `high` and `broadcasts` queues. No business records were modified by that recovery.

After the backend, Horizon, mail worker, and Reverb were restarted in place using `make dev-refresh-php`, the healthy-worker headless two-context E2E passed (1 scenario / 21.2 s). The Production Manager list/detail/actions/dashboard each received one event and one authorized API refetch per status transition; actor-response-to-viewer-render samples were 610–757 ms. With only `supervisor-broadcasts` paused, the same scenario passed after the outbox-health check and Horizon restart (1 / 23.2 s): delivery health was degraded, no machine event arrived, and list/detail/actions/dashboard refetched through authorized APIs after each viewer page was brought forward and its focus handler invoked. Samples were 729–2,398 ms after the actor response. Those degraded samples include explicit focus triggers and do not establish hidden-tab periodic-polling latency. Both runs archived their disposable machine and resumed/verified the worker. The machine route row records degraded focus catch-up; matrix PASS counts are unchanged. No badge, inbox, linked-record, cache-hit, or API-p95 claim is added.

### Current-state delta 58 — Laravel's persistent queue-restart marker now expires

A live cache audit found one no-TTL `illuminate:queue:restart` key among 72 entries. Source inspection confirmed Laravel's stock queue restart command writes this framework worker-control timestamp with `forever`; it is not cached record content, and deleting it indiscriminately could trigger an unnecessary worker restart. A red-first test was added for the command-finished listener (2 tests / 4 assertions); `QueueRestartSignalTtl` now rewrites the unchanged marker with a 30-day TTL after successful `queue:restart` and ignores unrelated or failed commands. After guarded `make dev-refresh-php`, the normal queue restart command ran; the marker remained and the audit reported 65 entries, zero without TTL, zero over 1 MiB and zero unknown size. The scoped prune dry-run found zero candidates and deleted zero. The broadcasts supervisor is running and `ogamiph.dev` returned HTTP 200. No Redis flush or database change was made.

A pre-subscribed Production Manager list received one private `entity.machine.visible` event, made one authorized list GET, and rendered the new row without reload 300 ms after the actor response. The same 24-second scenario transitioned idle→offline→idle; viewer list/detail/options/dashboard each received one event/refetch and rendered in 596–916 ms. Nine delivery-health checks were healthy, and test cleanup archived the disposable machine. E2E TypeScript and ESLint passed. This directly verifies actor-screen independence from WebSocket plus one other-role list path, not the full machine module. The matrix adds actor PASS for machine creation; current counts are actor 18, other detail 10, list 11, actions 9, linked 1, badges 0, dashboard 1, inbox 0. The test supplies valid optional specification values because the blank form can send null into non-null database columns; that separate functional failure is recorded in `FINDINGS.md`.

### Current-state delta 61 — Approval board cross-role trace

Source inspection found `ApprovalsBoardPage` lacked a realtime hook and instead used a 30-second fixed refetch interval. In the two-context baseline, a VP viewed a pending high-value purchase request while Finance approved its first step. The VP socket received the content-free `BadgesChanged` frame, but the board made no further request during a three-second observation and retained the stale Awaiting others column. The VP's authorized direct API request returned the fresh step-2 card in `my_action`. This reproduces the client invalidation failure with the WebSocket and backend read path both working.

The event now also broadcasts on private `approvals.visible`, gated by the same `approvals.board.view` permission as the board endpoint. The approvals page uses the shared event hook, invalidates its active query prefix, and relies on the provider's degraded fallback instead of its fixed 30-second timer. The actor's Approve button disappeared from the successful response with its socket disabled. This is one tested PR handoff, not evidence for every workflow in the aggregated board. Traces: `/tmp/ogami-approval-board-repro-20261030-d` and `/tmp/ogami-approval-board-final-20261030`.

## Customer invoice cross-role reproduction and current verification (2026-10-30)

The invoice portal originally had no customer-account invoice channel, and the detail/list did not subscribe to a common invoice refresh path. Red-first backend regressions observed no invoice refresh on create, update, prebill approval, cancel, customer payment submission, or finance rejection; another regression found credit application could change an invoice balance while leaving its status `partial`, so status-change-only dispatch missed it. Those tests now assert a refresh signal for the affected write paths. The customer invoice detail's 30-second timer and the portal invoice-signals 60-second timer were removed in favor of the shared event provider/degraded fallback.

For cross-role verification, one headless Playwright scenario used a customer portal context plus separate Finance maker/approver/finalizer contexts. It created a unique $0.02 prebill, approved and finalized it, kept customer invoice detail and list open, then finalized and applied a matching customer credit note. The customer portal broadcasting-auth request returned 200. Four invoice mutations each produced one `chain.refresh` frame on `private-customer.invoice.{hash}`; the payload contained only `event_id` and `refresh`. On credit application, authorized customer API reads refreshed the open detail and list to Paid / $0.00, removed the payment action, and did so without reload. The most recent request-start-to-visible-updates sample was 2,280 ms, including the mutation request and server processing; it is one sample, not commit-to-UI p95. The actor's finance screen was driven through its authenticated API client rather than an interactive UI. Exact multi-user evidence is the single customer-plus-finance browser scenario in `spa/e2e-real/customer-invoice-cross-role-realtime.spec.ts`; no claim is made for other invoice transitions or roles.

The run created a fully offset prebill/credit pair, leaving zero open test receivable and net-zero credit application in the local dev database. Earlier fixture exploration found two seeded open customer invoices whose `journal_entry_id` did not join to a posted journal entry; attempting to create a credit note against those fixtures returned API 422 (`The target invoice does not have a posted journal entry.`). That read-only fixture observation is tracked separately in `FINDINGS.md`; those seeded records were not modified.


### Current-state delta 76 — Portal notification event path

B2B portal notifications now emit `PortalNotificationChanged` on a private per-portal-user channel for inserted notices and read/delete mutations, after commit. The signal contains action plus an optional notification ID/type on create; notification content is fetched only from the customer or supplier session's own no-store API. The Echo authorizer routes each channel through the matching portal broadcast-auth endpoint. Channel callbacks require the exact session user's opaque hash and an active, unlocked, password-ready portal user plus active parent account. The portal bell and inbox share the scoped QueryClient key and no longer declare their own polling interval; the RealtimeProvider's degraded catch-up remains the fallback. Focused API and SPA tests cover producer/dedupe, rollback, channel ownership, read/delete signals, scoped invalidation, and the no-interval bell. No real-browser portal socket scenario was run in this slice.

### Current-state delta 77 — GRN incoming-QC handoff refresh path

Four incoming-QC handoff writes that previously bypassed Eloquent now save through the GRN model, and its observer watches the exposed handoff fields. The focused producer regression and GRN feature tests are recorded under root cause 39. No two-context browser check was run.

### Current-state delta 78 — Internal notification read/delete refresh path

Read, read-all, delete, and clear-read now emit a private after-commit signal when they change rows; the internal bell invalidates its scoped inbox. The focused producer, broadcast contract, and SPA test results are recorded under root cause 40. No two-browser notification UI check was run.

### Current-state delta 79 — Customer order and delivery portal refresh path

Before this change, customer order and delivery detail queries owned four 30-second `refetchInterval` timers and the portal layout had no account-scoped listener for the existing `ChainStepAdvanced` events. Added the private `customer.order.{customerHashId}` channel, authorized only for an active customer portal session matching that active customer account while B2B portals are enabled. Sales-order and delivery signals now include opaque entity type/ID in the minimal payload, and the customer portal hook invalidates order, delivery, receipt-group, list, and linked chain query families through the central map. The four page timers were removed; the shared provider retains catch-up and visibility-aware degraded fallback. Sequential verification passed `ChainBroadcasterTest` (7 warning-marked tests / 27 assertions), the focused customer channel authorization case (1 warning-marked test / 4 assertions), the broadcast contract suite (28 warning-marked tests / 454 assertions), and the SPA hook/map tests (36/36). The first SPA run found only a test assertion expecting full keys where the map intentionally returns prefixes; after correcting the assertion, both files passed. No browser two-context delivery or UI-latency evidence was collected; the matrix is unchanged.

### Current-state delta 80 — Internal O2C detail pages use the shared chain freshness path

The internal Sales Order detail owned 10-second intervals on both its record and chain queries; Accounting Invoice detail owned a 30-second interval while active. Both pages already mount `useChainProgress`, which listens for the private per-record `chain.refresh` event, invalidates those active query keys, and uses visibility-aware exponential backoff only when the shared realtime state is degraded. New page tests failed before the change because the active record queries still carried interval callbacks. Removed those three redundant page timers. The shared realtime hook and its existing fallback are unchanged. Three targeted SPA files passed 17/17 after removal. No browser latency sample or API-load benchmark was collected; 31 executable polling declarations across 29 files remain and are listed in the CSV.

### Current-state delta 81 — Production output refresh reaches authorized work-order lists

The Production work-order list displayed `quantity_produced` and used `useRealtimeList('work_order', 'visible')`, but `WorkOrderOutputRecorded` targeted only the individual work-order and Production dashboard channels. Output changes therefore missed the open list until its 60-second interval. Red-first API and SPA tests confirmed the missing entity-list target and missing `.output.recorded` list listener. Added `entity.work_order.visible` to the content-free output signal, subscribed centrally to `.output.recorded` for work-order list viewers, and removed the list timer. The generic connection/worker health path catches up active queries when realtime is degraded. Focused API event test passed 1 warning-marked test / 2 assertions; the focused work-order list channel authorization case passed 1 warning-marked test / 2 assertions; focused `useRealtimeList` tests passed 28/28. No production two-role browser or latency sample was run; current polling inventory is 30 declarations across 28 files.

The notification inbox page also declared a 30-second timer for portal routes. The customer and supplier portal layouts already mount the scoped NotificationBell subscriber; notification create/read/delete signals invalidate the same portal-user query prefix used by the inbox, and the shared provider owns degraded catch-up. Removed the inbox page's duplicate interval. The regression assertion exists but was not run after the instruction to stop testing; no portal browser trace or latency sample is claimed. At delta 82 the source polling inventory was 29 declarations across 27 files.


### Current-state delta 92 — Action Center task refresh path

`PATCH /api/v1/dashboards/action-center/tasks` changes shared triage state in `ActionCenterTaskService::apply()`. The service now writes one content-free `BadgesChanged` outbox row inside the task transaction per bulk request; `OutboxService` dispatches it after commit to the existing private badge/approval channels. `useRealtimeSignal` maps it centrally to active badge, approval-board, Action Center and dashboard query families, which refetch their own authorized API data. This source path is covered in the mutation matrix, but all route UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED` because no cross-role browser scenario was run. The Action Center's 60-second refresh is retained for computed SLA/snooze time boundaries; the Alert source's event coverage remains incomplete.


### Current-state delta 93 — Auth refresh ordering and logout cache races

Source tracing connected the permission-refresh callers in `usePermissionSync` (private permission/delegation signals, channel acknowledgement/error, effective-date timer, and focus/visibility return) to concurrent calls of `/auth/user`. Before this change, each `authStore.refresh()` applied its response immediately, so an older request could overwrite a newer role, employee/department, permission, delegated-approval, active-state, or feature snapshot. Bootstrap/login responses had no generation guard either. There is no delayed-response browser trace; this is an evidenced source race, not a runtime reproduction.

The auth store now guards bootstrap, login and refresh results with a session generation; refresh calls share one in-flight request and coalesce newer requests into a follow-up authorized `/auth/user` fetch. Sign-in/sign-out requests are serialized, and a queued sign-in is skipped if a newer session action superseded it. `applyUser()` clears the in-memory query cache when access scope changes. The same source review found logout awaited its API call before clearing the prior identity and query cache, while delayed cleanup could overlap a later login. Logout now clears user/query/local preference state synchronously and only awaits cleanup of app-shell Cache Storage. No test, typecheck, browser run, or timing measurement was performed under the current stop-testing instruction. Socket delivery and auth request ordering remain unverified at runtime.


### Current-state delta 94 — Overlapping realtime catch-up triggers retain one follow-up

`RealtimeProvider.catchUp()` previously shared an active invalidation promise and silently dropped any second trigger until that promise settled. A focus/reconnect/health transition during an in-flight API read could therefore lose its follow-up after a read that began before the state change. The provider now coalesces overlapping triggers into one trailing invalidation/refetch pass and releases its single-flight handle only after the queue drains. This is source-level correction only; no tests, typecheck, browser timing, or API request-count measurement ran under the stop-testing instruction.


### Current-state delta 95 — Session changes clear cached data across browser tabs

The API session cookie is shared by same-origin tabs, but each tab owns a separate QueryClient. Source review found logout cleared only its own tab, so a sibling could retain its previous user's cached API rows after server logout. Successful sign-in had the same cross-tab identity-change gap. The shared `authSessionSync` utility now sends allowlisted realm/stage/nonce signals over `BroadcastChannel`, with a transient `storage` fallback. The real login page uses the serialized session-mutation queue and sends the resolved sign-in realm; internal, customer and supplier logout paths synchronously clear local query/draft/PWA cache and notify peer tabs. Sign-in signals target their resolved realm, while logout signals target `all` because Laravel invalidates the shared session; every affected tab clears local identity and re-reads through its own guard. No identity, permission, or record fields cross tabs. This is source wiring only; no multi-tab browser trace or auth-session ordering test ran under the stop-testing instruction.


### Current-state delta 96 — Cross-tab session signals cover all three guards

The initial tab-sync change was internal-store-only. Source inspection showed the actual login form uses `authApi.signIn()`, while customer and supplier layouts call their own logout APIs. The shared `authSessionSync` transport now serves the real sign-in page and all internal/customer/supplier logout paths. Signals are allowlisted by realm and lifecycle stage, carry only a nonce, deduplicate recent messages, and use BroadcastChannel with a transient storage-event fallback. Cookie-backed sign-in/sign-out API mutations are serialized within the tab. Peer layouts clear their own query/draft/PWA cache and re-fetch identity through their own guard; scoped sign-in changes ignore unrelated guards, while logout invalidates every guard because the Laravel session container is flushed. No tests, typecheck or multi-tab browser run was performed.


### Current-state delta 98 — Whole-session invalidations notify every auth guard

Source review found that the internal and portal logout endpoints call Laravel `session()->invalidate()`, and portal password-change does the same after revoking its guard. The earlier realm-only logout signal could leave other guard tabs' local identity and QueryClient data intact. Internal/customer/supplier logout now signal `all`; portal password-change sends `all` before and after the serialized mutation, clears local query/draft/PWA state, and lets every peer guard revalidate its own API identity. No cross-guard browser reproduction or tests ran.


### Current-state delta 97 — Hashed service-worker assets use cache-first and replace v3 cache

Source review found that a Cache Storage hit for an immutable Vite `/assets/` URL still launched a network request on every access. The worker now returns the cached content-hashed asset directly and writes only cache misses; Cache Storage lookup/write errors fall back to the network. Install and activate work is included in `waitUntil`, and `clients.claim()` is awaited after old owned-cache cleanup. The worker cache namespace moved from `ogami-pwa-v3` to `ogami-pwa-v4`, so activation removes prior v1/v2/v3 entries. The focused VM-level service-worker policy suite passes 8/8 after the updated cache-first asset behavior and API/Sanctum bypass checks. No installed-PWA browser scenario was run.


### Current-state delta 156 — Accounting-period lifecycle now enters the shared refresh path

Source review of `AccountingPeriodService::close()`, `reopen()` and the hourly `accounting:relock-periods` schedule found that each saves `AccountingPeriod` rows, but no observer/event or SPA subscription covered them. Close/reopen success only invalidated the actor's `['accounting', 'periods']` query; row buttons were inferred from local `status`. The finance summary also caches `current_accounting_period` under a shared dashboard generation that those writes did not rotate. This explains a source-level stale path but was not reproduced with two browser contexts in this turn.

Added an Eloquent observer for create/lifecycle update/delete, a transaction-coalesced `AccountingPeriodChanged` outbox event, and a private `entity.accounting_period.visible` channel authorized by the same Accounting feature and `accounting.periods.view` grant as the list API. Close/reopen/scheduled relock now rotate `dashboard:cache:version` after commit. The SPA page and authorized Finance dashboard subscribe through `useRealtimeList`; map invalidation covers period lists and the finance summary. Successful mutation resources seed the actor's current list cache, and row buttons consume API `available_actions`; a stale second reopen returns HTTP 409 and triggers a refresh. Added regression test source but did not run tests, builds, typecheck, or browser sessions per the current instruction. Matrix results remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; delivery and latency are not runtime-proven.


### HR training source-audit delta (2026-10-31)

`Training` catalog CRUD/archive/restore and `EmployeeTraining` assignment/completion/cancellation/expiry-alert writes previously had no shared broadcast producer or page subscription. All HR training UI outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### Inventory item freshness source finding (2026-10-31)

Item create/update/archive/restore were already covered by the generic after-commit badge observer for the inventory dashboard cache generation and sidebar badges. The missing path was record/list freshness: no Item model observer emitted an item refresh, item detail had no record subscription, and the app had no global item-list subscription. `ItemResource` includes `safety_stock`, ABC class, and stock-derived availability/status, so `InventoryStockChanged` invalidated stock-level and dashboard queries but not the item queries that render those fields. These are source-confirmed coverage gaps; no new two-context failure was reproduced for Inventory in this slice.

Added `ItemChanged` through the durable outbox, private `inventory.item.{hashId}` and `entity.item.visible` channels, a root SPA bridge scoped to item index/catalog permissions, and item query families in the central invalidation map. Stock movement signals now also target `entity.item.visible`, so every active authorized item list/detail/selector query refetches derived stock. The safety-stock and ABC bulk writers send one catalog ping instead of per-row events; category/UOM/conversion model writes use the same entity-level refresh. The actor keeps the API response as its result. No tests or browser sessions were run; all affected matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


### Scheduled Inventory and RFQ source traces (2026-10-31)

inventory:reconcile-replenishment rechecks blocked item alerts and can create auto-generated purchase requests; under critical-shortage conditions, the service can instead create a pending auto-PO. The normal auto-PR writes stage chain/low-stock events and query-builder alert resolution rotates the badge generation and stages a content-free BadgesChanged event when rows change, but the critical auto-PO writer has no PO-specific chain/outbox refresh (delta 371). purchasing:close-due-rfqs calls the locked RequestForQuoteService::closeDue() path; its closed/cancelled transitions stage RequestForQuoteChanged and the lifecycle outbox entry, with no-quote closure handing the linked PR back. These are source classifications, not scheduler executions or UI evidence.


### Stock reservation event/cache source finding (2026-10-31)

`StockMovementService::reserve()` and `release()` save `StockLevel` rows but create no ledger movement and therefore did not enter the `StockMovementCompleted` outbox path. Those writes change `ItemResource` availability and the `material.reservations` dashboard summary; the latter and `inventory.low_stock` use independent 30-second widget generations. Added a primitive-only `StockReservationChanged` outbox event, coalesced by PostgreSQL transaction ID plus item/location, and routed it through the shared inventory stock publisher. That publisher rotates stock-summary, Warehouse, low-stock and reservation-widget generations before emitting the private, content-free `InventoryStockChanged` signal. Source tracing confirms the intended path; no outbox dispatch, cache result, browser session or latency was verified.


### Dashboard cache source-map update (2026-10-31)

A follow-up source trace compared each scalar/rich dashboard aggregate with `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver`. The widget keys are user/scope-partitioned and have 30-second TTLs, but multiple source models were absent from the scoped generation map, so an event-driven API refetch could still hit an old value. ### Browser cache and persisted draft audit follow-up (2026-10-31)

The SPA has no persisted TanStack Query cache. Source review did find two additional stale-display/retention paths: `useMetric` used previous-query placeholder data across metric/date/machine keys, and browser form drafts stored timestamps but had no age check. Removed the metric cross-key placeholder and added a seven-day expiry for ordinary `ogami:formdraft:*` entries. Custom delivery/return/production retry payloads used separate storage prefixes; auth cleanup now removes those owned prefixes, and the customer delivery report key includes the portal user ID. A generation guard prevents an old form's unmount autosave from recreating a draft after auth cleanup. Retry payloads remain until a lifecycle boundary because they may protect recovery of an ambiguous write. These changes are source-reviewed only; no browser storage isolation, expiry, or retry scenario was run.


### Admin dashboard cache-coherence follow-up (2026-10-31)

The Admin dashboard API response uses a 30-second `FailOpenCache` entry partitioned by viewer and current PanelGate scope. A source trace found that User/Role directory events can invalidate the SPA dashboard query, but those model classes and the login-history/audit/alert sources did not rotate the server-side cache generation; an authorized API refetch could therefore return the old cached dashboard. The dashboard now uses its own generation scope, and the identified Eloquent sources rotate it after commit and stage a coalesced, content-free event to a private channel authorized by `dashboard.admin.view`. Alert condition resolution has a query-builder update path, so it now explicitly rotates that scope and stages the same signal when it changes rows. ### Supplier-performance writer-path update (2026-10-31)

`SupplierPerformanceService::compute()` uses model `upsert()`, which bypasses Eloquent observers. Added one outbox-backed `CustomerComplaintChanged` broadcaster, registered after-commit observers for both parent/8D models, explicit staging for the query-builder handoff path, and linked-NCR fan-out. It targets private CRM detail/list channels authorized by `crm.complaints.view` and a private customer channel authorized under `customer_portal` for the exact active customer account. Its broadcast payload is only refresh metadata; all record data still comes from the user's authorized CRM or tenant-scoped B2B API. Internal actor mutations seed the detail query from the API response; internal and portal list/detail/dashboard query families use shared invalidation hooks, and an already-open portal 8D report modal refetches its tenant-scoped API. Cross-filter placeholders are removed and static complaint options have a separate cache key. At that inventory checkpoint, the source inventory contained 127 event classes / 72 broadcasters, 72 payload rows and 302 async entrypoints. ### Administrative generation rotation (source-wired, runtime unverified)

Admin Settings exposes three registered cache groups: dashboard/widget/badge generations, financial-statement snapshots, and the five procurement-chain summaries. The dashboard group rotates both `DashboardCacheGeneration` and the separate `badges.version` marker; direct-key dashboard caches are excluded. `GET /api/v1/admin/cache-groups` and `POST /api/v1/admin/cache-groups/{group}/clear` require `admin.settings.manage`; the mutation is throttled and writes an append-only audit row plus a durable refresh outbox row in one transaction. The publication callback follows generation rotation. Unknown groups are rejected. It does not accept cache keys or call a global flush. Existing versioned entries age out by their normal TTL. A content-free `CacheGroupInvalidated` signal targets the matching private channel; the actor also invalidates its local QueryClient from the response. Rotation write failures are returned when observable and logged, and old server snapshots remain bounded by their TTL. Source review found and corrected the missing server-side badge generation rotation; focused producer and channel-authorization regressions pass, but live endpoint, Redis, socket delivery, and UI behavior remain unverified under the current stop-testing instruction; see the cache registry and PROGRESS delta 303.

## Discovery delta — Admin Backups realtime path (2026-10-31)

The Admin Backups view at `/admin/backups` reads the authenticated `GET /api/v1/admin/backups` endpoint, which requires `admin.backups.view`. Before this delta, `spa/src/pages/admin/backups.tsx` owned a 5-second active-operation / 30-second idle refetch interval and had no Echo listener. `BackupOperation` lifecycle changes are written by `BackupService`, `CreateBackupJob`, `RestoreBackupJob`, and stale-operation reconciliation; the list exposes those states to the same permission. Scheduled `db:full-backup` and legacy verification can also add manifest-backed list entries without a `BackupOperation` status write.

The source-wired path now uses `AdminBackupsChanged` on the private `admin.backups` channel. Authorization requires an active user with `admin.backups.view`. Eloquent ledger changes are observed and coalesced into the durable outbox after commit; scheduled manifest creation and legacy adoption queue the same event. The payload contains only a refresh flag, event ID, hashed entity reference, action, timestamp version, opaque actor reference, and an empty related-entity list. The SPA central map invalidates `['admin','backups']` and refetches through the existing API. The page-owned timer is removed.

The separate `/recovery` break-glass UI uses a short-lived bearer token that can operate without the ERP user/database channel, so it cannot reuse this user-authorized channel. Its 2-second active-operation monitor remains an explicit security/availability exception pending a dedicated bearer-scoped broadcast-auth design. No test, browser session, queue delivery, API response, or latency trace was run for this delta; runtime channel/auth delivery and two-user freshness remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Discovery delta — Automation Recovery listener-run realtime path (2026-10-31)

The SPA central invalidation map refetches the current list and the `chain.recovery_backlog` dashboard widget through their authorized APIs. Before staging the signal, `ChainListenerRunRealtimeService` also retires that widget's 30-second backend cache generation; this is needed because query invalidation alone could refetch the prior server snapshot. The page-owned 30-second interval was removed; provider reconnect/focus catch-up and degraded fallback remain. No worker, API request, channel authorization, queue delivery, browser context, or latency trace was exercised; runtime delivery remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Discovery delta — Admin Sessions list freshness (2026-10-31)

`GET /api/v1/admin/sessions` and `DELETE /api/v1/admin/sessions/{session}` require `admin.settings.manage`. The read returns database session ID, user ID/name/email, IP, user-agent, last-activity time, and a current-session flag. `SessionController::destroy()` locks and deletes one row in a transaction and writes an audit log. The SPA query is `['admin','sessions']` with a 30-second page interval.

Source inspection confirms a stale row/button window after termination for another Admin viewer; the mutation endpoint rechecks row existence and returns 404. Successful explicit termination now stages `AdminSessionsChanged` in the same transaction as the delete and audit write. The private channel requires the same active-user `admin.settings.manage` permission, and the payload omits the session ID and other session fields. The SPA central map invalidates the list query; the actor updates from the API response. Normal requests also touch session `last_activity`, so the 30-second visible-tab refresh remains for that high-frequency framework activity. No two-admin reproduction or transport trace was run under the stop-testing instruction; delivery and UI remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

Delta 709's focused API run reached the route and reproduced a PostgreSQL failure before deletion: `FOR UPDATE` on the session query also attempted to lock the nullable side of the user/role `LEFT JOIN`. The lock now targets only `sessions`. Successful deletion also stages the existing exact-user `UserAccessChanged` signal in the same transaction, so the revoked user's active SPA refetches `/auth/user` and clears auth/cache on 401. The route regression passes (1 test / 6 assertions), covering deletion and both content-free durable outbox records. No two-user browser or WebSocket receipt was run; session-list and target-tab UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

A wider source scan found other internal session deletion callers: self-service password changes, password reset-link completion, employee archive/clearance account deactivation, and credential reset. These now pass through `SessionRevocationService`, which stages the same exact-user access ping only when it actually deletes at least one database session; `UserAdminService::resetPassword()` delegates to `UserProvisioningService`, and explicit admin deactivation retains its existing batched exact-user/delegate refresh. Focused service cases pass for self-service “keep current, revoke others” and provisioning deactivation/reset (3 tests / 14 assertions). No WebSocket/browser receipt was tested.

## Discovery delta — Customer lifecycle and dependent-view freshness (2026-11-01)

Accounting and CRM expose Customer create/update/delete/restore routes through `CustomerController`; `CustomerService` uses Eloquent and the restore endpoint directly calls `restore()`. Before this update there was no Customer realtime observer/event or directory/record hook. New-customer rows created by the atomic CSV importer coalesce to one list-channel outbox signal per PostgreSQL transaction; updates/deletes/restores stay record-scoped for open details and portal accounts. Regression sources were added but not run; channel authorization, outbox delivery, role-specific UI behavior, cache ordering and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


Static follow-up found that `ForecastingService::recomputeBatch()` and the manual `DemandForecastController::recompute()` endpoint commit each forecast row through a separate `compute()` transaction, which would have produced one dashboard ping per changed row. The individual cache-generation rotations remain after each row commit. `DemandForecastChanged` now refreshes the Forecasting list and derived views as well as the Purchasing dashboard. The scheduled command and event count were not run or measured; runtime channel delivery and Forecasting freshness remain unverified.

**Discovery delta 319 — inbound Shipment aggregate:** The source gap above is now wired through `ShipmentChanged`. `ShipmentRealtimeObserver` covers Shipment create/update/status/delete/restore (restore is observed through Eloquent's save/update lifecycle); `ShipmentComponentRealtimeObserver` covers Container, ShipmentDocument, and ShipmentLandedCost create/update/delete/restore writes. Landed-cost recalculation uses a query-builder delete for old allocations, but also saves the parent Shipment in the same transaction, so the parent observer stages the aggregate refresh. The outbox deduplicates by PostgreSQL transaction ID and shipment ID; clients receive only event ID, action, version/timestamp, an opaque actor hash, and the opaque shipment ID on authorized private channels. `shipment.{hashId}` and `entity.shipment.visible` use the same `modules.supply_chain` plus `supply_chain.view`/`supply_chain.shipments.view` grants as the read routes. Container responses seed the nested actor query and trigger central invalidation; legacy 204/message-only archive/restore responses refetch through the authorized API. Shipment status and metadata compare the API resource version, while Container updates compare child and parent versions under row locks; stale versions return 409. Existing `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` rotates the logistics shipment-count and Plant Manager source generations, and `BadgeInvalidationObserver` observes Shipment lifecycle writes; no cache family was added or removed. This is source wiring only; runtime channel checks, queue delivery, query freshness, p95, and UI conflict behavior remain NOT_VERIFIED.

Delivery assignment, status, archive and restore also change Vehicle-derived active-delivery eligibility without always writing the Vehicle row; `DeliveryVehicleRealtimeObserver` stages the Vehicle refresh for current and previous assigned vehicles. VehicleRealtimeObserver now records a per-vehicle coalesced VehicleChanged outbox event after commit; exact-record and entity-list channels use the same feature/active-user/supply_chain.view conditions as fleet APIs. The payload carries only opaque vehicle/actor hashes, event/action/version/timestamp metadata, and a refresh flag. The central invalidation map reaches fleet and available-assignment queries, active Delivery reads, badges and logistics queries. The fleet, delivery-create and shipment-plan screens subscribe through useRealtimeList; actor-side status/confirmation/truck-return handlers invalidate their vehicle query family. VehicleResource now carries permissions/actions/version and optional expected_version protects update/archive/restore against stale writes with 409 responses. BadgeInvalidationObserver now observes Vehicle so the existing maintenance badge generation is retired after commit. No Laravel Vehicle response-cache family exists; the SPA vehicle-options lookup was separated from dynamic fleet query keys. Static source review ruled out a Vehicle write from receiveLate. Runtime channel/delivery, UI results, cache metrics and conflict behavior remain NOT_VERIFIED. A focused Fleet editor test then reproduced a remaining modal gap: Fleet list invalidation refreshed the table, but the open edit modal had no exact-record subscription and only learned of concurrent state after a save attempt returned 409. The modal now subscribes while editing and warns/disables save immediately on the private vehicle signal; draft values remain present. Three missing or conditional Payroll event paths were identified at this 2026-11-01 source checkpoint; subsequent changes now wire each path. (1) `PayrollPeriodService::claimForCompute()` still uses a guarded query-builder update, so it explicitly rotates payroll/dashboard/badge generations and stages a coalesced `PayrollPeriodChanged(status_changed)` in the claim transaction; this covers scheduled auto-period claims before the durable compute request. These are source and focused-test results; no peer browser or live delivery scenario ran, so route UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

The PayrollPeriod API resource exposes current status and lifecycle metadata, while the detail page derives action controls from that client-held state and the current permission store; no per-user `available_actions` contract was found. Service methods revalidate state under locks, but controllers surface several stale-state `BusinessRuleException`s as 422 rather than a distinguishable 409. This remains a source-level contract gap; no stale-action race was exercised.

Source disposition (2026-11-01): individual Payroll period detail/mutation resources now include user-scoped `permissions` and server-computed `available_actions`; list rows avoid per-record action eligibility queries. The detail renders lifecycle controls from the server snapshot, and stale status transitions return a typed 409 that invalidates the central period query families. Existing service row locks and validation remain authoritative. This corrects the source-level gap, not its unverified browser behavior.

Payroll cache search found two backend cache families in the module: government bracket arrays keyed by agency/effective date plus a 7-day generation marker (300-second payload TTL), and `payroll:progress:{period}` snapshots (3600-second TTL, cleared on new claim/force-unlock). Payroll lists/details and disbursement proofs use in-memory QueryClient data (shared 30-second stale / 5-minute inactive-GC defaults), with authenticated API `private, no-store` behavior; no Payroll response cache was found. No cache hit rate, invalidation latency, API p95, or memory impact was measured. UI behavior cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; this pass used static source inspection only, with no tests or runtime scenario.

### 20. Return Management cases, RMAs, and customer portal linkage (2026-11-01)

Source-traced the remaining 21 ReturnManagement matrix transitions. `AppServiceProvider` registers realtime observers for ReturnRequest, ReturnCase, ReturnCaseEvent, ReturnCaseLine, ReturnCaseAttachment, and ReturnCaseReceiptAllocation. Case child writes resolve their parent and stage `ReturnCaseChanged`; the case service coalesces writes by parent and PostgreSQL transaction ID. Return-request state writes go through the locking services and observed parent Eloquent saves; ReturnRequest child edits are accompanied by a parent save for the inventoried split/disposition/receipt paths. The signal events implement `ShouldBroadcast` and `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`, use the dedicated broadcast queue contract, and carry refresh metadata rather than record fields.

Internal detail/list consumers use `useRealtimeRecord` and `useRealtimeList`. Return-case customer and supplier portal pages subscribe through the separate customer-account/vendor channels; authorization binds the active portal principal to its exact customer/vendor. Internal entity channels require `return_management.view` and distinguish customer-only users from broad internal readers. The API remains responsible for filtering each refetch. The acting case page seeds the action response; attachment upload invalidates its detail query. Internal RMA action handlers reconcile the returned resource immediately. ReturnCaseResource exposes selected capability flags, and ReturnRequestResource exposes approval flags, but the SPA still derives many controls from status plus permission state rather than a complete per-user `available_actions` field; services re-lock and validate before acting.

Two directional freshness gaps remain. The customer portal RMA list/detail uses `['portal','customer','returns']` and `['portal','customer','return', id]`, but only the actor's create path invalidates its local list; ReturnRequestChanged targets internal User channels and no portal hook is mounted. This is the existing source-confirmed cross-guard finding #185; it was not dynamically reproduced. Also, the ReturnCase resource embeds its linked RMA status, while a ReturnRequest event does not invalidate the `return-cases` detail family and does not carry/target the case relation. An open case detail can therefore retain the previous RMA status after receipt, disposition, or completion; this is finding #204, also source-only. ReturnCase events invalidate RMA lists but not necessarily singular `['return-request', id]` details when the case itself changes.

No Laravel Cache/remember/cache() response writer was found in the ReturnManagement module. RMA, case, timeline, attachment metadata, and portal transactional data are QueryClient memory behind authenticated `private, no-store` APIs; the registry records their query scopes and signal omissions. No tests, worker/socket, cache-audit, browser, or runtime checks were run in this continuation. All ReturnManagement UI result cells, as well as the B2B customer-RMA row, remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Discovery delta — Restore cache boundary implementation (2026-11-01)

The earlier source finding that restore/rollback left Redis generations active is addressed in source. `BackupService::executeRestore()` and `rollback()` now invoke `DatabaseRestoreCacheInvalidator` after installing the snapshot and before releasing maintenance/queues. The registry covers every identified application data-snapshot owner in the 118-call-site, 46-file audit; coordination, queue, session, rate-limit, and durable-validated idempotency keys are not purged. Settings, DSS and payroll progress use a restore-wide marker because removed rows cannot be individually enumerated. Rotation failures prevent service resume.

After service resumes, `DatabaseRestoreCompleted` is broadcast on private channels authorized separately for active ERP, supplier portal, and customer portal sessions. Each client reloads and refetches through its current API session. This is source wiring, not reproduction: Redis generation rotation, failure behavior, queue delivery, session reload, cross-role results and latency remain unverified. Old-generation payloads are unreachable and expire under their existing TTLs; no physical keyspace sweep is run.
