The central invalidation map covers the employee profile list, training matrix, and the existing skill-matrix/gap-analysis API query families. Assignment response data is installed into the actor's list cache; delete removes the row locally before invalidation. The update/restore helpers have no current SPA call site. Focused producer/channel/map/hook tests are authored but not run; the route UI outcomes, migration-independent event delivery and multi-user behavior remain NOT_VERIFIED. No backend cache entry was added.


### Discovery delta 325 — Skill catalog freshness

Source tracing confirmed three Skill mutation routes backed by `SkillService::create/update/deactivate`, with Eloquent transactions and no prior Skill observer/event. The Skills page performed actor-only `['hr','skills']` invalidation after create/deactivate; its assignment selector uses a separate `['hr','skills','all']` key, and EmployeeSkill profile/matrix resources embed or derive Skill catalog fields. Remote catalog, selector, profile, and competence-matrix readers therefore had no shared freshness trigger. Delta 325 adds a Skill observer and coalesced after-commit `SkillChanged` outbox event, with private catalog channels gated by the HR feature, active user, and `hr.trainings.view`, plus the existing permission-scoped EmployeeSkill refresh channel for viewers who cannot read the catalog. The payload includes only an opaque skill reference and refresh metadata, never skill text or actor identity; the actor hash stays in the outbox. The central invalidation map covers Skill catalog, EmployeeSkill lists, training matrix, and skill-matrix/gap-analysis queries. The actor applies returned create/deactivate resources to any matching in-memory row before active-query invalidation; the update helper currently has no SPA caller. Source-only: focused tests are authored but unrun, and runtime channel authorization, queue delivery, peer UI freshness and latency remain NOT_VERIFIED. No Laravel cache call or backend response-cache entry is involved.


### Discovery delta 326 — Profile-update request approvals

Source tracing confirmed submission plus HR approve/reject and Finance approve/reject all mutate ProfileUpdateRequest using Eloquent within transactions; the model previously had generic dashboard/badge invalidation but no HR queue, self-service history, or central invalidation event. The review UI is active and uses `['hr','profile-update-requests',status,page,awaitingMyAction]`; the requester uses `['self-service','profile-requests']`. Delta 326 adds a coalesced after-commit event through the durable outbox. HR viewer channels split view-all from the request employee's department. Finance receives the event only for bank requests in Finance-visible states (`pending_finance`, `approved`, or `rejected`). Requesters use an exact authenticated-user channel. Payloads contain refresh metadata and opaque request/employee references, not actor identity, profile fields, encrypted changes, bank numbers, notes, or reviewer remarks; actor identity remains in the outbox for traceability. The central map also invalidates related Employee, badge and HR dashboard keys; review mutation responses are installed into the actor's matching list rows before active-query invalidation. Source review found no query-builder/bulk ProfileUpdateRequest writer. No backend response cache was found; existing HR dashboard/badge cache generations rotate before this observer's outbox enqueue callback. Focused tests are authored but not run, so runtime authorization, event delivery, peer UI freshness and latency remain NOT_VERIFIED.


### Discovery delta 341 — conflict-only Production scheduling refresh

`POST /production/work-orders/{workOrder}/assignment-suggestion` invokes `CapacityPlanningService::run()`. The planner can create/supersede observed schedule rows or, on a conflict-only result, update only `WorkOrder.scheduling_diagnostic`. The scheduler snapshot reads the diagnostic array, so the latter path had no producer event and no schedule-row observer to invalidate connected scheduler viewers. Delta 341 adds a WorkOrder observer that reacts only when this field actually changes and stages a coalesced `WorkOrderSchedulingDiagnosticChanged` event through the durable after-commit outbox. It uses the existing `chain.refresh` envelope and private work-order record/list, ProductionSchedule list and Production dashboard channels, whose callbacks enforce the corresponding API read permissions. The event payload contains refresh metadata, a work-order hash, version, timestamp and opaque actor hash only. Existing map entries invalidate the work-order and scheduler snapshot query families; no cache entry was introduced. Source wiring is confirmed. Queue delivery, runtime channel authorization, browser rendering and latency remain NOT_VERIFIED; no scenario was run.

### Discovery delta 342 — keep realtime invalidation from replaying planner writes

Source tracing found that CrewPanel's generated-assignment suggestion query called the schedule-writing POST, while both WorkOrder and ProductionSchedule invalidation families include the active assignment-suggestion key. TanStack active invalidation refetches the query, which could replay the planner when a schedule signal arrived. Delta 342 retains automatic generation for a new staffing/date key, tracks successful generated keys within the dialog, and serves later realtime refetches through the read-only assignment-suggestion GET. Concurrent invalidation while the initial POST is running shares that promise; a failed request remains retryable. No endpoint or business rule changed. Static source review only; no browser/request trace, build or typecheck was run.


### Discovery delta 343 — approval delegation list freshness

ApprovalDelegationAccessRealtimeObserver already refreshed the delegate's auth state, but no event invalidated the delegator's list or an authorized manager's all-delegations list in another tab. Delta 343 stages a separate coalesced ApprovalDelegationChanged event for create, visible update, and delete. Its exact-user private channels target the active delegator and delegate; a private manager list channel requires `admin.delegations.manage_any`, the same permission used by ApprovalDelegationService::list() for its all-row scope. The SPA routes both through the central approval-delegation signal invalidation key. The event contains only refresh metadata, action/version and opaque actor identity. Delta 362 verifies a manager subscription with HTTP 200 and a finance/system-admin two-context UI scenario: create and revoke events reached the manager at 450 ms and 246 ms; the manager list changed from actionable Active to Revoked with the Revoke button removed. This verifies only these list transitions; detail pages, delegate access rendering, badges, dashboards, inboxes, and other consumers remain unverified.

### Discovery delta 345 — Production assignment conflict response and actor reconciliation

`WorkOrderAssignmentService::assign()` checks the expected current crew while holding the WorkOrder lock. `WorkOrderAssignmentController::apply()` checks the proposal's schedule ID and `updated_at`, then checks the locked schedule again before commit; selected-worker eligibility is also re-evaluated. Those concurrent changes were reported by `BusinessRuleException`, rendered as 422, while the assignment review disables retry on any error and left its proposal/crew reads unchanged. Delta 345 gives the concurrent crew/schedule/eligibility checks a typed 409, refreshes the open suggestion and crew through the authorized API, preserves manual worker selections, and allows retry only after both current reads succeed. The same shared service also supports repair-slot review; its UI now performs the equivalent authorized suggestion/crew refresh on 409 while preserving timing and approval inputs. Both direct and review assignment mutations install their returned `CrewContext` into the actor's current query before dependent invalidation. No API, browser, WebSocket, database, queue, or multi-user scenario was run; delivery and rendered UI outcomes remain NOT_VERIFIED.

### Discovery delta 346 — assignment proposals invalidated by every source dependency

Static tracing of `WorkOrderAssignmentController::suggestion()` found two query classes with different dependency sets: worker/equipment eligibility (`WorkOrderAssignmentService`) and confirmation readiness (stock coverage, child work orders, and inspection specifications via `WorkOrderService::proposalReadiness()`). The central event invalidation map refreshed some Production context but omitted the open assignment and repair suggestion queries for changes in those source records. Delta 346 adds both active proposal prefixes for shared worker/equipment dependencies (work-order/schedule, machine/mold, maintenance, employee, shift/shift-assignment, leave, attendance, and holiday); inventory-stock and inspection-spec events target the regular assignment proposal, while NCR changes target repair proposals. The events remain content-free and clients refetch their authorized API queries. This is a source-only correction; no live event or proposal rendering scenario was run, and every affected UI outcome remains NOT_VERIFIED.

### Discovery delta 347 — delivery recovery key cleanup

`DeliveryStockCostPanel` keeps UUID request keys in localStorage so an ambiguous network result can safely replay the same stock reservation or accounting handoff. Source tracing confirmed no cleanup on successful responses, so already-resolved UUIDs remained until the auth-scope cleanup. The stock-reservation batch and cost-handoff rows have unique durable request keys; for cost handoffs, a `manual_required` response must retain its key because a later retry can update that same row. Delta 347 removes the stock key only after the API response reports a reservation, and removes cost keys only for the response's terminal `generated`/`not_required` statuses. Ambiguous failures and retryable `manual_required` outcomes preserve the original key. The same browser-persistence scan classified the landing cookie-choice preference and the tab-scoped recovery-operation ID; neither stores transactional API data, and recovery status still refetches through the in-memory-token-authorized recovery API. This is lifecycle and inventory documentation from source evidence; no local-storage, API, database, or retry scenario was run.

### Discovery delta 348 — expired ordinary form drafts are pruned at startup and daily

`useFormDraftAutosave::readDraft()` checked the seven-day TTL for the form being opened, but expired drafts belonging to pages the user did not revisit stayed in localStorage. Delta 348 adds a startup and daily cleanup of keys under the `ogami:formdraft:` prefix for long-lived floor tabs. It inspects only entries shaped like ordinary autosave envelopes and deletes expired or invalid timestamp/value envelopes; the delivery-submission retry journal uses a different shape and remains available for idempotent recovery. The existing page-specific read check remains in place. This is source-derived lifecycle coverage only; no browser/local-storage expiry scenario was run.

### Discovery delta 349 — recent items no longer retain mutable status

The command palette's `/search` response status was copied by `CommandPalette.pick()` into the persisted `ogami:recent-items` store. The “Recent” section rendered the stored status chip without refetching, so it could stay stale for the row's 30-day lifetime. Delta 349 removes status from the recent-item type and persistence path; search response status remains transient and is no longer copied to recents. During hydration, the storage adapter accepts and removes legacy status fields, persists the sanitized history, and returns only the status-free records. Owner/access-scope clearing and the 30-day recent-history TTL remain in force. The separate active `/search` invalidation gap was logged in delta 350 and received centralized source wiring in delta 351. No browser or persisted-cache migration scenario was run.

### Discovery delta 350 — active global-search results lacked record-event invalidation

`CommandPalette` uses `['command-palette','search',debouncedTerm]` with the shared 30-second stale time. Before delta 351, the record invalidation map contained no search prefix and the shared QueryClient disabled window-focus refetch. A matching record event could therefore leave the status-bearing result stale while the palette was open or a term was reused after reopening. Delta 349 had already removed status persistence from Recent. Delta 351 adds rate-limited invalidation for the fourteen searchable record types; the active search refetch uses the caller's existing authorized API scope, and inactive terms are marked stale. The five-second limiter is per tab/QueryClient, so concurrent tabs still aggregate against the API's 30/minute throttle; cross-tab request coordination remains open. This addresses the source path only. No browser or network trace or search request-rate measurement was run.

### Runtime reproduction delta 352 — B2B portal deactivation revocation

On 2026-11-02, a real-stack Playwright scenario at `https://ogamiph.dev` used an internal admin plus two separately isolated customer portal browser contexts for the same customer principal. Each customer context authorized `portal.customer.user.{hashId}` with HTTP 200; customer `/me` was 200 before deactivation; the internal deactivation endpoint returned 200; and customer `/me` was 401 after deactivation. Before the producer/client fix, neither open socket received `b2b.portal-access.revoked` within 8 seconds. The customer tabs therefore had no server-pushed reason to discard their current session view.

After the fix, one measured run delivered the content-free signal to both exact-principal tabs, removed the portal navigation and redirected each to sign-in. Event and redirect observations were 640 ms and 641 ms after the deactivation request began. The endpoint remained 401 for the revoked account. This confirms one customer principal across two browser contexts; it does not prove distinct-customer isolation, supplier runtime behavior, p95 latency, reconnect while a browser is offline, or other portal query families. See `spa/e2e-real/portal-access-revocation.spec.ts` and root-cause entry #228. The seeded account was restored active with password-change enforcement disabled after each run.

### Runtime cache audit delta 354 — recurrent worker restart marker bounded

On 2026-11-02, `php artisan cache:audit --quiet-keys` scanned 185 keys under the active application cache prefix: auth=1, badges=1, dash=2, dashboard=15, dss=15, illuminate=1, inv=1, settings=149. Exactly one lacked a TTL: Laravel's `illuminate:queue:restart` worker-control timestamp. The timestamp was checked only to confirm its control-marker role; cached business values were not read. `php artisan queue:restart` invoked Laravel's existing command-finished TTL handler and caused the worker to restart gracefully. The next audit found zero entries without TTL, above 1 MiB, or with unknown size. `php artisan cache:prune-unbounded --limit=500` in default dry-run mode found zero candidates and deleted nothing. No cache entry was deleted. The queue worker returned healthy, the local Vite HMR process remained active, and `https://ogamiph.dev` returned HTTP 200. The reason the marker had recurred is unknown; production Redis was not inspected.

### Discovery delta 358 — Alert widget cache candidate ruled out

`DashboardWidgetDataService` caches the configurable `alerts` scalar for 30 seconds. An isolated call to `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` did not rotate its named scoped generation, but that is not the registered write path: `Alert` is also observed by `BadgeInvalidationObserver`, whose `BadgeService::touch()` rotates the shared dashboard generation included in every widget-summary key. Alert dismiss/resolve/prune query-builder paths call `BadgeService::touch()` before recording their after-commit refresh signal. Focused existing tests for Alert prune and Action Center dismissal passed (2 warning-marked tests, 14 assertions) with assertions that the `alerts` widget cache generation changes. The initial isolation-only assertion was discarded as a false-positive test design; no backend cache change was needed. Broad cache-generation rotation may lower unrelated widget hit rates, but no cache hit or database load measurement was made.

The same earlier SPA typecheck reported that `ChainBottleneckWidget` used the shared `formatDateTime` formatter without importing it. Added the existing utility import; no UI behavior or refresh cadence changed. No typecheck was rerun after this import.

### Discovery delta 359 — live metrics use event refresh instead of 60-second API polling

The registry currently defines two metrics with `refresh=live`: `returns.pipeline` reads ReturnRequest state; `production.wo_lead_time` reads WorkOrder timestamps/machine. `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` retires each independent AnalyticsCache generation after commit. Both models are also registered with `BadgeInvalidationObserver`, which stages `BadgesChanged` after commit; Sidebar mounts the shared listener and the central signal map invalidates the dashboard query prefix. RealtimeProvider supplies active-query catch-up on degraded delivery. `useMetric` no longer polls the API; a one-shot browser timer only makes the stale badge visible after its threshold. Focused MetricPanel regression: red baseline made three API calls over 120 seconds; after change, 6/6 tests passed with one initial request, no time-driven refetch, and a stale badge at two minutes. This verifies local query behavior only, not websocket receipt, event-to-UI latency, multi-user behavior or cache hit/load impact. Future `live` registry entries need equivalent source/event/cache mapping before they can rely on this path.

### Runtime audit delta 361 — broadcasts, Redis cache TTLs, and scheduler recovery

The active dev Laravel config resolves the application cache store to Redis connection `cache` (the separate `redis-cache` container), and broadcast jobs to Redis connection `broadcasts`, queue `broadcasts`, logical Redis DB 1. `horizon:status` reports running; its configured broadcasts supervisor has one process. Framework-source tracing confirms all 116 broadcast event classes implement `ShouldBroadcast` and `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`; all use `BroadcastsOnDedicatedQueue`, and none use `ShouldBroadcastNow`. `BroadcastsOnDedicatedQueue` selects the connection/queue but does not itself defer dispatch, so the explicit interface is the commit-boundary guarantee. This rules out an event-class-level before-commit broadcast cause, but does not verify that every mutation dispatches, the job reaches Reverb, or each SPA consumer refetches.

`cache:audit --quiet-keys` inspected 185 keys under the active cache prefix without reading values or deleting keys: 0 had no TTL, 0 exceeded 1 MiB, and 0 had unknown size. The bounded apply command `cache:prune-unbounded --apply --limit=500` found 0 candidates and deleted 0. The scheduler was found exited with code 1 since 2026-10-29, after the `ar:run-dunning` scheduled command failed. Its old `set -e` loop and missing Docker restart policy had stopped recurring cache audit/prune and outbox recovery work. The dev override now logs a failed tick and continues after 60 seconds, with `restart: unless-stopped`; only the scheduler container was recreated. It is currently running, and its startup tick reported the outbox recovery dispatch successful. The reason for the dunning failure remains unknown and is tracked separately in `FINDINGS.md`.

This metadata and infrastructure evidence does not demonstrate the semantic freshness of values, old generation-key cleanup, delivery latency, two-user UI behavior, or cache hit/performance impact. No tests or business mutations were run in this audit.

## Runtime follow-up — dead-lettered refreshes degraded the global health signal (2026-11-02)

During the Approval Delegation two-context run, the system-admin page displayed `Reconnecting` while the manager's private channel had authorized and create/update frames were received. A read-only runtime inspection found `RealtimeQueueHealth::deliveryHealthy()` false, Redis `high` and `broadcasts` queue depths both 0, no overdue pending outbox row, and 35 `event_outbox` rows with status `failed`: 19 `AdminDashboardChanged`, 10 `ActivityFeedChanged`, and 6 `AdminUserChanged`, all at three attempts. The stored error category was `RuntimeException` for an event type not registered for durable publication. Current `OutboxEventCodec::supportedEventTypes()` includes all three classes.

The Horizon container has a bind-mounted API tree and is long-lived; PHP queue workers keep loaded classes in memory. The old worker that marked these rows failed was not available to inspect directly, so stale worker code is the strongly supported cause rather than a captured process-memory fact. The currently observed `high` worker had restarted after the current codec source timestamp. `RealtimeQueueHealth` treats any failed outbox row as degraded; `RealtimeProvider` therefore shows `Reconnecting` and falls back to visibility-aware active-query catch-up. This explains the indicator and degraded fallback, but does not invalidate the successful manager-list frame evidence. `outbox:dispatch` retries dead letters only with explicit `--retry-failed`; the rows remain until reviewed and retried. At the time this pre-fix snapshot was recorded, queue state had not yet changed.

### Recovery follow-up (2026-11-02)

After confirming that the three event classes are currently supported, their payloads are refresh-only, and decode failed before event dispatch, ran `php artisan outbox:dispatch --retry-failed --limit=100`. It requeued and enqueued exactly 35 rows. Follow-up runtime state showed all 2,575 outbox rows published, zero failed rows among the affected event types, `high=0`, `broadcasts=0`, and `delivery_healthy=true`. No business record was edited and no cache key was deleted. The source deployment instructions already provide `make dev-refresh-php` to restart local PHP-FPM, Horizon, mail worker, and Reverb after mounted PHP changes; production instructions use Horizon termination/restart.

A subsequent single Playwright rerun with finance and system-admin contexts verified the manager status indicator changed to `Live` after health recovery. The manager then received create and revoke events and refreshed the active action/status. Request-start-to-peer-frame samples were 443 ms and 201 ms; these are not commit-to-UI p95 measurements.

## Runtime follow-up — Purchase Request approval inbox cross-role refresh (2026-11-02)

Ran only `spa/e2e-real/approval-board-realtime.spec.ts` against `https://ogamiph.dev` with independent Purchasing, Finance, and VP browser contexts (one creator plus the two live approval contexts). The first run stopped before the approval action because the spec waited for `private-approvals.visible`; the current board hook subscribes to `private-badges`. The Finance actor's WebSocket was deliberately closed; after the approve PATCH returned 200, its detail removed the Approve action. In the measured run, this took 157 ms. The open VP board's private badge subscription succeeded, it received one `BadgesChanged` frame and refetched `/api/v1/approvals/board`; the request moved from Awaiting others step 1 to My action step 2 within 740 ms of the actor API response. A separate authorized board GET confirmed `step_order=2`. This establishes one measured API-response-to-UI sample, not commit-to-UI p95. It does not verify the regular Purchase Request list, a second detail page, numeric badge values, linked records, dashboard data, or degraded-worker catch-up. Evidence is recorded in the approval row of `01-stale-risk-matrix.csv` and root cause 34.

## Source inventory delta 369 — explicit state-machine edges

A repository-wide `rg` scan of `api/app` found 15 constants named `TRANSITIONS`, `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS`, or `LIFECYCLE_TRANSITIONS`; the Production `WorkOrderService` re-exports the same map but does not define another one. Read-only PHP reflection inside the API container enumerated the values without querying the database. The resulting [`00-explicit-state-transitions.csv`](00-explicit-state-transitions.csv) contains 113 edges: Accounting 7, HR 30, Loans 7, Maintenance 7, Production 9, Quality 13, Return Management 12, Assets 3, and CRM 25.

The edge count excludes self-loops. Employee's 9 conditional terminal edges (active/on_leave/suspended → resigned/terminated/retired) are included because the source allows them only after clearance. Complaint's service map lists operation names and allowed current states, so its resolve/close edges were expanded to their target statuses. Three AssetTransfer edges are kept in the inventory but marked deferred because `api/app/Modules/Assets/routes.php` explicitly says the service is not routed. Candidate endpoints and writer services are recorded per row, with route-to-edge mapping status kept explicit.

This supplemental list covers only these centralized source maps; it does not claim a complete project transition inventory. Direct status assignments, enum-backed records without one of these maps, implicit workflow changes, scheduled/import/webhook writers outside the mapped services, and related-record side effects remain to be catalogued. All 113 edge-level runtime/UI outcomes are `NOT_VERIFIED`; existing route-row or narrative scenarios are not inferred to cover an exact edge. No tests, browser runs, application mutation, database write, Redis operation or cache-value read occurred in this source inventory slice.

### Source inventory delta 371 — critical-stock auto-PO has no PO refresh signal

`StockMovementService` stages `StockMovementCompleted` through `OutboxService`; after-commit outbox dispatch invokes `CheckReorderPoint::handle()`, which calls `AutoReplenishmentService::checkAndReplenish()`. The scheduled `inventory:reconcile-replenishment --limit=100` command invokes the same service for active blocked-item alerts. For a critical shortage with exactly one qualified preferred supplier and an authoritative positive price, `AutoPurchaseOrderService::createForCriticalShortage()` creates a `PurchaseOrder`, sets it to `PendingApproval`, creates its line, and submits its approval workflow in a transaction.

The PO write is covered by the after-commit `ProcurementChainCacheObserver`, dashboard cache invalidation and generic badge invalidation. The badge signal can refresh approval counts/inbox, but this auto-PO writer does not call `ChainBroadcaster` or stage a PO-specific refresh event. The purchase-order list subscribes through `useRealtimeList('purchase_order', 'visible')` and the detail subscribes through `useChainProgress('purchase_order', id)`. Source therefore establishes a missing PO record/list signal on this path; it does not establish stale API cache data or reproduce a visible two-user failure. The current P2P status-write matrix contains 27 source-mapped PR/PO paths, including supplier-response states and receipt/return recalculation. It has actor, peer-detail, list, action, related-record, badge, dashboard and approval-inbox columns; all transition-level outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The source disposition is recorded in `00-direct-status-write-paths.csv` and the scheduled row in `01-stale-risk-matrix.csv`. No scheduler, stock movement, browser, test, database, Redis or queue operation was run for this finding.

At delta 371, `AutoPurchaseOrderServiceTest` covered canonical `PendingApproval` creation and the approval records, but contained no assertion for a PO chain/outbox refresh. `PurchaseOrderWriteRealtimeTest` covered PR conversion and PO update/delete/restore refreshes, not the critical-stock writer. Those files were read but not run at that point; see the focused reproduction and fix in delta 373 below.

## Discovery delta — stock-adjustment list refresh path (2026-11-02)

The stock-adjustment page had no list subscription; its actor-side mutation callbacks invalidate locally, but peer viewers had no `StockAdjustmentChanged` producer for pending creation, rejection or withdrawal. `BadgeInvalidationObserver` refreshes counts and the approval board, not this list query. Added an Eloquent observer that coalesces refresh-only outbox events per adjustment and transaction; `entity.stock_adjustment.visible` authorization matches the GET endpoint's `inventory.adjust` / `inventory.adjust.approve` permission gate. The page now uses the shared `useRealtimeList` hook and central invalidation map. The new event payload carries only the opaque adjustment and actor identifiers, action, version and event ID. A focused backend test failed before the change with zero outbox rows and passed after it; channel allow/deny and frontend list-hook tests also passed. No two-user browser scenario, queue/Reverb delivery, approval/reject/withdraw runtime transition or latency measurement was run. The approve route's existing generic-list PASS is for a separate stock-level quantity refresh, not the adjustment list; adjustment-list and related actor/detail/action/badge/dashboard/inbox outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Discovery delta — ItemCostReview list refresh (2026-11-02)

Source tracing found ItemCostReview creation from accepted-receipt pricing review and decision/renew writes from `ItemCostReviewService`, but no dedicated event reached the cost-review list. The SPA page had no subscription; its modal also held a local record copy, so a list refetch alone would not refresh visible status/actions. Added a transaction-coalesced `ItemCostReviewChanged` outbox ping, a private reviewer channel matching the API read permission, and central SPA query invalidation. Actor mutations seed the returned resource immediately; remote changes refetch the authorized list and replace the modal record while preserving draft notes and requiring acknowledgement before another action.

Focused backend event/payload tests (22 and 10 assertions), channel authorization (3 assertions), and the SPA page tests (9 tests) passed. No two-user browser, queue/Reverb delivery, or latency trace ran. Both cost-review route rows keep all UI outcome cells `NOT_VERIFIED`. Inventory source gaps remain for MaterialReviewRecord and MaterialIssueSlip. Catalog totals are 173 event classes, 118 broadcast payload rows, 329 async entries; no cache/performance metric was collected.

### Discovery delta — ItemCostReview action projection (2026-11-03)

Rechecking the workflow against the server-action contract found that the event path was already present: the registered observer stages `ItemCostReviewChanged` after commit, the private list channel matches the GET permission, and the SPA uses the shared list hook and invalidation map. The resource still omitted viewer-specific permissions/actions, however, and the page derived Review/Renew labels and modal controls from local permissions plus record status. A focused API regression failed on the missing permission field and then passed for Finance, Purchasing, direct approval, delegated approval, and pending/rejected states (1 test / 22 assertions). A focused SPA regression reproduced a pending row rendering Review when the API explicitly denied its actions; it passes after the page consumes `available_actions`.

This confirms an action-projection contract gap, not a missing event producer. A post-change real headless Chromium scenario with a Finance actor and delegated Purchasing viewer passed (1 / 16.3 s): the viewer received the exact private event, made an authorized list refetch, rendered Approved in its list and open modal, and lost Approve/Reject. This single 1,749 ms actor-response-to-viewer-render sample is not commit-to-UI or p95. The decide route actor/detail/list/action cells are now PASS; related-record, badge, dashboard and inbox outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. The authorized list cache remains in-memory only and is invalidated by the event map; no cache was removed and no cache hit/request cost was measured.

## Discovery delta — ProductRouting refresh path (2026-11-02)

Source tracing found four parent writes in `ProductionRoutingService`: create, update (publishes a new version), duplicate, and activate. Each completes BOM recalculation/MRP replan propagation inside its transaction. Before the change, no ProductRouting event producer or routing list/detail subscription existed. The red-first create regression failed with zero `event_outbox` rows.

The current path stages `ProductionRoutingChanged` from the shared service propagation method through `OutboxService::recordCoalesced()`, so the row is committed with the routing transaction and dispatch is scheduled after commit. The broadcast targets only private list and record channels and carries refresh metadata plus opaque product/routing/actor hashes. Channel callbacks require the production feature, active user, `production.view` and `production.routings.view`; the record callback also validates the routing hash and row. The SPA list and detail use the central hooks/map; actor mutations seed the API response into detail cache.

Focused backend producer, authorization, payload/codec, and SPA map checks passed. The contract guard was also brought back into alignment with the source: it had omitted 24 existing direct `ShouldBroadcast` classes; the corrected guard covers all 121 and passes its private-channel/after-commit/dedicated-queue assertions. No two-browser scenario, queue/Reverb delivery trace, mutation network trace, or latency measurement was run. Therefore the reported cross-user rendered failure is not claimed as reproduced; the missing producer was reproduced at the outbox boundary and the UI delivery remains NOT_VERIFIED.


## Discovery delta — paused work-order resource release and soft-delete refreshes (2026-11-02)

Source tracing confirmed `releaseResources()` updates the paused WorkOrder release timestamp while Machine/Mold availability observers publish separate resource events. The work-order detail response embeds that release state and assigned-resource data, but the work-order itself had no refresh producer. The focused pre-fix release-resources regression found no `ChainStepAdvanced` outbox row despite related machine/mold events. `delete()` and `restore()` also changed WorkOrder visibility with no direct WorkOrder refresh producer.

The fix uses `ChainBroadcaster::refreshWorkOrder()` to stage the existing content-free `chain.refresh` event to the authorized private work-order detail/list channels. It calls `OutboxService::record()` instead of the chain-step ledger helper because release/delete/restore do not advance a business chain status. The event is staged inside the owning transaction. Focused release, delete/restore, and channel/payload unit checks passed. No peer browser, actual outbox worker delivery, or latency trace was captured; the UI matrix remains NOT_VERIFIED.


## Discovery delta — finished-goods receipt retry refresh (2026-11-02)

`POST /production/work-orders/{workOrder}/outputs/{output}/retry-receipt` invokes `WorkOrderOutputService::retryProductionReceipt()`. Before this slice, a successful retry created the inventory receipt and requested quality close, but did not signal the WorkOrder when its workflow status remained unchanged. The no-good-output branch could also change receipt-handoff state without a WorkOrder refresh. When an expected retry failure changed the output to `manual_required`, the actor's page only showed a toast and did not refetch its detail/output state. These are source-confirmed propagation gaps; a second browser's stale view was not reproduced.

Retry now stages the existing content-free WorkOrder `ChainStepAdvanced` refresh in the same transaction when receipt state changes without a WorkOrder status transition. A status transition continues to emit its normal canonical chain event. Successful API responses immediately update the actor's embedded detail and output query entries before related query invalidation; an error invalidates/refetches both keys. The central `work_order` map now also invalidates `['production','work-orders','outputs']`, which is a child query family separate from WorkOrder detail.

The red-first `test_zero_product_cost_requires_recovery_without_posting_zero_value_stock` failed before the producer change with no `ChainStepAdvanced` outbox row after a successful retry; the focused case passes after the change (4 assertions; existing test fixture warnings include missing COA 5010). The SPA invalidation-map case, successful actor-cache application, and failed-retry actor-refetch tests pass. No two-user browser, queue/Reverb delivery trace, or commit-to-UI latency was captured. Every UI outcome cell for this route remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Discovery delta — ordinary WorkOrder draft creation refresh (2026-11-02)

The WorkOrder create endpoint routes ordinary draft requests to `WorkOrderService::createDraft()`. The service creates the parent WorkOrder, its BOM materials, and any operations from an active routing. When no routing operations were created, the parent had no direct WorkOrder event or observer; the scheduled-diagnostic observer only handles updates. The focused regression created a product with no active routing and failed because `event_outbox` was empty. This confirms the server producer gap; it does not reproduce another user's rendered list.

`createDraft()` now stages `ChainBroadcaster::refreshWorkOrder()` in its transaction when no operation rows exist. If active routing operations were generated, their existing observer coalesces one `WorkOrderOperationChanged` parent refresh for the transaction, so the create path avoids adding a duplicate WorkOrder signal. Stock planning and MRP/NCR callers share this service path and their downstream writes remain inside their outer transactions.

The focused no-routing regression passes (2 assertions), including no fabricated chain-step ledger row. A second focused active-routing test confirms its existing coalesced `WorkOrderOperationChanged` signal remains the sole WorkOrder refresh (no duplicate `ChainStepAdvanced` row). No two-browser, outbox-worker/Reverb delivery, or latency trace ran. The WorkOrder create route's actor/peer/list/UI outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

### Delta 402 — SupplierItemListing lifecycle source trace

`SupplierListingService::submit()` creates Pending; `approve()` changes Pending→Approved, query-updates a prior Approved listing to Superseded, and upserts the matching ApprovedSupplier; `reject()` changes Pending→Rejected. `submitMany()`, `bulkApprove()`, and `bulkReject()` delegate to those single-record paths and stage one vendor-coalesced catalog event after successful rows. Updating offer fields is a write while Pending but is not a status edge. No scheduled/import/webhook status writer was found in the Purchasing/B2B source search.

Each service mutation records `SupplierCatalogChanged` in the owning transaction. The event implements `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`, uses the dedicated broadcasts queue trait, and contains only refresh/action/version/event metadata. It targets permission-gated `entity.supplier_listing.visible` and `entity.approved_supplier.visible` channels plus `supplier.vendor.{vendorHashId}`. The portal callback accepts only the active portal user for that exact active vendor; the supplier list hook invalidates the vendor-keyed listing query. The internal review list subscribes to the entity signal and invalidates its current filters; approval also affects approved-supplier query families. Registered `BadgeInvalidationObserver` runs after commit for the created/updated listing model and stages badge refresh. The older listing changed by the approval query-builder update does not invoke that old row’s model observer.

The mutation resource returns the updated listing to the actor, now including per-user `permissions` and pending/delegation-aware `available_actions`. The SPA immediately reconciles single-row responses across cached filter/page variants, then invalidates the central supplier-listing map. Bulk responses retain their existing `{id,status,message}` contract; successful statuses are reconciled into cached rows before central invalidation on settlement. Row controls and bulk selection use the server action flags, with a legacy status/permission fallback only when the API omits the new field. `SupplierListingService` continues to revalidate permission and Pending state on every action. No supplier-listing detail page/API consumer was found. No listing-specific backend Cache/remember entry was found.

Post-change evidence from `spa/e2e-real/supplier-catalog-live.spec.ts`: a Purchasing actor and separate supplier portal session observed approval; the actor row changed to Approved and removed Approve, while the supplier list received the vendor-private signal, refetched through its authorized API, rendered Approved and removed Edit 523 ms after the actor response. Exact vendor channel authorization succeeded in the scenario, and the existing authorization test denies another vendor. This is one response-to-render sample, not commit-to-UI or p95. The scenario also submitted a listing and observed the actor Pending row/Edit action. It did not keep a Purchasing list open during create, assert an older row becoming Superseded, test rejection, approved-supplier consumers, badges, dashboards or the approval board. The run passed one scenario in 25.5 seconds and left its uniquely named approved test listing in the authorized development database.

Added four transition rows and four writer rows. The transition catalog has 205 rows, the direct writer catalog has 70 rows, and 54 of 83 enum-backed fields remain without an explicit edge map. The 752 route/scheduled matrix row count is unchanged.


### Delta 403 — SalesOrderResponse lifecycle source trace

`SalesOrderResponse.status` has five distinct reachable edges: ∅→Pending; ∅→Accepted for customer Accept; Pending→Superseded; Pending→Accepted; and Pending→Rejected. Pending rows come from internal delivery proposals, customer cancellation requests, and customer propose/decline replies. A new customer reply supersedes an older pending reply; `SalesOrder::supersedeDeliveryProposals()` supersedes older pending date proposals when replaced, when a draft order is updated, or when the order is cancelled. The cancellation service also query-updates pending cancellation requests to Accepted. Customer date decisions and internal Sales resolution transition Pending to Accepted/Rejected under locks and guards. The API transition endpoints are CRM propose-delivery-date, CRM accept/reject response, customer order respond/cancel, and customer delivery-proposal respond. The existing GET response-history endpoint has no SPA call site found; SalesOrder detail embeds the latest response and delivery negotiations.

All status-changing paths stage the parent SalesOrder with `ChainBroadcaster`/OutboxService in the mutation transaction. `ChainStepAdvanced` dispatches after commit to the dedicated broadcasts queue. It publishes only refresh/event/entity identifiers. Internal listeners use `chain.sales_order.{hashId}` and `entity.sales_order.visible`, authorized against the SalesOrder read permission; customer listeners use `customer.order.{customerHashId}`, authorized to an active portal user for that active customer account. CRM detail/list subscribe to the parent signal; CustomerPortalLayout binds the account-scoped hook and central invalidation map. The map refreshes the order detail/list/chain, customer portal order families, related views, badges, and dashboard query families. The APIs return response/order resources; SPA mutation handlers invalidate their local order queries after success, so actor refresh does not depend on the socket, but it currently uses a refetch instead of applying the returned response to cache. Buttons use latest response status/capability and permissions; server services lock and revalidate the state.

No SalesOrderResponse-specific Laravel cache entry or standalone SPA response query was found. Parent SalesOrder query/dashboard caches remain governed by their existing generation/query invalidation owners. Related effects include proposed SalesOrder item/price changes, delivery-date item updates, and cancellation cascade to eligible deliveries, work orders, MRP plan, and purchasing demand; each path uses the relevant parent/service refreshes. No distinct response status edge has multi-user browser evidence. Existing docs record a SalesOrder create-only two-context scenario, not customer response submission/proposal/resolution. No new tests, API mutations, browser sessions, queue/socket operations, or cache measurement occurred.

Added five transition rows and nine direct status-writer groups. Current totals: 210 explicit state edges, 79 direct writer rows, 53 unmapped enum-backed status fields, and 752 route/scheduled matrix rows.


### Delta 404 — PriceRequest state and cache/realtime trace

`PriceNegotiationService` reaches nine status edges: ∅→Requested from customer request; ∅→PendingFinance from a direct internal offer; Requested→PendingFinance and Offered→PendingFinance when sales creates or revises terms; PendingFinance→Offered after Finance approval; Offered→Accepted or Rejected from customer decision; Requested/PendingFinance/Offered→Withdrawn from guarded internal withdrawal; and PendingFinance/Offered→Expired from guarded daily expiry. Editing an already-PendingFinance proposal increments version and refreshes the record without a state-edge change. Daily `pricing:review-agreements` calls `expire()`; the every-minute `--dispatch-only` sibling does not. No other direct query, import, webhook or scheduled status writer appeared in the app source scan.

The registered PriceRequest model observer stages PriceRequestChanged for each Eloquent create/update. PriceRequestRealtimeService coalesces same-record writes in the current PostgreSQL transaction; OutboxService publishes after commit on the dedicated broadcasts queue. Channels include `crm.price-request.{hashId}` and `entity.price_request.visible`, authorized by CRM feature and view/approve/propose permission, plus `customer.price-request.{customerHashId}` restricted to the active portal account. The payload contains refresh/event/time/entity/action/version/opaque actor metadata and optional opaque related agreement ID; price, quantity, customer details and notes are omitted.

CRM AppLayout subscribes to the entity-list channel when the user has a pricing permission; selected CRM rows/details also subscribe per record. CustomerPortalLayout subscribes to the account channel. Central invalidation covers CRM list/detail/cost-review, customer pricing lists and dashboard query families. API mutations return fresh resources; CRM and customer pages seed returned rows into their local caches before invalidating related families. PriceRequestResource provides version, permissions and available_actions, while the service checks state/version under lock and maps stale conflicts to HTTP 409. DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver rotates `widget-summary:sales.price_requests` and `widget-rich:sales.price_requests` generations. The cache registry already lists these query and dashboard owners; no standalone PriceRequest backend/API response cache was found.

Delivery lifecycle refreshes cover the embedded reservation data; reserve/release use `StockReservationChanged`, and issue uses `StockMovementCompleted`. Existing inventory dashboard generation invalidation is a separate cache owner. No new event class or backend cache key was found.

### Delta 415 — Accounting receivables, payables, payment submissions, periods, and credit notes

Mapped six Accounting enum-backed fields from their model casts, status writers, route rows, and realtime/cache owners. InvoicePaymentSubmission is created Pending and can become Verified only through collection recording or Rejected through the Finance review path; the owning Invoice chain refresh is staged in the same outer transaction. CreditNote is created Draft, finalized after GL posting, becomes Applied only when its balance reaches zero, or becomes Void after reversing an unapplied posted journal. Its registered observer stages CreditNoteChanged after commit, and credit application also refreshes the target Invoice or Bill.

Invoice is created Draft, finalized after GL posting, cancelled only under the service’s paid-balance/pending-submission guards, and moves from Finalized/Partial to Partial/Paid through collection or credit application. Bill is created Draft or directly Unpaid, posted Draft→Unpaid, cancelled before payment, moves Unpaid/Partial→Partial/Paid on payment/credit application, and is recomputed after posted-payment void. BillPayment can be committed PendingApproval or directly Posted when the approval chain is already complete, then moves PendingApproval→Posted/Rejected or Posted→Voided. The service stages Bill parent ChainBroadcaster refreshes on these write paths. AccountingPeriod closes Open/absent periods, reopens Closed periods with an audit reason, and the scheduled relock moves Reopened→Closed; the registered observer stages coalesced AccountingPeriodChanged and retires the Finance dashboard generation.

No new source-confirmed missing producer or new cache family was identified in these six status families. Existing procurement-chain, finance-dashboard, financial-statement and period cache entries remain separately owned; no cache was removed and no result cache was found for the transactional record resources. All actor/peer/list/action/badge/dashboard UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. This is source evidence only; no test, browser, API mutation, queue/cache operation, or performance measurement ran.

This is a source-confirmed side-effect ordering concern, not a dynamically reproduced unauthorized request or data disclosure; it is recorded in `FINDINGS.md` for separate review. Matrix outcome cells for the five GET rows remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. No code, database state, or cache was changed in this inventory reconciliation.

## Delta 476 — admin real-time/cache health telemetry (2026-11-02)

Source review found `/api/v1/realtime/health` returned only a shared healthy/degraded bit and Settings → System Info showed runtime versions/drivers without queue backlog, Redis cache memory, or the last scheduled cache-audit outcome. Added a separate `GET /api/v1/admin/system-health` response under `admin.settings.manage`, plus a 30-second Settings panel. `RealtimeQueueHealth` now caches one global aggregate snapshot for one second: pending broadcast-queue size, pending/failed outbox counts, oldest pending age, and the same delivery state used by the existing realtime-health endpoint. Reverb is checked with a 250 ms TCP connection to the configured server target; this only demonstrates listener reachability, not a Pusher/WebSocket handshake, authenticated channel subscription, or client delivery. Cache memory reports `used_memory` and `maxmemory` from the dedicated cache Redis connection, without returning its host or credentials.

`cache:audit` writes an optional aggregate snapshot under `ops:cache-audit:last:v1` with an eight-day TTL. It contains only completion time and counts for entries, missing TTLs, oversized entries and unavailable size reads; it stores no key names or values. A snapshot-write failure is logged but does not convert a successful scan into command failure; readers display Unknown if the snapshot is absent or malformed. The new API route is guarded by the same permission as Settings and returns no record data. Focused permission/shape, cache-summary and existing queue-health tests are recorded in `PROGRESS.md`. A SPA typecheck still reports unrelated pre-existing errors; no authenticated browser view, WebSocket handshake, queue-worker failure, cache hit-rate, or API latency was tested in this delta.

Before delta 478, the cache-call appendix had 384 recorded source/line snippets (120 application, 207 test, and 57 migration/seeder rows). Delta 478 reindexed changed cache-helper and audit call sites; the current count is recorded at the top of this discovery file and in `PROGRESS.md`.

## After-fix two-context retest — final Purchase Request approval (2026-11-02)

Ran one headless Chromium Playwright scenario against the `APP_ENV=local` dev deployment at `https://ogamiph.dev`, using four isolated browser contexts: Purchasing created/submitted a ₱60,000 PR, Finance advanced it to the VP step, System Administrator held the record detail open, and the Vice President performed final approval. The actor's WebSocket was deliberately closed. The PATCH returned 200; the actor rendered Approved and removed Approve within the test's 2 s assertion bound. The peer's exact private record channel acknowledged, three `chain.refresh` frames were observed, and an authorized detail GET returned 200. Without reload, the peer rendered Approved and removed its stale Cancel button. The actor-response-to-peer-render sample was 3.508 s, above the proposed 1–2 s target; this is not commit-to-UI p95. Eight peer detail GETs were recorded across page setup and final render, but the individual pre-action refetch triggers were not isolated. This confirms the after-fix final-approval peer-detail path for one record and two roles; lists, linked records, counts, badges, dashboards, inbox and other PR transitions remain unverified.

Read-only follow-up of the successful record found three final-state `ChainStepAdvanced` outbox rows, all published on their first attempt, matching the three peer frames. The PR's asynchronous conversion handoff subsequently ended in `manual_required`; these are refreshes for the approval and linked conversion updates. The stored timestamps have one-second precision, so this does not isolate the 3.508 s client-visible delay or prove a queue-lag cause.

The first run stopped before approval on a test-only PR-number assertion after the new record had been submitted; that PR was subsequently cancelled through the existing PurchaseRequestService. The successful final-approval PR remains approved as test data. The exact browser scenario is `spa/e2e-real/purchase-request-final-approval-realtime.spec.ts`; Playwright artifacts were directed to `/tmp` so the root-owned `spa/test-results` directory and existing report were preserved.

## Delta 482 — remove initial real-time catch-up duplication and retest PR final approval (2026-11-02 UTC)

Source tracing found three overlapping refetch triggers during page startup: `RealtimeProvider` globally invalidated all active queries on the first Echo connection; each authorized private channel already ran its own `onSubscribed` catch-up; and the provider also globally caught up immediately when the first health sample was degraded. The provider then treated initial connect separately from a real reconnect, skipped the redundant initial global pass, and skipped a second global pass for the first degraded-health observation. Later health transitions, reconnect, focus/visibility, and browser-online still perform global active-query catch-up. At that stage the visible delivery-health probe ran every ten seconds (request timeout remained two seconds); a one-second shared cache entry held aggregate queue/outbox status, never user or record data. The peer was visible and focused. Its responses had `Cache-Control: max-age=0, must-revalidate, no-store, private`, `CF-Cache-Status: DYNAMIC`, and `fromServiceWorker=false`, ruling out browser/service-worker/shared-proxy caching as the cause in this sample. ## Delta 487 — same-user dashboard layout freshness and cache ownership (2026-11-02 UTC)

Source tracing confirmed that `PUT /api/v1/dashboard/layout` and `POST /api/v1/dashboard/layout/reset` commit only the acting user's layout rows and return a current layout/version to the actor. Before this delta, neither path staged an event, so sibling tabs had no signal to invalidate their personal `['dashboard','layout']` and `['dashboard','widget-data']` queries. The stale layout version could then produce the existing 409 on a later save. The dashboard layout read itself has no Laravel response-cache entry. The React Query keys also omitted user/permission identity even though the layout and widget results are permission-dependent; the auth store already clears its client cache when identity or access scope changes, but key ownership relied on that global clearing.

A red-first API regression observed zero outbox rows after a changed personal save. A second red regression observed the empty-widget save delete saved rows but return early before event staging. A red-first picker regression confirmed that a remote layout prop update reset the in-progress draft. Delta 487 adds `DashboardLayoutChanged`, staged through the durable outbox inside the layout transaction only when canonical layout rows change, including empty-save clear and reset. The signal uses the active owner's existing exact-self private `user.preferences.{userHashId}` channel; the wire payload omits user hash and layout/widget content. AppLayout subscribes once through the shared realtime hook, and the central map invalidates both personal dashboard query families. The save/reset API response now seeds the actor's identity-scoped layout cache immediately. Dashboard layout and widget-data keys include `realtimeIdentityKey`, which contains user, role, employee, permissions, delegated approvals and enabled features. The picker retains its draft on an incoming layout change, shows a warning and disables stale submission until the user closes and reopens to review the new layout.

Four exact DashboardLayout feature regressions pass (4 tests / 14 assertions): changed save, changed reset, empty-save clear, and no-op reset. Two broadcast contract/inventory assertions pass (2 tests / 1,221 assertions). The private preferences-channel authorization case passes 1 test / 3 assertions, allowing the owner and denying a different account. Focused SPA DashboardPicker and central invalidation files pass 84 tests, including identity-key isolation for the widget catalog, layout and widget data across both accounts and same-account permission changes; actor response application; sibling refresh invalidation; and draft preservation. Targeted ESLint and scoped Pint `--test` pass. These checks do not run two tabs against live Reverb/Redis; browser delivery and peer render latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. No cache was removed and no cache-hit or performance impact was measured.

## Delta 489 — central mutation cache reconciliation across module query families (2026-11-02 UTC)

The red-first cache harness reproduced an in-flight detail fetch replacing a newer mutation response with the older status. Source tracing found the same pattern in full-record mutation handlers outside the shared status hook. The exact-key exclusion was therefore moved into the central invalidation implementation and exposed through both `invalidateRealtimeChange()` and `invalidateRealtimeSignal()`. Before refreshing any active matching family, it cancels older reads with `revert:false`; callers may preserve exact keys already seeded from an authoritative API resource. Generic page families use `invalidateQueryPrefixesExcept()` and the same cancellation implementation. Peer events preserve their existing behavior: they invalidate and refetch through each viewer's API scope.

Migrated source-reviewed callers cover Assets, inspection/calibration/spec/NCR Quality flows, Inventory item/material-issue/MRB/quality-plan flows, Production routing/Work Order/crew/late-report flows, Recruitment application actions, journal entries, customer portal price/delivery actions, shipment plans, Maintenance assignment, Warehouse transfer flows, and dashboard save/reset. The CSV matrix annotates 72 route rows; actor, peer and related-view UI cells stay `NOT_VERIFIED` because these callsites were not executed as real browser sessions. No cache layer was disabled or removed, and there is no API/cache-performance measurement for this delta.

The targeted SPA invalidation, generic query-invalidation, and status-hook tests pass 83/83. Targeted ESLint passes. An isolated Work Order retry-after-error test passes. A larger selected UI run had one Asset association timeout that passed alone; the complete Work Order detail test file emitted Pusher-constructor errors and had additional failures, so those suites are not reported as green. No API suite or two-user browser scenario ran.


## Delta 490 — align central query matching with TanStack semantics (2026-11-02 UTC)

The exact-key preservation introduced in delta 489 used JSON serialization per key segment. TanStack Query canonicalizes object properties in its query hash, so an exclusion key with the same fields in another insertion order did not match the cached query and caused an unnecessary detail refetch. A red-first focused test reproduced this: `['work-orders','detail',{id,view}]` was refetched when the equivalent `{view,id}` key was excluded.

The central prefix helper now uses TanStack `partialMatchKey`, and exact exclusion requires structural equivalence in both directions plus equal key lengths. The regression passes, as do the focused invalidation, generic query-family and status-action files (**84 tests**); targeted ESLint and `git diff --check` pass. No route/browser/API/socket scenario ran and no cache entries were removed. This does not upgrade any matrix UI outcome from `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Delta 491 — cancel stale reads when Payroll Adjustment actions return a full record (2026-11-02 UTC)

Source tracing found that Payroll Adjustment approve/reject cached the returned detail response but directly invalidated only the plural list key. A page-level red-first test mounted an in-flight detail GET, applied an approved response, then completed the older GET with the pending record; the page's status and Approve button reverted. The mutation path now uses `invalidateRealtimeChange()` for the registered `payroll_adjustment` entity and excludes the exact detail key already updated from the API response. This cancels older affected in-flight reads and refreshes list/related families through the central map.

The exact page file passes **2 tests**, including the red/green case. No broad suite, API call, browser, WebSocket or performance/cache-hit measurement ran. Updated only source evidence for the two approve/reject route rows; every actor/peer/list/action/badge/dashboard/inbox outcome cell remains `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Delta 492 — keep cancelled realtime reads out of the error state (2026-11-02 UTC)

After delta 489, the GRN source path was migrated to preserve its API response and cancel an older detail GET. Its page regression then showed a second problem in the central helper: using `cancelQueries(..., {revert:false})` prevented stale data from winning but changed the query to `error`, causing “Failed to load GRN” after a successful rejection. A focused generic query assertion confirmed `status === 'error'` after exact-key cancellation.

The invalidator now captures data and `dataUpdatedAt` for excluded exact keys, cancels in-flight queries with TanStack's normal revert behavior, restores the saved response to the idle query, and refetches other active affected families. GRN full-response mutations now use `invalidateRealtimeChange()` with the exact detail excluded; rejection remainder also supplies the affected PO detail prefix so list and detail invalidation share one traversal. The central cache file passes its targeted tests; all **7** GRN detail tests pass. No full suite, API mutation, browser, Reverb or performance measurement ran.

## Delta 493 — fan out attendance-driven crew refreshes to production list viewers (2026-11-02 UTC)

The source path was traced end to end. `AttendanceRealtimeService::stage()` resolves active work orders whose crew includes the changed employee. `AttendanceChanged` already sent a minimal event to `chain.work_order.{id}`, and detail hooks listened there, but production list hooks subscribe to `entity.work_order.visible`; they did not subscribe to the exact per-record channels. Production viewers also need not have `attendance.view`, so they cannot rely on `entity.attendance.visible`. Before the change, an affected production list viewer could therefore miss the crew/readiness refresh even while a linked detail viewer received it.

When at least one active related work order exists, `AttendanceChanged` now also targets the private `entity.work_order.visible` channel. That callback already requires the active user and both `production.view` and `production.work_orders.view`; the payload remains content-free and contains no work-order IDs. `useRealtimeList('work_order')` listens for `.attendance.changed`, and the central work-order invalidation map now includes work-order lists plus crew, crew options, readiness, and assignment/reschedule suggestion query families. The API still supplies each recipient's authorized result.

Red/green evidence: the list-hook regression failed before the listener existed and passes after; the event-contract regression failed before the list channel was added and passes after. `BroadcastEventContractTest.php` passes 37 tests / 1,349 assertions; the work-order channel authorization case passes 1 test / 2 assertions; the focused SPA hook and invalidation files pass 120 tests. ESLint and Pint pass. A two-context browser setup against `https://ogamiph.dev` authenticated the Production viewer and observed the private work-order list subscription, but this dev dataset has no active work-order crew member with a current attendance row. No mutation or event was attempted; cross-role delivery, list refetch/rendering and latency remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Delta 494 — route private realtime refreshes through the shared invalidator (2026-11-02 UTC)

A frontend source scan found custom private-channel hooks issuing direct `queryClient.invalidateQueries()` calls instead of using the central cancellation/coalescing path. This included notifications, customer portal complaint/pricing, driver deliveries, assigned work orders, time-clock refreshes, maintenance, scoped counts, and compatibility badge/list handlers. Those calls split related key families across multiple invalidation traversals and skipped the helper that cancels an older active read before refetching.

Added `scheduleRealtimeQueryInvalidation()` for scoped key families without a single record-map entry and `scheduleRealtimeCountInvalidation()` to derive per-user count families from the entity map. The listed hooks now use the shared prefix matcher, stale-read cancellation, active authorized refetch and 100 ms coalescing. Existing channel scopes and query families selected by custom handlers remain the same; count handlers now use the map's declared count families. `useArchiveWithUndo` remains a mutation utility, not a realtime listener, and is outside this hook migration.

The red-first coalescing regression failed before the helper existed. Focused verification after migration passed 133 SPA tests across the central map, realtime list, notifications/catch-up, driver and time-clock files; targeted ESLint passed. Two existing hook assertions were updated to wait for the central invalidation predicate instead of expecting separate synchronous calls. ## Delta 496 — complete Leave action projection and stale-decision recovery (2026-11-02 UTC)

Leave list/detail source review found that the page could treat a legacy `can_approve` flag as sufficient even when the API explicitly returned no action. Leave resource responses did not consistently include `available_actions`, `permissions`, or a version; create and mutation responses did not always attach the current actor's approval projection. Detail actions used the shared status hook but invalidated only the broad Leave prefix, and stale approval/rejection state came back as HTTP 422. Leave create/approve/reject/cancel mutation handlers on the list page also bypassed the entity invalidation map.

The Leave resource now returns per-viewer actions, permissions and a microsecond `updated_at` version. Controller create, show, single-action and bulk-success resources carry the current actor's approval projection; stale locked-state decisions return typed 409. Detail/list/create mutations use the central Leave invalidation map, and the shared status hook can reconcile mapped changes while preserving a successful actor response. UI actions honor an explicit empty server action list; old bundles without the field retain the prior server-computed `can_approve` fallback during rolling deploy.

Red/green evidence: after a separate observer namespace bug was found and fixed (tracked in `FINDINGS.md`), the focused Leave API tests pass **2 tests / 14 assertions** and the SPA status-hook, Leave list and detail tests pass **8/8**; targeted ESLint and Pint pass. The earlier live Leave two-user scenario has not been run. Matrix UI outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`; no Reverb receipt/refetch, peer action button update, cache hit rate, API p95 or commit-to-render timing is claimed. No cache policy or retention was changed.

## Delta 498 — refresh P2P parent details and lists when linked records change (2026-11-02 UTC)

Source tracing found that PO detail embeds GRN/Bill status and PR detail embeds converted PO/GRN/Bill summaries. PO, GRN and Bill events previously targeted the changed entity plus its list audience. A PO/PR viewer may lack permission to subscribe to a child module's channels, and the parent detail only listens on its own channel, so its existing linked-query invalidation map could receive no signal.

`ChainBroadcaster` now routes PO changes to their linked PR detail and entity-list channels, and GRN/Bill changes to linked PO and PR detail and entity-list channels. The detail channels and generic entity-list callbacks enforce the same read permissions as the corresponding APIs. Hash IDs are used only to select authorized channel names and are not added to the content-free payload. Existing parent detail hooks apply their central maps and refetch each user's authorized records. The Purchase Request page's own Post Bill mutation uses central invalidation with the current PR detail response preserved; sourcing-method and budget-acknowledgement mutation responses reconcile their fresh PR resource through the same map.

Focused verification: `ChainBroadcasterTest.php` passes **12 tests / 79 assertions**, including PO/GRN/Bill parent channel routing and payload checks; the focused `useRealtimeRecord` regression passes **1 test** and waits for the scheduler's near-live list lane. No broad API suite, live browser, peer event receipt, or cache/performance measurement ran. P2P source evidence was added to the relevant matrix rows; UI outcome cells were not reclassified.

### Cache hygiene/topology snapshot (2026-11-03)

The local API reports `APP_ENV=local`, `APP_DEBUG=false`, PostgreSQL database `ogami`, Redis cache, database sessions, Redis queues, and Reverb broadcasts. Redis cache is a separate service/DB (`redis-cache`, DB 3, 96 MiB, `allkeys-lru`); the persistent Redis service uses DB 2 for queue data with `noeviction` (256 MiB max), DB 1 for broadcasts, and DB 4 for generation markers. ## Delta 550 — Payroll Adjustment server-computed action contract (2026-11-03 UTC)

Source inspection found `PayrollAdjustmentResource` omitted `permissions` and `available_actions`, while the list/detail pages showed controls from a client permission snapshot plus Pending status. The mutation service did recheck status, self-approval, and active delegation under a row lock, but the rendered action set could still be misleading. A focused API regression first failed on the missing resource field, and a red-first SPA test rendered Approve despite `available_actions.approve=false`. The resource now exposes per-request permission/action booleans. Active delegated approver IDs are memoized in Laravel's request attributes so list serialization does not perform the delegation query once per record. The list and detail render controls only from the returned `available_actions` and disable both while either decision is in flight. API coverage passes 2 tests / 22 assertions (with warnings from the test container trying to read the absent `/var/www/.env`); the detail page passes 3/3 and SPA typecheck/targeted ESLint pass. Post-change, one approve and one reject three-context E2E both passed: actor socket disabled, actor actions refreshed from API responses, HR detail refetched, and the separate board removed the card. Board refetch-start samples were 1,075 ms for approval and 969 ms for rejection after the actor responses; these are two individual samples, not commit-to-UI or p95. ## Delta 556 — Invoice prebill action projection mismatch (2026-11-03 UTC)

Confirmed the prebill button could be visible when the server would forbid that viewer. `InvoiceResource` omitted `available_actions`; the detail page derived approval from Draft/prebill state, a client-side self-maker check, and the general `accounting.invoices.prebill_approve` permission snapshot. `InvoiceService::approvePrebill()` separately excludes the invoice creator from delegated authority and blocks approvers/delegators who edited after the last approval. Thus a user inheriting permission from the invoice maker could see Approve and receive 403 on submit. A focused database-backed API test establishes that the delegate's general permission snapshot includes the approval permission, GET omitted the expected `data.available_actions.approve_prebill` (red failure), and POST remains forbidden. A red-first React test also rendered Approve despite an explicit server `false`.

The API now projects `approve_prebill` from the same permission/delegation, state, maker, reason, existing-approval, and segregation-of-duties checks used by the write service. The mutation still re-reads and validates under its existing row lock. The invoice detail button now renders only when the API action is true. This is an action-projection gap, not evidence that invoice broadcasting or caching failed: existing invoice change signaling and actor response reconciliation remain in place. No live two-context browser transition or cache mutation was run; matrix UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Delta 557 — Budget Transfer action eligibility mismatch (2026-11-03 UTC)

Source inspection confirmed two distinct server rules were collapsed in the UI. Approval requires a different role than the requester (`BudgetTransferService::canApprove()`); rejection requires a pending transfer, a non-requester actor, and the approval permission/delegation, but not a different role (`reject()`). The resource previously exposed only `can_approve`, and detail used that flag plus client permission state to gate both buttons. The list also used the general client permission to decide whether to expose Reject.

A focused API test first failed on the missing `available_actions`; after the change it proves a same-role Finance reviewer receives approve=false/reject=true, rejects successfully, then receives reject=false from the response. The UI component test first failed to find Reject when the API fixture permitted it and the local permission snapshot denied it. The controller/resource now return separate viewer-scoped approve/reject actions, and both list/detail controls use the server fields. Three adjacent API scenarios pass 36 assertions; the new API scenario passes 7 assertions; the detail component passes 1/1; targeted Pint/ESLint and final SPA typecheck pass. This is component and API evidence, not a live multi-user browser run; all Budget Transfer route matrix UI cells stay `NOT_VERIFIED`. No change was made to write rules, caches, or records.


### Delta 552 — Loan approval path evidence reconciliation (2026-11-03)

The bulk-approval trace first appeared to show no Loan detail/list refresh, but reading the registered model observers and central SPA map corrected that conclusion. `LoanApprovalRecordRealtimeObserver` handles the intermediate approval case where status remains Pending; `EmployeeLoanRealtimeService::stageApprovalChange()` advances the parent version and stores a coalesced `EmployeeLoanChanged` event in the durable outbox. `EmployeeLoanRealtimeObserver` covers parent writes, and `LoanPaymentRealtimeObserver` covers payment writes. The event carries refresh metadata only and targets private record, permission-gated list, and exact borrower channels; the SPA subscribes through detail/list hooks and `SelfServiceLayout` mounts `usePersonalLoanRealtime(userHashId)` for the exact borrower channel. List invalidation includes the self-service loan query family, and all refreshes use existing scoped APIs. A focused endpoint test passed 1/9 assertions on the intermediate approval and decoded the outbox event/channels. Matrix source notes were corrected. This does not reproduce two-user UI delivery or establish latency; those cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. No cache entry was removed or changed.


### Delta 553 — Supplier Listing actor reconciliation and action projection

Focused regressions confirmed two client gaps in the internal review queue: a pending row rendered approve/reject controls when the API action flags were false, and the acting page retained its old row while its list refetch was unresolved. The API resource also lacked user-scoped action fields. `SupplierItemListingResource` now returns view/review permission booleans and approve/reject availability derived from current status plus the same active permission/delegation check used by `SupplierListingService`; the delegation result is memoized on the request to avoid per-row lookups. The SPA consumes these booleans for row actions and selected bulk rows. Single-row API responses are applied to all matching cached list pages before the central map invalidates list, approved-supplier, portal, badge and approval families. Successful bulk outcomes update current actor rows from the existing `{id,status,message}` response, then invalidate mapped families.

Red-first evidence: the API feature test first failed because `data.permissions.view` was absent; it now passes 1 test / 15 assertions (PHPUnit reports one warning). The SPA tests showed the stale controls and pending actor row before reconciliation. A separate bulk-action regression failed when the server-action filter was temporarily replaced with status-only filtering. The final focused SPA file passes 4/4; targeted ESLint and the full SPA `tsc --noEmit` check pass. A separate live two-context approval scenario passed after the fix; see Delta 554. No backend cache entry was found or removed, and no cache-hit or request-cost measurement was collected.
