## Discovery delta 574 — Stock Adjustment stale decision recovery (2026-11-03)

A new exact-file API regression reproduced a stale second approval returning HTTP 422 after the first checker had committed. Source tracing found the service's row lock/state guard correctly prevented replay, but the controller mapped that stale precondition through generic `BusinessRuleException` handling; the SPA's error callback reported the error without closing the confirmation or refreshing the visible row. The service now raises a typed state conflict for stale approve/reject/withdraw and the controller returns HTTP 409. The SPA closes the current decision action and refetches its authorized list on 409. Approve/reject/withdraw stale routes are verified by two API tests (12 assertions total); a mocked SPA regression verifies latest-row/action reconciliation. Live two-user conflict delivery remains unverified. No cache behavior changed.

These branches now return typed HTTP 409 with `stock_count_state_conflict`; scope, variance tolerance, maker/checker and form/business-rule failures remain 422. ## Discovery delta 578 — Transfer Order detail event missed scoped query key (2026-11-03)

The first two-context live Transfer Order run showed the Warehouse peer received both private record and entity-list events, while its open detail made no second API GET in five seconds. ## Discovery delta 579 — Transfer Order stock-level fan-out (2026-11-03)

A separate Warehouse tab on `/inventory/stock-levels` was opened before the System Administrator executed a one-unit transfer, with the actor socket closed. The stock page subscribed to private `inventory.dashboard`; it received one `InventoryStockChanged` event and issued a successful stock-level GET. The latest event offsets were −116/−116/−123 ms relative to the test timestamp captured after the actor response body had been read; do not treat this as commit-to-event latency. Authorized response offsets were 1,134 ms for detail, 1,705 ms for list, and 2,530 ms for stock. This single sample exceeds the 2-second stock target and is not p95. A previous failed-test trace (failure was the test matcher omitting location ID) captured a 200 stock GET starting +130 ms and ending +1,096 ms after actor response; this variability is not enough to assign a stable latency cause. Six successful one-unit dev test transfers moved stock between locations; two orders created by preflight failures were cancelled before execution. No cached response was served, no backend cache entry was added/removed, and aggregate stock was not changed by a transfer.


## Discovery delta 581 — service-worker browser/CDN cache mismatch (2026-11-03 UTC)

Compared the actual public HTTPS response, local TLS origin, source templates and active runtime override. Before the fix, local TLS Nginx and Vite returned `Cache-Control: no-cache` for the 3,533-byte `/sw.js`, but the public Cloudflare response returned `Cache-Control: max-age=14400` with `cf-cache-status: REVALIDATED`; a unique URL returned `MISS`. The active ignored `.dev-runtime/nginx.conf` had only the generic SPA proxy, unlike the production template's exact worker path. Added a no-store exact route with `Cloudflare-CDN-Cache-Control` and standard `CDN-Cache-Control` directives to the active dev override, tracked dev template, and production template. `nginx -t` passed and the active Nginx worker reloaded gracefully. Local origin then returned the no-store headers; the public response returned the same browser policy with `cf-cache-status: BYPASS` on two consecutive requests. `/` and five sampled Vite endpoints (`/src/main.tsx`, `/@react-refresh`, `/@vite/client`, `/src/lib/echo.ts`, `/src/hooks/useRealtimeEvent.ts`) remain 200/no-cache with Cloudflare `DYNAMIC`; `/api/v1/auth/user` remains 401/private/no-store. Five serial GET samples transferred 3,533 bytes each: 6.8–7.5 ms via local TLS origin and 56.9–283.3 ms via public edge. This measures the small script request only, not SPA or record-update latency. Current SW code excludes API paths, and the dev register path unregisters old workers; no record UI staleness was reproduced from a prior worker. No Cloudflare API credential was available for an explicit purge of the previously cached object; with current headers, requests bypass it until that object expires.


## Discovery delta 582 — current broadcast delivery foundation source/runtime audit (2026-11-03 UTC)

A static scan of `api/app` identified 122 broadcast event classes. All matched classes declare `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit` and use `BroadcastsOnDedicatedQueue`; zero `ShouldBroadcastNow`, direct public `Channel`/`PresenceChannel` constructors, or event classes without a literal `PrivateChannel` reference were found. The shared SPA subscription path reaches `echo.private()` through `subscribeRealtimeEvent()`. Runtime `php artisan horizon:supervisors` showed a running broadcasts supervisor with one worker consuming `redis_broadcasts:broadcasts`. These checks rule out those broad structural/runtime hypotheses for this dev snapshot. They do not prove each mutation path dispatches once, channel-policy parity across every callback, or actual latency/failure behavior under load. The checked-in `BroadcastEventContractTest` dynamically checks event inventory, after-commit, private-channel and queue contracts; it was not rerun in this source audit. No code or database/cache data changed.

## Discovery delta 587 — live dev cache headers and deploy-version path (2026-11-03 UTC)

Used browser-like HTTPS requests to the active `ogamiph.dev` endpoint after a Python client without a browser User-Agent received a perimeter 403. The Vite development SPA shell returned 200 with `Cache-Control: no-cache` and Cloudflare `DYNAMIC`; `/sw.js` returned 200 with `no-store, no-cache, must-revalidate, max-age=0`, both CDN no-store directives, and Cloudflare `BYPASS`; unauthenticated `/api/v1/auth/user` returned 401 JSON with `private, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, `Vary: Cookie, Authorization`, and Cloudflare `DYNAMIC`. `/version.json` returned the Vite development HTML fallback (same size as `/`), as expected because this build runs in `import.meta.env.DEV` and `DeployVersionPrompt` skips version polling in dev. The production build emits the manifest from the embedded build ID and `docker/nginx/prod.conf` has an exact no-store `/version.json` route. This confirms the currently served dev cache headers and expected dev-version behavior; it does not exercise a production build, an authenticated API response, or version prompting after deployment. No app data or cache entries changed and no tests ran.

## Discovery delta 588 — broadcast channel registration name check (2026-11-03 UTC)

Its only unmatched expression was the dynamic `job_posting` detail prefix; source inspection confirmed a `foreach` registers the `job_posting` and `job_application` detail/list authorization callbacks. No missing callback was found in the extracted set. No code or runtime state changed, and no tests ran.


## Discovery delta 589 — Inspection action controls honor the API viewer decision (2026-11-03 UTC)

The Inspection resource returns `can_manage` for the current API viewer, but the detail page's Cancel and Replace with PASS/FAIL buttons used the SPA permission snapshot instead. A focused component reproduction supplied `can_manage: false`, `can_replace_checklist: true`, and stale client permission `true`; the red-first test failed because Cancel remained visible (15 other cases passed). The page now requires `data.can_manage === true` for both controls. The same focused component file passes 16/16, and targeted ESLint passes. The public Vite-transformed Inspection module then returned HTTP 200 and contained both explicit `can_manage === true` predicates, confirming that HMR serves the edit but not proving a rendered browser interaction. No API role change, live multi-user action, WebSocket delivery, or cache measurement ran, so matrix route cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. No API permissions, business rules or cache entries changed.

## Discovery delta 590 — stable event IDs on every broadcast payload (2026-11-03 UTC)

A source scan of all 122 `ShouldBroadcast` classes found 10 `broadcastWith()` payloads without a literal `event_id`. Added a shared `HasBroadcastEventId` initializer and optional trailing constructor arguments to those 10 event classes, preserving existing call sites, channel names, event names and prior payload fields. Each ID is generated when the event object is created and is part of its serialized state. A second source scan now finds `event_id` in all 122 broadcaster payloads. A separate literal-key scan found `entity_type` in 84/122, `entity_id` in 20/122, `action` in 89/122, `version` in 86/122, `actor_id` in 48/122, `timestamp` in 80/122, and related-entity references in 8/122 `broadcastWith()` bodies. These are literal-source counts; dynamic payload merges may be missed, and aggregate/personal signals may not correspond to one record. The envelope remains a system-wide architecture gap, not proof that every omitted field causes stale UI. `BroadcastEventContractTest` passes 38 tests / 1,497 assertions; focused dashboard, permission override and output payload tests pass 8 tests / 30 assertions; two targeted Sales Order/MRP outbox compatibility cases pass 2 tests / 6 assertions. Older queued outbox rows without the optional constructor argument remain decodable and receive a new ID at decode time, as covered for Badges and Permission Override signals. This closes event tracing identity coverage only: the full entity/action/version/actor/related envelope remains incomplete on some events, and there was no live socket, browser, latency, cache or multi-user verification in this slice. No cache entries or business data changed.


## Discovery delta 591 — Work Order status event envelope (2026-11-03 UTC)

The Work Order lifecycle event already used the durable after-commit outbox and private detail/dashboard/parent/operator channels, but its `broadcastWith()` contained only `refresh` and `event_id`. Added the shared content-free envelope and passed the authenticated actor as an opaque hash from `WorkOrderService::recordStatusChange()`. The broadcast now includes entity type, opaque WorkOrder hash, `status_changed`, UTC `updated_at` version (timestamp fallback), opaque actor hash or null, the opaque parent WorkOrder reference when present, event ID, and timestamp; it omits from/to status, reason, raw IDs, quantities and model fields. The staged event remains codec-compatible with the existing outbox path. This closes one payload-contract gap, but does not establish that the prior refresh-only payload caused a stale screen.

Reproduction/evidence: a red-first payload regression failed before the implementation with `Unknown named parameter $actorHashId`; after the change, `DashboardBroadcastPayloadTest.php` passed 7 tests / 21 assertions, `BroadcastEventContractTest.php` passed 38 / 1,497, and the filtered committed Work Order transition/outbox feature test passed 1 / 10. Pint passed on the six scoped PHP files and `git diff --check` passed. No full suite ran. No two-context browser/socket trace was run, so delivery and UI behavior remain NOT_VERIFIED. No cache entry or business record was changed.


## Discovery delta 592 — accounting cache invalidation commit ordering (2026-11-03 UTC)

Verified the candidate that statement cache invalidation might run before commit. `FinancialStatementCacheGeneration::rotateAfterCommit()` and `DashboardCacheGeneration::touch()` register their marker rotations with `DB::afterCommit()`; both Account and Journal Entry observers call those APIs. The observers also register legacy tag cleanup with `DB::afterCommit()`. `JournalEntryObserver` registers these cache callbacks before staging its outbox refresh, so a post-commit client refetch is not intentionally sent ahead of marker rotation. This candidate is ruled out for these Eloquent observer paths; direct SQL or quiet writes that bypass these observers remain a separate coverage question. The targeted `StatementCacheAfterCommitTest.php` passed 1 test / 2 assertions; no code or cache data changed. This is a source-timing result, not a concurrent runtime proof or measured cache freshness result.


## Discovery delta 593 — Work Order parent invalidation reference (2026-11-03 UTC)

The Work Order status signal also refreshes a child’s parent readiness channel. The new envelope initially emitted `related: []`, so generic consumers had no parent record reference. Added an optional trailing `parentWorkOrderHashId` snapshot to the event, populated it in `WorkOrderService::recordStatusChange()`, use the same opaque hash for the parent channel and emit it as `related: [{entity_type: work_order, hash_id: ...}]`. Legacy/direct event construction remains compatible and produces an empty list when no parent is supplied. No raw database ID is included. This improves the event contract but does not establish a user-visible stale-data failure.

A red-first payload test failed with `Unknown named parameter $parentWorkOrderHashId`; the full focused dashboard payload file passes 7 tests / 22 assertions. The filtered committed Work Order outbox test passes 1 / 10 and verifies parent-reference persistence through the codec. Pint passed on six scoped files and `git diff --check` passed. No full suite, live socket/two-user test, or cache change ran; Work Order runtime delivery and UI matrix cells remain NOT_VERIFIED.


## Discovery delta 594 — Work Order detail channel cross-check (2026-11-03 UTC)

A source comparison initially showed that `WorkOrderStatusChanged` broadcasts on `production.wo.{hash}`, while `useRealtimeRecord('work_order')` subscribes to `chain.work_order.{hash}` with `.chain.refresh`. Traced the paired producer before classifying it as a defect: `WorkOrderService::recordStatusChange()` stages both `WorkOrderStatusChanged` and `ChainStepAdvanced` in the same transaction, and `ChainStepAdvanced` broadcasts `.chain.refresh` to the exact work-order channel that the detail hook listens to. The filtered committed-transition feature test verifies both durable outbox rows are present. This rules out a missing event-name/channel pair for the traced WorkOrderService status transitions. It does not verify every direct/bulk status writer or live subscription/delivery, and UI matrix cells remain NOT_VERIFIED. No code, cache, or business data changed in this check.


## Discovery delta 595 — output event envelope and outbox replay recovery (2026-11-03 UTC)

`WorkOrderOutputRecorded` had a refresh-only wire payload, and its durable outbox record pinned the WorkOrder row version. A focused regression advanced the WorkOrder `updated_at` after output staging but before codec decode; `OutboxEventCodec` reproduced `Outbox model ... changed after publication`, which prevents that queued refresh from broadcasting. This is a concrete event-delivery failure mode for rapid follow-up updates, reproduced at the codec boundary rather than through a live worker/socket.

The event now uses the shared envelope (`entity_type=work_order`, opaque WorkOrder hash, `action=updated`, UTC version, opaque actor hash derived from `recorded_by`, optional parent hash, event ID and timestamp). It still excludes quantities, defect data, operator identity and model fields. Because its only consumer is a content-free refresh broadcast, it implements `ToleratesNewerModelState`; after a later WorkOrder update the stale event decodes as a refresh of current authorized API state. The WorkOrder output list/detail/dashboard/operator channels and event name are unchanged.

Evidence: the first red regression failed on the missing timestamp constructor field; the new version-race feature regression then failed with `changed after publication`. After the change, the focused `WorkOrderOutputIdempotencyKeyTest` scenario passes 1 test / 9 assertions, including outbox encode/decode after a newer WorkOrder version. The focused realtime unit set passes 47 tests / 1,529 assertions; Pint passes on nine scoped files and `git diff --check` passes. No full suite, live Reverb delivery, two-user UI, cache performance test or cache mutation ran. This source/codec fix does not establish end-to-end SLO behavior; matrix UI cells remain NOT_VERIFIED.


## Discovery delta 596 — Mold threshold outbox replay tolerance (2026-11-03 UTC)

The outbox audit found that `MoldShotLimitNearing` and `MoldShotLimitReached` retain a version-pinned Mold model but send only refresh metadata. The same `OutboxEventCodec` newer-row rejection reproduced by the WorkOrder output regression applied to these model-bearing outbox events. Added the shared content-free envelope and `ToleratesNewerModelState`: warning uses `action=updated`, reached uses `status_changed`, both carry the opaque Mold hash/version/event ID/timestamp and a null actor; threshold values and shot counts remain API-only. The private dashboard audience and event names are unchanged. PayrollProgressEvent also carries a model but dispatches directly through Laravel's queued broadcast/SerializesModels path, not the durable codec, so it is not affected by this particular rejection.

The red-first contract assertion failed because Mold threshold events did not opt into newer-state tolerance. After the change, `DashboardBroadcastPayloadTest` passes 9 tests / 27 assertions, and `MoldRealtimeTest::mold_threshold_outbox_signals_survive_a_newer_mold_version` passes 1 test / 8 assertions for both event classes after advancing the Mold row. The combined focused realtime unit set passes 48 / 1,533; Pint passes on 12 scoped files and `git diff --check` passes. No live Reverb/two-user run, full suite or cache change ran. Generic `MoldChanged` already refreshes record/list/dashboard views; this closes a queued signal failure mode and does not prove that it caused a stale UI. Runtime UI cells remain NOT_VERIFIED.

## Discovery delta 600 — version the shared O2C/P2P chain signal (2026-11-03 UTC)

`ChainStepAdvanced` is the central after-commit refresh signal for Sales Order, Work Order, Purchase Request, Purchase Order, Delivery, GRN, Bill and Invoice paths. It previously carried only `refresh`, event ID and primary entity references. `ChainBroadcaster` now supplies a shared envelope with `status_changed` on state transitions, `updated` on related-record refreshes, UTC `updated_at` version (timestamp fallback), opaque actor hash when the initiating user is known, event ID and timestamp. Event names, queue, private channel set and API refetch scopes are unchanged. Trailing optional constructor fields preserve call-site compatibility, and old durable outbox rows decode using defaults.

The `related` list remains empty by design for this shared event because one outbox payload fans out to internal, customer, supplier, driver and dashboard channels whose viewers do not share one record-access scope. Existing related hashes continue to select private channels and are omitted from the wire. This preserves least privilege; a future per-audience signal split would be needed before broadcasting related IDs generically.

Evidence: red-first test reproduced the missing event timestamp/envelope fields. The focused ChainBroadcaster and queue tests plus the broadcast contract suite pass 54 tests / 1,600 assertions; the Sales Order route coverage file passes 13 / 76, and the Purchase Request submit/outbox scenario passes 1 / 2. Pint passes on four PHP files and `git diff --check` passes. No full API suite, live socket or two-context chain action was run. This standardizes event metadata but does not establish that the former minimal ping caused stale UI. All 111 source-traced route rows that use ChainBroadcaster/ChainStepAdvanced are annotated; their actor/peer/list/action/related/badge/dashboard/inbox outcomes are unchanged. No cache or business data changed.

## Discovery delta 606 — Contact Inquiry action projection and scope (2026-11-03 UTC)

Red-first API regressions reproduced two contract gaps: the inquiry resource had no version or `available_actions` projection, and a second status request carrying stale `expected_status` returned generic 422 instead of a conflict response. The SPA component regression reproduced a Closed inquiry still exposing “Mark in progress” because the page inferred actions from status/client permission rather than server eligibility. Source review confirmed the server already locks the row and enforces the existing transition graph; the transition rules did not change. The same reads include permission-dependent IP/user-agent fields, while the prior inquiry list/detail query keys omitted user identity, so keys now include the authenticated identity/scope fingerprint.

The API now returns `version`, `permissions.manage` and transition-aware `available_actions`; the locked service accepts an optional expected status and returns a typed 409 when stale. The detail page renders server actions, applies the mutation response immediately, refreshes authorized state after 409, and the list/detail caches are identity-scoped. The red-first API cases now pass 2 tests / 16 assertions; `crm/inquiries/detail.test.tsx` passes 5/5, including switching between users with different PII visibility. Targeted ESLint, Pint, PHP syntax checks and `git diff --check` pass. A whole-SPA typecheck finds an unrelated existing `TS2769` at `quality/inspections/detail.test.tsx:159`; it is tracked in `FINDINGS.md`.

A real dev-stack Playwright run now verifies one two-user, three-browser-context scenario. The System Administrator actor had its WebSocket forcibly closed; the Customer Service Officer viewer had private entity-channel subscriptions ready in detail and list contexts. After the actor closed the inquiry, one status_changed event with matching actor/event ID was observed by each viewer context, both authorized APIs returned Closed, the detail and list status chips changed without navigation, and both stale action buttons disappeared. The actor DOM changed in the mutation-response window; observer timing was 8 ms before Playwright recorded the API response. Peer event-to-detail DOM was 735 ms, event-to-list DOM 758 ms, and actor-response-to-peer DOM 908 ms. This is one route/transition sample, not a p95; the event has no record ID, so correlation used the only actor status action in the quiet test window. Related records, badges, dashboards and approval inbox were not asserted. The focused API tests pass 2 / 16 and the SPA detail tests pass 5/5. Synthetic inquiry probes from repeated runs were removed by exact test marker. No cache payload or unrelated business data was changed.

## Discovery delta 607 — Contact Inquiry event envelope and stale Horizon worker (2026-11-03 UTC)

A test-first event contract exposed that `ContactInquiryChanged` had no entity reference. Added a nullable `entity_id` carrying the opaque inquiry hash for single-record create/status changes; the retention job's coalesced bulk-delete ping intentionally has no one-record ID. The targeted broadcast contract test first failed with `Unknown named parameter $entityHashId`, then passed. The current API/outbox code decoded the persisted status event to a broadcast payload containing the exact inquiry hash, but the first live E2E captured the actual Reverb frame without `entity_id`. The Horizon container had been running since before the source change. After restarting Horizon so its long-lived PHP event/queue workers loaded current code, a repeat of the same two-user, three-browser-context scenario received the exact-ID frame and passed. This confirms stale loaded worker code as a runtime event-envelope propagation cause in the dev stack; deployment and automatic code-reload behavior outside this run remain unverified.

The passing rerun observed one exact matching private status event in both Customer Service Officer contexts. A focused channel-authorization regression passes 1 test / 3 assertions for the reader permission, unrelated permission and CRM-disabled cases. Event-to-detail/list DOM was 547/529 ms; actor-response-to-peer DOM was 561 ms for detail and 395 ms for list. These are one-run measurements, not p95. It also verified the System Administrator actor with its WebSocket closed rendered the API response and both peer views updated status/actions without navigation. Twelve uniquely marked E2E inquiry fixtures were force-deleted after verification; a follow-up count returned zero. No other business rows were selected for cleanup.

## Discovery delta 608 — Inspection refresh event envelope (2026-11-03 UTC)

The Quality `InspectionChanged` broadcaster previously sent only `refresh`, `event_id`, `version`, and `action`, so its detail/list refresh signal lacked standardized entity type, opaque entity ID, actor reference, and timestamp. Its outbox constructor also stored the acting user's internal integer ID. Red-first contract coverage failed because the event did not accept `actorHashId`; after the change, `broadcastWith()` sends `entity_type=inspection`, the opaque inspection hash, the opaque actor hash when available, version, action, event ID, and timestamp. The legacy integer `actorId` argument remains optional only for decoding already-persisted outbox payloads and is not included in the broadcast. `InspectionRealtimeObserver` now stages the opaque actor hash. The private per-record/list channels and content-free refetch model are unchanged.

Focused verification passed serially: the broadcast contract selection passed 3 tests / 1,349 assertions, and the inspection coalesced-lifecycle feature case passed 1 / 5. No live socket, two-user browser scenario, route-specific Quality mutation or cache test ran. This is a confirmed envelope-contract gap, but not a reproduced cause of stale Inspection UI; Quality runtime matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Discovery delta 609 — prevent stale legacy Inspection measurement writes (2026-11-03 UTC)

A two-request API regression reproduced a stale editor overwriting newer legacy measurement evidence. Both requests submitted `expected_result_version: 0`; the first saved a passing verdict and the second stale request still returned HTTP 200 and replaced it with failing evidence. Before the fix, legacy `recordMeasurements` neither accepted/checked the version nor advanced `result_version`. The endpoint now optionally validates the submitted version under the existing inspection row lock, increments `result_version` on every accepted measurement write, and returns the existing typed `409 inspection_state_conflict` on mismatch. Omitting the new field remains backward compatible.

The SPA measurement editor sends the version with measurement writes. On conflict it triggers authorized detail/list invalidation and keeps the locally typed values for review. The red-first API case failed exactly at the expected 409 (received 200); after the change, the route regression passes 1 test / 6 assertions. Related backend checks pass: the lifecycle concurrency file 4 / 13 and measurement contract file 7 / 19. `InspectionChecklistPanel.test.tsx` passes 15/15, including typed-input retention and version submission. No two-user browser scenario ran; actor/peer rendered outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## Discovery delta 610 — version legacy Inspection item-result writes (2026-11-03 UTC)

A red-first route regression reproduced a second stale-write path. Two legacy `POST /api/v1/quality/inspections/{inspection}/item-results` requests carried version 0; both returned 200 and the later failed decision could overwrite the first passing decision. The request validator accepted `expected_result_version`, but `InspectionController::recordItemResults()` forwarded it only for checklist-capture inspections. The legacy service branch neither compared nor incremented the resource version. Legacy item-result saves now compare a supplied version under the Inspection row lock and increment it after each accepted write. The legacy batch-result submit route also checks an optional version and advances the version for its outcome. Existing callers that omit the field retain their route contract.

The SPA sends the current result version on item drafts/submissions. Its lot workflow feeds each successful measurement and item-result response version into the next request, then submits the batch with the latest version. A typed 409 triggers authorized detail/list invalidation and leaves user-entered choices visible. Red-first evidence reproduced HTTP 200 for the second stale request; after the change, the focused API regression passes 1 test / 8 assertions, the lifecycle concurrency file passes 5 / 21, and the component test passes 16/16. Targeted PHP syntax/Pint, ESLint and whitespace checks pass. No live Inspection two-user browser scenario ran, no cache changed, and UI runtime cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.



## Discovery delta 611 — legacy Inspection cancellation ignored the result version (2026-11-03 UTC)

A red-first two-request API regression reproduced a stale action: an Inspection measurement save changed `result_version` from 0 to 1, but a subsequent Cancel carrying version 0 still returned 200 and cancelled the row. `InspectionService::cancel()` previously enforced the version only for checklist/count capture, and `InspectionDetailPage` sent no version for legacy mode. The service now compares any supplied version under the row lock for every capture mode and advances the version on cancellation; the detail page supplies it for every mode. Review likewise receives the current version from the page and enforces an optional version on its legacy service branch, advancing the version on completion. Old callers may omit these fields as before.

The stale cancel API regression now passes 1 test / 6 assertions and leaves the record InProgress with its latest saved measurement. The complete focused concurrency file passes 6 / 27. The Inspection detail component suite passes 17/17, including the current-version request and immediate API response cache update. These are backend and mocked component checks only; no live two-user Inspection browser scenario ran, and actor/peer/list/action UI outcomes remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. No cache data or business record changed.


## Discovery delta 612 — legacy Inspection lot-result route ignored the version (2026-11-03 UTC)

A red-first route regression found that `RecordLotResultRequest` had no `expected_result_version` rule and its `decoded()` payload therefore discarded the client precondition. Two `POST /api/v1/quality/inspections/{inspection}/lot-result` requests carrying version 0 both returned 200; the second changed the saved checklist measurement. The request now accepts the optional version and passes it to `InspectionService::recordLotResult()`, which compares it under the locked Inspection row before writes. It advances the version if no nested `recordMeasurements()` call already did so. Older requests that omit the optional field remain compatible.

The after-fix focused API case passes 1 test / 6 assertions and verifies a stale retry returns `409 inspection_state_conflict` without replacing the original passing measurement. This endpoint is a legacy API path with no current SPA caller; there is no live two-user/browser evidence, so Quality UI outcome cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`. No cache or business data changed.


## Discovery delta 613 — restart long-lived PHP consumers after source edits (2026-11-03 UTC)

Before refresh, `ogami-api` and `ogami-horizon` had been up about 53 and 52 minutes; `InspectionChanged` had been edited after that Horizon start. This is a concrete code freshness risk for the new Inspection payload because workers retain loaded event classes. The guarded local `make dev-refresh-php` verified the API image `ogami-erp-dev:local` and `APP_ENV=local`, cleared config/route/event/view caches, and restarted only API, Horizon, mail worker and Reverb in place. It did not rebuild, clear Redis, or modify DB data.

After the restart, API reports `local`, Horizon reports running and its health settled healthy, and the public site, Vite client and transformed Inspection detail module return HTTP 200; the module contains the current `result_version` forwarding. This confirms dev process freshness and HMR source availability, not a live Inspection WebSocket frame or cross-role rendered result. Future mounted PHP class/config changes on this stack need the documented refresh because HMR only reloads frontend modules.


## Discovery delta 614 — reproduce and fix Inspection peer refetch cancellation (2026-11-03 UTC)

The Inspection cancellation Playwright scenario uses three contexts: a `qc_inspector` actor with the socket blocked before Echo starts, and `system_admin` detail/list viewers. It verified subscriptions to `private-inspection.{id}` and `private-entity.inspection.visible`; both viewers received correlated `inspection.refresh` frames containing a nonempty `event_id`. ## Discovery delta 615 — reproduce catch-up cancellation of a slow first read (2026-11-03 UTC)

## Discovery delta 622 — Laravel queue restart control key recurs without TTL (2026-11-03 UTC)

The live `cache:audit` scan inspected 496 payload/generation entries and reported one no-TTL key, `redis:illuminate:queue:restart`. A direct Redis read confirmed the key is in cache DB 3 and has TTL `-1`; the cache instance was using `allkeys-lru`. Source tracing found Horizon's `TerminateCommand::handle()` writes this key with `Cache::forever()`, while the prior `QueueRestartSignalTtl` listener only handled `queue:restart`. This explains recurrence after the earlier cleanup, although no lost worker restart or stale record was observed. The cache prune would also have deleted this framework coordination key because it lives under the application cache prefix. Runtime now uses `volatile-lru`; the exact key is allowlisted from TTL-violation counts and pruning, and the lifecycle listener preserves it after both restart commands. Recreating the disposable dev cache container applied the persistent Compose policy and cleared its prior payload keyspace; no database/business data changed, and the transient cold-cache performance impact was not measured. After startup, audit inspected 449 entries: 0 unbounded application entries, 1 expected persistent infrastructure key, 0 oversized/unknown-size entries; prune apply found/deleted 0 keys. Current outbox payload sample: 11,911 rows, maximum stored JSON 645 bytes, p99 372 bytes; no oversized event payload was found in that sample. Production Redis and cache-pressure eviction behavior were not tested. The exact command that recreated the worker marker after Redis restart was not captured.

## Discovery delta 630 — locate and remove the retired Horizon namespace in Redis DB 0 (2026-11-03 UTC)

The active `cache:audit` scan covers the payload and generation stores, not every DB in the persistent queue Redis service. `INFO keyspace` exposed 39 keys in DB 0. A metadata-only Lua scan found all 39 used the exact `ogami_erp_horizon:` namespace; 38 had no TTL and one was expiring. Effective dev config showed Laravel/Horizon's `default` Redis connection on DB 2, broadcast jobs on DB 1, generation markers on DB 4, payload cache on the separate `redis-cache` DB 3, PostgreSQL-backed sessions, and Reverb scaling disabled. The running Horizon container was started after the local `.env` timestamp and its own Laravel config reported DB 2. Thus DB 0's keys were orphaned Horizon telemetry in this dev runtime, not queue payloads or application response-cache entries.

Post-cleanup `INFO keyspace` has no DB 0; Horizon still reports running. The active cache audit then inspected 514 entries: zero TTL-less application entries, one expected persistent queue marker, zero oversized/unknown-size values; prune dry-run found/deleted zero. This cleanup touched no cache payload/generation store and no business/queue/session data. `.env.example` and `api/.env.example` still default `REDIS_DB=0`, so this one-time cleanup is specific to the verified local configuration and must not be applied to another runtime without checking its connection map. The removed keys were operational Horizon telemetry rather than an application query cache; no API latency comparison was collected, and the measured Redis memory change cannot be attributed separately from concurrent queue-key expiry.

## Discovery delta 650 — reproduce failed Echo bootstrap recovery (2026-11-03 UTC)

A red-first focused provider regression made the initial `getEcho()` call reject once, then return a connected client on retry. Before the fix, only one call occurred and the provider stayed `offline`; the test failed on the expected second call. Source tracing showed the provider effect had only a one-shot Echo initialization attempt. Active hooks had a second gap: `subscriptions.openChannel()` reported the failed load and cleared its in-flight promise, but no provider path retried those still-mounted channel registrations after Echo recovered. The visible-tab HTTP catch-up could preserve read freshness, but it could not restore WebSocket delivery.

The provider now retries failed Echo initialization with exponential backoff from 1 second to a 30 second cap, tries immediately when the browser returns online or the active tab regains focus, cancels the timer when the provider unmounts, and asks the shared subscription registry to reopen still-active channels after Echo becomes available. A red-first registry regression confirmed the missing recovery function; after implementation, the provider suite passes 8/8 and the subscription registry suite passes 5/5. Targeted ESLint and a direct trailing-whitespace check pass. This is a controlled unit-level transient-load reproduction, not a two-browser or live Reverb outage reproduction; no deployment, API/cache operation, database data change, full suite, or latency sample occurred. The matrix runtime UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED` for this recovery path.

## HR explicit-transition route reconciliation (Delta 711)

Read each HR status edge against its writer and route caller rather than leaving route candidates as if they proved reachability. All 30 previously candidate-labeled HR edges now have an exact route/scheduled path or an explicit `NO_REACHABLE_WRITER` disposition. Employee status has only three current writer calls through `SeparationService`: initiation enters `on_leave`, cancellation restores the recorded `active` or `suspended` state, and finalization exits `on_leave` to the terminal status selected by the clearance reason. Although the state-machine helper permits active/suspended-to-terminal and several active/suspended status pairs, no current service, route, job, or importer call reaches those exact pairs; employee update prohibits status and import accepts active only. Training completion/cancellation map to their exact routes, and expiry is scheduled with a delivery-target gate. Recruitment stage and posting routes are mapped with transition guards; reaching the posting slot count can also derive `open -> filled`. Observer/event wiring is source-confirmed, but no browser scenario was run and all corresponding runtime UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## CRM explicit-transition route reconciliation (Delta 712)

The 25 CRM rows previously labeled as route candidates are now linked to the actual Complaint and Sales Order writers. Complaint resolution/closure use their matching endpoints and keep the linked-NCR closure/disposition guard. Sales Order status changes are cross-module: work-order confirmation starts production; delivery confirmation advances delivery coverage; invoice finalization marks invoiced; collection/payment verification, delivery confirmation, and credit-note application recompute paid/closed; invoice cancellation can reconcile an invoiced order back to its remaining physical state. These writes use the SalesOrderService locked transition helper and chain refresh. This reconciles source entry paths only; it does not prove every state combination was exercised or that every UI surface updates in real time. Runtime UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Quality explicit-transition route reconciliation (Delta 713)

Mapped all 19 Quality candidate edges to their current write paths. Inspection lifecycle edges use evidence-submission, completion, cancellation/replacement, and independent-review endpoints, with capture-method and maker-checker branches documented. NCR action effectiveness verdicts are written only through the verify endpoint; the scheduled `ncr:check-effectiveness` path sends reminders and does not change verdict status. Closing an NCR schedules CAPA verification or sets the parent to not-applicable in the special full-piece/no-action branch, while parent effective/ineffective status is derived after action verification. Registered observers provide the source event path, but no Quality browser scenario ran and runtime UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Return Management explicit-transition route reconciliation (Delta 714)

Mapped all 12 Return Request candidate edges to their actual lifecycle entry points. The ordinary submit path enters approval, while a QC-generated supplier return can pass through a transient approved state and complete inside one transaction. Approval/rejection, cancellation, final receipt, Quality handoff/retry, disposition completion, and line-split recovery now identify their exact routes and the guards that keep partial receipts from falsely changing parent state. Observer/event wiring is source-confirmed; no browser scenario ran and runtime UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Production explicit-transition route reconciliation (Delta 715)

Mapped the nine Work Order candidate edges to their individual confirm, start, pause, resume, complete, close, and cancel routes. Each route calls the lock-guarded WorkOrderService lifecycle path; the work-order observer stages the event after commit. This is source evidence only; no production workflow was executed and UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

Resolved the seven Accounting candidate status edges. Journal entries map to the manual post/reverse endpoints and the internal `postSystem`/`reverseSource` service callers spanning Accounting, Inventory, Supply Chain, Payroll, HR, and Loans. Budget submit/approve/reject/close edges map to their specific endpoints with fiscal-year, maker-checker, approval-completion, and legacy-status guards recorded. Registered model observers are source-confirmed; no mutation or browser scenario ran and UI cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Loans explicit-transition route and ledger reconciliation (Delta 717)

Mapped all seven Loan status edges. Approval/bulk approval, rejection, finance cancellation, employee withdrawal, write-off request/approval, and payment-driven settlement now identify their exact paths and guards. A payroll recompute can temporarily reopen a paid loan while reversing the previous deduction ledger, but the intermediate state is inside the replacement transaction; the committed state is based on the replacement payroll entries. The model observers bridge both parent loan and payment ledger writes. No loan runtime UI scenario ran; matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Maintenance explicit-transition route reconciliation (Delta 718)

Mapped the seven Maintenance Work Order candidate edges to the exact assign/start/complete/cancel routes. Completion notes include the alternate submit-repair plus independent verify flow when closed-loop verification is enabled; cancellation, assignment, and start retain their role, parts, presence, and readiness guards. ## Payroll progress subscription catch-up (Delta 721)

The only application consumer of the compatibility `useEcho` wrapper was Payroll period detail. Before delta 721, the wrapper forwarded event handlers to the shared subscription layer but discarded the optional channel-ready and channel-error callbacks already supported by that layer. The payroll progress event handler refetched the authorized period after each event, but no page-level authorized refetch ran when private-channel authorization completed or failed. Delta 721 forwards both callbacks and refetches the period resource for either outcome. A focused hook regression failed before the wrapper change and passes after it. This is source and mocked lifecycle evidence; private-channel denial and recovery were not induced against the running stack.

The same `useRealtimeEvent` consumer audit found missing readiness catch-up on the role-permission and cache-generation channels, plus missing readiness/error catch-up on the display-preference channel. Delta 721 wires these to the existing authorized auth refresh or cache-group invalidation. Focused hook regressions cover preference, cache-group and role-channel readiness; they do not simulate a live auth denial or a real cache-generation broadcast.

A TypeScript AST scan of production frontend hook calls found no other record/list/badge/preferences/cache invalidation call sites missing readiness/error catch-up. It lists the internal and portal `database.restore.completed` listeners as three-argument calls: their handler performs a one-time hard reload, so running it on every subscription acknowledgement would cause reload loops. It also lists `.permission.override.changed` without an event-specific error callback; that event shares the same exact-user private channel with `.user.access.changed`, whose error callback refreshes server identity, and the registry invokes each registered event's error handlers on a channel error. Channel failure/reconnect is also handled by provider degraded-mode active-query catch-up; actual restore delivery/recovery remains unverified.


## Discovery delta 722 — repeat Purchase Request approval after reducing healthy delivery probes (2026-11-03 UTC)

One four-context headless Chromium rerun against `https://ogamiph.dev` used Purchasing, Finance, System Administrator, and Vice President users. VP's WebSocket was deliberately disabled. The API response returned `approved`; the actor displayed Approved from that response. The System Administrator's private record channel was ready and delivered three `chain.refresh` frames at +7, +598 and +775 ms from the actor response. Its authorized detail GET began +272 ms, returned `approved` with `actions.can_cancel=false` in 1,392 ms, and the peer rendered Approved and removed Cancel at +1,620 ms without reload. The Purchasing list also rendered Approved without reload at +2,220 ms. This is one response-to-render sample: the detail result meets the proposed 2 s target, while the list falls within the near-live few-second target; neither is p95 or a commit-to-UI measurement. The earlier 4.047 s peer-render miss remains valid, so latency consistency is unproven.

The rerun's detail response was `Cache-Control: max-age=0, must-revalidate, no-store, private`, `CF-Cache-Status: DYNAMIC`, had no `Age` header, and was not served by a service worker. This request therefore did not receive stale browser, service-worker, or shared-proxy content. It does not establish freshness for other endpoints or backend cache families. Delivery-health samples remained healthy and arrived at +2.425, +8.962 and +14.352 seconds from scenario start; this run did not exercise degraded delivery or the polling fallback.

Before the rerun, a red-first provider regression showed the connected healthy-state delivery probe firing three times within five seconds. The provider now probes every five seconds while healthy and retains the two-second cadence while degraded. The focused provider file passes 9/9 tests and targeted ESLint passes. This reduces steady healthy-state probe frequency by 60% per visible authenticated tab; the single browser scenario is not a controlled load comparison and does not establish that this change caused the lower refetch latency. PHP-FPM saturation and the source of the prior 3.769 s detail GET remain unisolated. The scenario passed 1/1 in 53 seconds. No broad suite ran.

A separate read-only PHP CLI profile of the Purchase Request service path measured `PurchaseRequestService::show()` eager loading at 24.9 ms, `PurchaseRequestAccessPolicy::actionsFor()` at 2.4 ms / one query, and the budget snapshot at 54 ms. Across user/record lookup, eager loading, action projection and snapshot, the listener saw 25 SQL queries totaling 74.3 ms; seven shared one normalized SQL fingerprint. This probe bypassed PHP-FPM, HTTP middleware, and the network and is not comparable to the Playwright GET. It does not isolate the 3.769 s request, but query work in this CLI sample alone does not account for that duration.

After the E2E run, a read-only Redis audit inspected 506 cache/generation entries: zero application entries without TTL, one expected persistent queue-restart marker, zero entries above 1 MiB and zero with unavailable size metadata. The unbounded-key and retired-generation cleanup dry-runs each found zero candidates and deleted zero keys. This verifies retention hygiene for that snapshot only, not semantic freshness, cache-hit rate, memory trend, or API latency.

A read-only source scan found 122 backend files declaring broadcast events; every `ShouldBroadcast` class in the scan also implements `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`. No `ShouldBroadcastNow` or `toOthers()` usage was found. SPA Echo channel construction/listening appears only in the shared subscription registry; no page-level direct Echo usage was found. The application scan found no executable `Cache::flush()` call. These are source checks, not proof of event dispatch for every transition, channel authorization at runtime, or cache freshness.


## Discovery delta 731 — classify invoice payment submission refreshes (2026-11-04 UTC)

The Accounting service stages a parent Invoice refresh when a customer submits payment proof and when Finance rejects it, because both writes change the pending-payment state shown in invoice detail and available actions. A focused red-first regression read the persisted outbox payload and found both writes inherited `action: status_changed` although the Invoice status itself remained unchanged. Both call sites now label the refresh `updated`; the existing parent refresh count and transaction boundary are unchanged. The focused regression passes (1 test / 6 assertions), and PHP syntax plus `git diff --check` pass. This is an event metadata contract correction, not evidence that the previous ping was missing or that it caused stale UI. No browser, second user, live WebSocket, cache mutation, or latency sample ran; the route matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.


## Discovery delta 732 — restart local PHP consumers on mounted-source edits (2026-11-04 UTC)

The live dev Compose stack bind-mounts `api/` into API, Horizon, mail-worker and Reverb containers. FPM runs with `opcache.validate_timestamps=1` and `revalidate_freq=2`, but Horizon and `queue:work` retain booted PHP classes until the process/container restarts. A prior two-user Contact Inquiry trace reproduced a stale Horizon event envelope; manually restarting Horizon fixed the same scenario. Before this delta, only the manual `make dev-refresh-php` target restarted consumers, so ordinary file edits could leave background broadcasts on old code.

Added Compose Watch `restart` rules for Horizon/mail worker on `api/app`, `api/config`, and `api/bootstrap`, and for Reverb on `api/config`. Added guarded `make dev-watch-php` to run the watcher without building/recreating images. The active local watcher detected a temporary PHP source probe and restarted Horizon and the mail worker; removing the probe left no source artifact. Horizon reports running, all ten Compose services remain up, and `ogamiph.dev` plus `/@vite/client` return HTTP 200. No application/database/cache values changed. The watcher runs only while `make dev-watch-php` remains active; `.env`/Compose/Docker/dependency changes still need `make dev-refresh-php`, and production restart behavior is unchanged. This closes the confirmed dev loaded-worker refresh gap, not every stale-data cause; route-level matrix cells remain unchanged.


## Discovery delta 723 — isolated Inventory E2E stopped before mutation on WSS handshake failure (2026-11-03 UTC)

An already-running one-worker isolated process runner completed its auth setup (pass, 121.9 s) and attempted `INV01-HIGH-VALUE-APPROVAL` (failed after 3.7 s). Its saved Playwright result records a connection to `wss://nginx/app/{app-key}` failing the WebSocket handshake with HTTP 200 instead of an upgrade response. The browser observation remained on `/dashboard/warehouse` where the scenario expected `/inventory/stock-adjustments`; the failure was raised during the first “Create … adjustment” step, before form entry or submission. No Inventory record mutation occurred, so this is not evidence of a stale-record update or missed post-commit event.

The isolated HTTPS Nginx source has an explicit `^~ /app/` proxy to `reverb:8080` with HTTP/1.1 Upgrade and Connection headers. The E2E containers had exited before their runtime config/logs could be inspected, so the actual responder and reason for HTTP 200 are unknown. This confirms a one-run isolated-stack WebSocket handshake failure only; it does not establish a repeatable cause or contradict the separate successful `ogamiph.dev` Purchase Request scenario. No retry was launched. Do not count this run as a two-user record-transition reproduction.


## Discovery delta 724 — confirmed slow API upstream during approval refetch under FPM saturation (2026-11-03 UTC)

Three single-worker repetitions of the four-context final Purchase Request approval scenario produced mixed outcomes. At the original dev pool ceiling of 10, one run propagated the update and rendered the peer in 2.259 s (test failed its 2.0 s assertion); a second run did not reach the approval action because the peer's initial Pending detail did not render within its 15 s wait. The browser remained on a private/no-store authorized API path, so these misses were not evidence of stale HTTP caching.

The active local HTTPS proxy was initially using the ignored `.dev-runtime/nginx.conf`, not the tracked `docker/nginx/default.conf`. After adding query-free timing fields to that active config, requests during the failed 10-worker run showed PHP-FPM upstream response/header times of 2.3–5.0 s for Purchase Request detail reads and up to 5.1 s for the options endpoint, with upstream connect times near zero. Several client-aborted requests were logged as 499 while the upstream continued. PHP-FPM logged `server reached pm.max_children setting (10)`. This directly confirms dev worker-pool saturation during the multi-context load; it does not isolate each request's PHP work from time waiting in the FPM queue.

The dev API image's FPM ceiling is now 16 (`docker/php/Dockerfile`); the production image remains unchanged. At the observed host snapshot, about 9.9 GiB was available and existing FPM workers were about 56–63 MiB RSS. After the rebuild, the service reported `pm.max_children = 16`, `php-fpm -t` passed, the main Compose services were running, and `ogamiph.dev` plus Vite returned HTTP 200. A single post-change E2E run passed: actor socket disabled, API response rendered on actor, three peer refresh frames received, peer status/actions rendered at 1.821 s, and list at 2.694 s after the actor response. The detail GET was private/no-store, Cloudflare DYNAMIC, and bypassed the service worker. This one uncontrolled sample is not proof the pool change caused the faster render; other upstream reads in that run still took up to 4.545 s, and API p95 is unmeasured. Route matrix statuses and counts remain unchanged.


## Discovery delta 726 — classify Purchase Request change pings by mutation action (2026-11-03 UTC)

The four-context final-approval scenario received three distinct `ChainStepAdvanced` event IDs for one Purchase Request. A read-only outbox trace showed three committed writes: approval, PO-conversion-pending, and manual-conversion-required. Each row was published once; the latter two changed conversion metadata while the Purchase Request remained `approved`. Source inspection found `ChainBroadcaster::broadcastFor()` hard-coded `action: status_changed` across the Purchase Request mutation path, including create, edit, soft-delete, restore, budget acknowledgment, partial approval, sourcing selection, and conversion updates. `ConsolidatePurchaseOrders` also updates conversion notes without changing PR status. This did not produce duplicate queue delivery and does not explain the measured refetch latency or establish a stale-cache cause.

Added an optional event action to `ChainBroadcaster::broadcastFor()` and classified PR writes as `created`, `updated`, `deleted`, `restored`, or `status_changed` according to the mutation. Red-first lifecycle coverage reproduced all four draft lifecycle pings as `status_changed`; conversion helper coverage reproduced the three conversion pings with that same wrong label. The focused cases now pass (2 tests / 13 assertions). No full suite ran. The action label is metadata; the ping-and-authorized-refetch behavior and browser matrix outcomes are unchanged.


## Discovery delta 727 — classify automatic and cross-module Purchase Request refreshes (2026-11-04 UTC)

The call-site audit found more PR writes outside `PurchaseRequestService`: PO conversion, RFQ sourcing/handback, stock-production planning, Inventory auto-replenishment, MRP generation/reuse, and the `ConsolidatePurchaseOrders` listener. These code paths changed PR conversion fields or line contents but used the broadcaster's default `status_changed` action, or created a draft PR and labeled it as a status transition. Focused regressions reproduced five paths: PO conversion, RFQ sourcing, stock-planner create/replay, auto-replenishment creation, and MRP PR creation. The RFQ test assertion initially exposed a missing DB test import; after correcting the test setup, the MRP action assertion reproduced the same `status_changed` classification.

These callers now label non-status changes `updated`, new requests `created`, and status transitions `status_changed`; MRP and stock-planner paths distinguish a new PR from a reused/recalculated one. The five focused scenarios pass together (5 tests / 74 assertions). This is source/action-contract evidence only: no browser peer, event queue/recovery, or updated latency sample ran, and this action metadata did not cause the earlier slow refetch. The MRP reused-PR branch remains source-verified but has no new targeted test.

## Discovery delta 789 — local cache retention and runtime recheck

The local `cache:audit --quiet-keys --limit=1` inspected 447 payload/generation entries: no application entries without TTL, one expected persistent infrastructure marker, none above 1 MiB, and no unknown-size entries. All 441 source rows in `00-cache-call-sites.csv` match the current source text after indentation normalization; a narrower direct-facade/helper scan found zero unregistered candidate lines in `api/app`. No cache payload or business data was removed. This is local point-in-time retention and source-inventory evidence, not semantic invalidation, hit-rate, latency or production evidence. `ogamiph.dev` and `/@vite/client` returned HTTP 200. Two isolated E2E runner processes reappeared during verification; both runner/Compose pairs were terminated and all E2E-labeled containers stopped without volume deletion. No browser scenario was executed.
## Delta 787 — Action Center boundary timer re-arms past browser maximum (2026-11-04 UTC)

The shared Action Center task API allows snoozes through 30 days (`api/app/Modules/Dashboard/Controllers/ActionCenterTaskController.php`), but its page capped one `setTimeout` at 2,147,000,000 ms and invalidated at the cap. A server boundary beyond that point caused an early read and no subsequent timer because `next_transition_at` remained the same. A focused regression reproduced the early callback before the fix. The page now uses `scheduleBoundaryInvalidation()` to re-arm capped timers and invalidate only at the requested timestamp. The targeted test passes 1/1 and ESLint passes for the touched page/helper/test. No browser scenario ran; UI runtime matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

`Bill` is mapped to `dashboard:finance`, and the Finance dashboard service caches the response under that source generation. ## Discovery delta 800 — delegated-role bottleneck refresh

Source tracing confirmed that `ChainBottleneckController::index()` derives the visible audiences from both the signed-in role and `ApprovalDelegation::actsForRoles()`. `ApprovalDelegationChanged` already targets the exact private channels of the affected delegator/delegate, but only the delegation-management page subscribed and `approval_delegations_changed` invalidated only the delegation-list query. A dashboard viewer could therefore miss a changed delegated-role bottleneck until its minute safety refresh. The authenticated `AppLayout` now listens to the same exact-user channel and the central map invalidates `chain-bottlenecks` alongside the delegation list. A red-first focused map test failed on the missing key and passes after the change (one selected test; 93 skipped); targeted ESLint passes. The existing channel callback is exact-user authorized and the event remains content-free. ## Discovery delta 801 — rich widget cache generation did not follow scalar invalidation

`DashboardWidgetDataService` and `WidgetAnalyticsService` use separate `widget-summary:{key}` and `widget-rich:{key}` generations, both with 30-second payload TTLs. Before the fix, an Invoice source write rotated `widget-summary:finance.ar_aging` but did not rotate `widget-rich:finance.ar_aging`; a client refetch after a source event could select the prior rich payload. The observer now pairs summary invalidation with the same-key rich generation for all 28 registered literal rich keys, maps rich-only Executive/Quality inputs, and derives dynamic KPI generations from `KpiDefinition`/`KpiSnapshot` codes. A separate trace found that `ApprovalDelegationChanged` refreshed the delegation list and bottlenecks but not `approvals.pending`; `ApprovalDelegation` now retires both approval widget generations and the frontend signal map also invalidates dashboard widget data. Red-first Invoice/delegation generation checks and the dynamic KPI case pass (3 API tests / 6 assertions; PHPUnit warning is the container's missing `/var/www/.env`); the delegation signal-map regression passes (1 selected test; 93 skipped). PHP syntax checks and `git diff --check` pass. No browser, two-user WebSocket trace, cache-hit or API latency measurement ran; dashboard route matrix outcomes remain unchanged.

## Discovery delta 804 — dashboard widget keys have generation scopes; writer coverage remains open

Static reconciliation found cache-generation scopes for all 64 literal scalar widget keys (60 match arms plus four explicit placeholder cases) and all 28 literal rich provider keys. The two inventory stock scalar keys use `PublishInventoryStockChange`; KPI keys are derived from the active KPI catalog and `KpiDefinition`/`KpiSnapshot` writes. This confirms key-to-cache-scope alignment, not that every direct/bulk writer emits a client invalidation or that wall-clock boundaries are covered. ## Discovery delta 808 — configurable dashboard widget refetch gap

The backend `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` retires both dashboard snapshot generations and affected `widget-summary:`/`widget-rich:` generations before staging dashboard aggregate signals. Source tracing found the SPA signal map invalidated the dedicated HR/employee/Admin/Purchasing/Quality/Plant Manager snapshots but omitted `['dashboard', 'widget-data']` for those six signals. In addition, the shared `DashboardShell` listened to production/PPC/Finance signals, while Admin, Purchasing, and Quality listeners were mounted only by their dedicated pages; a generic widget page therefore did not hear those private signals. A red-first map regression failed on `admin_dashboard_changed` because its mapping contained only `['dashboard', 'admin']`. The fix adds widget-data invalidation for the six aggregate signal families and moves permission/feature-gated Admin, Purchasing, and Quality listeners into the shared shell; existing listeners were removed from the three dedicated pages to avoid duplicate ownership. Signal payloads remain refresh-only and each widget refetch uses the permission-filtered API. Updated the event-catalog consumer references for Admin, HR, Plant Manager, Purchasing, and Quality. The focused invalidation-map suite passes 95/95, targeted ESLint passes, and SPA typecheck passes. This does not prove event dispatch for every source, live two-user delivery or render latency; no runtime matrix cell changed and the connected 60-second widget safety refresh remains for unmapped source families.

## Discovery delta 816 — chain-bottleneck refresh producer and source coverage

`ChainBottleneckWidget` previously depended on its 60-second query interval. `ChainBottleneckService` reads 16 detector families across primary records and two line-item existence dependencies; the SPA map had a signal entry but no matching backend producer. Added one content-free private after-commit outbox signal, coalesced by transaction. `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver` stages it for the detector sources, and the provider registers `StockMovement`, `GrnItem`, and `ReturnRequestItem`; GRN line writes also rotate the Inventory dashboard generation. The channel requires the same `dashboard.view_bottlenecks` permission as the API. Finance's mismatched legacy listener was removed; the widget subscribes through the shared signal and uses `staleTime: 0`. Its 60-second periodic recomputation remains because detector eligibility can change as an SLA age crosses its threshold without a database write. Red-first frontend/backend producer regressions pass, as does focused channel authorization. No multi-user socket/browser trace or commit-to-render latency was captured; bottleneck matrix cells remain `NOT_VERIFIED`.

The same widget includes asynchronous chain-listener health; the existing `chain_listener_runs_changed` signal now invalidates its query family. Non-model queue/outbox telemetry still has the one-minute periodic freshness bound.

Delta 816 source-coverage addendum: the widget's automation summary also reads the `chain_listener_runs` table. `ChainListenerRun` model writes now stage the same coalesced bottleneck refresh so users with `dashboard.view_bottlenecks` can update even when they lack the separate recovery-list permission; the existing `chain.listener-runs` event and route continue to serve recovery-list viewers.

It now explicitly calls `DashboardCacheInvalidationObserver::invalidateSource()` before staging that event. A red-first focused feature regression showed the coalesced bottleneck outbox payload was not refreshed by this path; after the change it verifies the event ID is replaced inside the same test transaction's coalesced row. The focused test passes 1/6 assertions with the known PHPUnit container warning.

## Post-fix two-context reproduction update — Delta 832 (2026-11-04 UTC)

The interval from completion of HR's correction step to completion of Employee's observation step was 1.654 seconds in this one sample. It is not exact event-to-render latency or p95. No raw browser WebSocket frame was archived, so event publication and UI refresh are correlated but direct frame receipt was not separately captured. This reproduces and verifies the actor/list symptom for this Attendance path; separate record-detail pages, available-action buttons, linked records, badges, dashboards, and approval inboxes remain unverified. ## Discovery delta 972 — isolated final-approval latency and queue evidence (2026-11-05 UTC)

One four-context headless Chromium run used the disposable `ogami_e2e` database with Purchasing creator, Finance intermediate approver, System Administrator peer detail, and VP actor. The VP socket was deliberately closed. The PATCH returned 200 / `approved`; the actor rendered the API result and removed Approve; the peer's private record subscription received three `chain.refresh` frames. Its first final-approval signal arrived +3.445 s after the actor response; an authorized detail GET began +3.639 s, returned 200 / `approved` / `actions.can_cancel=false` with `Cache-Control: max-age=0, must-revalidate, no-store, private`, and completed in 615 ms. The peer rendered Approved and removed Cancel at +4.270 s; the creator list rendered Approved at +4.616 s. The test failed its 2 s detail assertion. `Age` was absent and Playwright reported `fromServiceWorker=false`, so this sample does not implicate browser/service-worker/HTTP caching.

The same E2E runtime log showed 60 `DispatchOutboxMessage` jobs and 22 `DashboardWidgetDataChanged` broadcasts in the 01:33:10–01:33:19 interval. The three PR `ChainStepAdvanced` broadcast jobs had queue waits of 3,294 ms, 2,494 ms and 2,299 ms, while each broadcast execution took under 10 ms. The isolated Compose stack used one worker for `high,broadcasts` (high first), so this run confirms a verification-stack queue backlog; the local dev runtime was separately observed with Horizon workers on `redis_broadcasts:high` and `redis_broadcasts:broadcasts` (Delta 955), and no equivalent live-dev backlog was measured. The E2E Compose file and readiness contract now split those workers. Its only rerun timed out at 90 s on the create page before POST; read-only DB inspection found no tagged PR, and the test's context-close timeout obscured the initiating assertion. No corrected-topology pass or latency improvement is claimed. The first successful business scenario remains evidence of eventual cross-role detail/list/action freshness, but its 4.270 s peer render is outside the target and the transition remains PARTIAL.

The initial isolated attempts also found two harness mismatches before business mutation: the real Playwright config did not trust the generated self-signed E2E TLS certificate, and the real E2E actor seeder uses a private generated password plus `e2e+<role>@ogami-e2e.test` emails while legacy journeys passed demo addresses. The guarded real config now accepts the isolated certificate, and the shared login helper resolves legacy role aliases through the read-only actor manifest and uses the dedicated E2E password. Those fixes only affect verification infrastructure. The isolated Compose project was stopped with volumes preserved; the live dev project was not changed.
